# Adelic algebraic groups and arithmetic quotients

This roadmap connects algebraic points over the adeles to arithmetic quotients, their measures, reduction theory, approximation and changes of level. It builds on the pinned Mathlib and Tau Ceti libraries and imports algebraic structure, places, Lie decompositions and cohomology from their owning roadmaps. Every target in AA.0–AA.5 has a declaration and a prerequisite chain. All six layers are planned; the exact unresolved inputs below prevent proof closure. Nothing here claims an implementation.

## Conventions and ownership

Let F be a number field, O_F its ring of integers, and G the affine group represented by a finitely generated commutative Hopf F-algebra H. Algebraic morphisms are represented contravariantly by Hopf coordinate maps. Points use Tau Ceti’s existing convolution group. Local, adelic and quotient topologies are the canonical evaluation or quotient topologies; an unspecified topology is never part of the data. Finite sets S of places contain infinity; the integral-model prototype indexes their finite part.

Use product-formula absolute values, with the complex absolute value squared. Additive Haar normalizations are vol(O_v)=1 at finite places, dx at real places and 2dxdy at complex places. The ordinary idele Haar measure instead normalizes O_v× to volume one. For Mathlib’s modular character, right translation pushes left Haar to Δ(g) times left Haar. Mutually inversion-normalized measures satisfy dμ_left=Δ⁻¹dμ_right. The Weil formula for H\G uses right Haar on G and H and requires Δ_G restricted to H to equal Δ_H.

For connected reductive G, a_G=Hom_ℤ(X*_F(G),ℝ). The positive real split component A_G(ℝ)⁰ comes from the largest ℚ-split central torus in Res_{F/ℚ}G; it is smaller than the full archimedean centre in general. A central character is continuous, unitary and trivial on the relevant rational central intersection. A gauge form is a nonzero vector in the invariant top-form line, realized by the top exterior power of the identity cotangent space.

AA.0 owns measures on existing restricted products. AA.3 owns generic adelic algebraic heights and reduction. AA.4 owns neat elements and neat levels, including their exports to ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0, ShimuraData:D5 and ShimuraVarieties:V0. AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups imports these packages under the accepted RS-04 boundaries. None of the upstream group, topology, finite-field or Lie packages is re-planned here.

## AA.0 — Restricted products of Haar measures

The carrier and restricted-product topology come from Mathlib. This layer supplies their Borel measures, using compact open principal pieces and normalized probability tails. Compatibility on open pieces gives the directed measure, and uniqueness gives the box, finite-factor and transport formulas. A separate absolutely convergent rescaling construction normalizes all good factors before restoring their positive infinite product; it is the construction AA.2 actually needs.

Test measure identities as identities of pushed measures, including empty and full finite splits. A finite rescaling theorem cannot justify the SL₂ tail, whose good volumes are positive but not identically one.

### The restricted product of countably many second countable groups is second countable

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/second-countable` · lemma.

Let ι be countable, let each G i be a second countable topological group and each B i an open subgroup of G i. Then Πʳ i, [G i, B i] is second countable. Consequently its Borel σ-algebra is generated by the boxes Π i, C i with C i open in G i and C i = B i for all but finitely many i.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i open subgroups.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The finite subsets S of ι form a countable set, and Πʳ i, [G i, B i] is the union of the open subgroups U_S = Π_{i∈S} G i × Π_{i∉S} B i (RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup and the open embeddings of the principal pieces).
2. Each U_S is homeomorphic to a countable product of second countable spaces, hence second countable.
3. A space that is a countable union of open second countable subspaces is second countable; the boxes of the statement restricted to the U_S give a countable basis.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For ι = the finite places of a number field, the finite adele ring is second countable.
- The statement fails for uncountable ι: an uncountable product of nontrivial compact groups is not second countable.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.isOpenEmbedding_inclusion_principal`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The box basis of the restricted product, here proved to generate the Borel σ-algebra. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Borel structure on a restricted product

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/borel-structure` · construction.

For the data of AA.0 put the Borel σ-algebra on Πʳ i, [G i, B i]. With it the restricted product is a Borel space in which every open subgroup U_S, every box Π i, C i (C i Borel, C i = B i cofinitely) and every inclusion of a principal piece is measurable, and the coordinate maps x ↦ x i are measurable.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Take the Borel σ-algebra of the restricted-product topology.
2. Boxes are countable intersections of preimages of Borel sets under continuous coordinate maps intersected with the open U_S, hence Borel.
3. By second-countable they generate the σ-algebra.

**Uses.**

- `AA.0/restricted-haar-product`: the carrier σ-algebra of the restricted Haar product
- `AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.0`: measurability of adelic Schwartz–Bruhat functions
- `AA.1/adelic-points`: Borel structure on G(𝔸_F) through the restricted-product comparison

**API.**

- `RestrictedProduct.borelSpace` (instance): Πʳ i, [G i, B i] carries the Borel σ-algebra and is a BorelSpace.
- `RestrictedProduct.measurable_eval` (simp): For each i the coordinate map x ↦ x i is measurable.
- `RestrictedProduct.measurableSet_box` (characterisation): For Borel C i with C i = B i for all but finitely many i, the box {x | ∀ i, x i ∈ C i} is measurable.
- `RestrictedProduct.measurable_inclusion` (functoriality): The inclusion of each principal piece Πʳ i, [G i, B i]_[𝓟 S] is measurable.
- `RestrictedProduct.borel_eq_generateFrom_boxes` (characterisation): For countable ι the Borel σ-algebra is generated by the boxes with open factors.

**Unit tests.**

- `RestrictedProduct.measurableSet_structureMap_range` (computation): The set {x | ∀ i, x i ∈ B i} is measurable.
- `RestrictedProduct.borel_finite_index` (degenerate): For finite ι the Borel structure agrees with the product σ-algebra on Π i, G i under the homeomorphism of homeoBot.
- `RestrictedProduct.measurableSet_not_box_infinite` (non-example): For ι = ℕ, G i = ℤ/4, B i = 2ℤ/4 and C i = {0}, the family C differs from B at every index, so Π i, C i is not a box of the generating family (it is a null set for the product measure, not a basic open).

**Acceptance checks.**

- The finite adele ring with this structure is a Borel space.
- Every compact open subgroup of the restricted product is measurable.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/second-countable`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The σ-algebra generated by boxes; identified here with the Borel σ-algebra. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Product measure on a level subgroup

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/level-measure` · construction.

Let S ⊂ ι be finite with B i compact and μ i (B i) = 1 for i ∉ S, where μ i is a left Haar measure on G i. On U_S = Π_{i∈S} G i × Π_{i∉S} B i define μ_S as the product of the finite product measure Measure.pi (fun i : S => μ i) and the infinite product measure infinitePi (fun i : ι∖S => μ i restricted to B i), each factor a probability measure on the compact group B i.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; μ i is a left Haar measure on G i; S finite with μ i (B i) = 1 for i ∉ S.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The restriction of μ i to B i is a probability measure for i ∉ S.
2. Apply infinitePi to these probability measures and Measure.pi to the finitely many μ i, i ∈ S, and take the product measure.
3. Transport along the homeomorphism of U_S with the product, which is measurable for the Borel structures by second-countable.

**Uses.**

- `AA.0/restricted-haar-product`: the restrictions of the global measure to the open subgroups U_S
- `AA.0/level-measure-compat`: compatibility as S grows

**API.**

- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure` (constructor): The measure μ_S on the open subgroup U_S.
- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure_box` (simp): μ_S of a box with factors C i (i ∈ S) and B i (i ∉ S) is ∏_{i∈S} μ i (C i).
- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure_isHaar` (instance): μ_S is a left Haar measure on the topological group U_S.
- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure_univ_compact` (example): If every B i is compact and μ i (B i) = 1 for all i, then μ_∅ is a probability measure.

**Unit tests.**

- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure_empty_prob` (degenerate): For S = ∅ and μ i (B i) = 1 for all i, μ_∅ (univ) = 1.
- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure_two_factor` (computation): For ι = Fin 2, S = univ, μ_S is Measure.pi of the two Haar measures.
- `RestrictedProduct.levelMeasure_needs_normalization` (non-example): If μ i (B i) = 2 for infinitely many i ∉ S, the restrictions μ i|B i are not probability measures and the construction does not apply; rescaling by 1/2 changes the measure.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For S = ∅ and every B i compact, μ_∅ is the Haar probability measure of the compact group Π i, B i.
- μ_S(Π_{i∈S} C i × Π_{i∉S} B i) = ∏_{i∈S} μ i (C i).

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.pi`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.infinitePi`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.prod`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/borel-structure`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.isOpenEmbedding_structureMap`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The product formula on boxes realised by Measure.pi on S times the infinite product of normalized measures off S. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. The level-wise product measure on G_A(S).

### Compatibility of level measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/level-measure-compat` · lemma.

For finite S ⊆ S' as in level-measure, the restriction of μ_{S'} to the open subgroup U_S ⊂ U_{S'} equals μ_S.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; μ i (B i) = 1 for i ∉ S.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Split U_{S'} as the product over S, over S'∖S and over ι∖S'.
2. On U_S the factors over S'∖S are B i, whose μ i-mass is 1, so the finite product over S'∖S of μ i|B i is the infinite product restricted to those coordinates (infinitePi_map_restrict).
3. Compare the two product measures on the generating boxes and conclude by uniqueness of measures agreeing on a π-system generating the σ-algebra.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For S' = S the statement is trivial.
- For ι = {1,2}, S = ∅, S' = {1} and μ 1 (B 1) = 1, the restriction of μ 1 ⊗ μ 2|B 2 to B 1 × B 2 is μ 1|B 1 ⊗ μ 2|B 2.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/level-measure`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.infinitePi_map_restrict`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.pi_pi`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. The compatibility that makes the inductive limit well defined.

### Restricted product of Haar measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product` · definition.

Given left Haar measures μ i on G i with μ i (B i) = 1 for all but finitely many i, the restricted product measure μ = ∏ʳ μ i on Πʳ i, [G i, B i] is the unique Borel measure whose restriction to each open subgroup U_S (S finite and containing the finitely many i with μ i (B i) ≠ 1 or B i not compact) is μ_S. It is defined as the supremum of the directed family of measures (U_S ↪ Πʳ)_* μ_S.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; μ i left Haar measures on G i; μ i (B i) = 1 for all but finitely many i.

**Planet:** Restricted product of Haar measures.

**Construction or proof.**

1. By level-measure-compat the pushforwards ν_S = (U_S ↪ Πʳ)_* μ_S form a monotone family indexed by the directed set of admissible finite S.
2. A directed supremum of measures is a measure: countable additivity follows by exchanging two suprema of monotone families.
3. Because the U_S are open and cover, ν restricted to U_S is μ_S; uniqueness follows because a Borel measure is determined by its restrictions to the countably many open sets U_S.

**Uses.**

- `AA.0/finite-adele-haar`: the normalized Haar measure on the finite adeles
- `AA.2/tamagawa-measure`: Tamagawa measures as restricted products of convergence-factor-corrected local measures
- `AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.0`: adelic Schwartz–Bruhat integrals and Poisson summation
- `AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.1`: Tate's global zeta integral over the ideles
- `FunctionFieldArithmetic:FA.2`: the characteristic-independent restricted Haar product for function-field adeles (RS-04 link AA.0 → FA.2)
- `ExcursionOperatorsAndSpectralAction:ES7:function-field-automorphic`: compatible Haar measures on D^×(A_K) (RT-AREA-geomlanglands/12)

**API.**

- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct` (constructor): The measure ∏ʳ μ i on Πʳ i, [G i, B i], given hμ : ∀ᶠ i in cofinite, μ i (B i) = 1.
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_restrict_level` (characterisation): For admissible finite S, the restriction of ∏ʳ μ i to U_S is μ_S.
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_eq_of_restrict` (extensionality): A Borel measure whose restriction to every admissible U_S is μ_S equals ∏ʳ μ i.
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_box` (simp): ∏ʳ μ i of a box Π i, C i with C i = B i cofinitely equals ∏ᶠ i, μ i (C i).
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_isHaarMeasure` (instance): ∏ʳ μ i is a left Haar measure.
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_smul` (relation): For positive finite real c_i equal to 1 cofinitely, rescaling μ_i by c_i rescales the product by ∏ᶠ c_i.

**Unit tests.**

- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_compact_open_box` (computation): If μ i (B i) = 1 for every i, then ∏ʳ μ i of {x | ∀ i, x i ∈ B i} is 1.
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_finite_index` (compatibility): For finite ι, transported along homeoBot, ∏ʳ μ i equals Measure.pi μ.
- `RestrictedProduct.haarProduct_not_probability_product` (non-example): If some factor has infinite total mass (as μ_p on ℚ_p), then ∏ʳ μ i has infinite total mass; it is not an infinite product of probability measures.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For finite ι with all B i = G i compact this is the product Haar measure Measure.pi μ.
- For the finite adeles of ℚ with μ_p (ℤ_p) = 1 the measure of ∏_p ℤ_p is 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/level-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/level-measure-compat`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/borel-structure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/directed-supremum-measure`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The restricted product of normalized Haar measures and its invariance. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. The measure as the inductive limit over the finite sets S.

### Restriction of the restricted product measure to a level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-restrict-level` · lemma.

For every finite S containing the exceptional indices, (∏ʳ μ i).restrict U_S = (U_S ↪ Πʳ)_* μ_S, and ∏ʳ μ i is the unique measure with this property.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Immediate from the construction as a directed supremum and level-measure-compat.
2. Uniqueness: U_S cover Πʳ and are countably many, so measures agreeing on each U_S agree.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Restricting to Π i, B i gives the infinite product of the probability measures μ i|B i when μ i (B i) = 1 for all i.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/level-measure-compat`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The values on boxes inside a level determine the measure. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Measure of a box

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-box` · lemma.

For Borel sets C i ⊂ G i with C i = B i for all but finitely many i, ∏ʳ μ i (Π i, C i) = ∏ᶠ i, μ i (C i), the product being finite because almost all factors equal 1.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose S containing every i with C i ≠ B i or μ i (B i) ≠ 1; the box lies in U_S.
2. Apply restricted-haar-restrict-level and the product formulas Measure.pi_pi and the infinite product of probability measures on the box.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the finite adeles of ℚ, the box ∏_{p≠2} ℤ_p × 2ℤ_2 has measure 1/2.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-restrict-level`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.pi_pi`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. Exactly this formula. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### The restricted product measure is a Haar measure

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-is-haar` · theorem.

∏ʳ μ i is a left Haar measure on the locally compact group Πʳ i, [G i, B i]; if every μ i is also right invariant then ∏ʳ μ i is right invariant.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Left invariance: for g in the restricted product choose S with g ∈ U_S; left translation by g preserves U_{S'} for S' ⊇ S and preserves each μ_{S'} because each factor is left invariant.
2. Choose a finite exceptional set S outside which B_i is compact and normalized. At each i in S choose a compact neighbourhood C_i of 1 of positive finite Haar mass. The box (product over S of C_i) times (product off S of B_i) is a compact neighbourhood of finite mass. Its interiors and their translates cover every compact set by finitely many translates, proving finite-on-compacts.
3. Positive on opens: every nonempty open set contains a translate of an open box with factors of positive measure.
4. Right invariance is proved in the same way.

**Acceptance checks.**

- On the finite adeles of ℚ, translating ∏_p ℤ_p by 1/p keeps measure 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-restrict-level`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.locallyCompactSpace_of_group`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.isTopologicalGroup`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.IsHaarMeasure`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. Haar property of the product. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Change of local normalizations

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-rescale` · lemma.

If μ′_i = c_i μ_i, where 0<c_i<∞ and c_i=1 outside a finite set, then the normalized restricted Haar products satisfy ∏ʳ μ′_i = (∏ᶠ c_i) ∏ʳ μ_i. An infinitely rescaled family needs the separately stated convergent-product construction; finite rescaling alone gives no such theorem.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose S containing the indices with c i ≠ 1; both measures are determined on U_S by level-measure, where the scalar ∏_{i∈S} c i appears by multilinearity of Measure.pi.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Doubling the measure at one place doubles the global measure.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-restrict-level`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. The rescaling law used for the independence of Tamagawa measures.

### Changing the restricting subgroups at finitely many places

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-change-subgroups` · lemma.

Let B' i ≤ G i be open subgroups with B' i = B i for all but finitely many i. The identity on Π i, G i restricts to an isomorphism of topological groups Πʳ i, [G i, B i] ≃ₜ* Πʳ i, [G i, B' i], under which ∏ʳ μ i corresponds to ∏ʳ μ i (the normalization condition being cofinite). Thus the restricted product and its measure depend only on the B i up to finitely many indices.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; B' i open subgroups, B' i = B i cofinitely.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The underlying sets agree because the defining eventuality conditions agree cofinitely.
2. The topologies agree because both are the supremum over the same cofinal family of principal pieces (topologicalSpace_eq_iSup).
3. The measures agree on every U_S with S containing the indices where B i ≠ B' i.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Changing B 2 = ℤ_2 to 2ℤ_2 in the finite adeles of ℚ gives the same topological ring.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.continuous_dom`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.6, p. 6. Independence of the exceptional finite set.

### Splitting off finitely many factors

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/split-finite-factors` · construction.

For a finite S ⊂ ι, the map x ↦ ((x i)_{i∈S}, (x i)_{i∉S}) is an isomorphism of topological groups Πʳ i, [G i, B i] ≃ₜ* (Π_{i∈S} G i) × Πʳ (i : ι∖S), [G i, B i].

**Hypotheses.** ι arbitrary, S finite; G i topological groups, B i open subgroups.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The map and its inverse are well defined because the eventuality condition only concerns indices outside the finite S.
2. Continuity in both directions follows from continuous_dom applied to the principal pieces and the product topology.

**Uses.**

- `AA.0/restricted-haar-split`: Fubini for the restricted product measure
- `AA.1/adelic-points-split`: G(𝔸) = G(F_∞) × G(𝔸_f) and G(𝔸) = G(F_S) × G(𝔸^S)
- `AA.4/strong-approximation-property`: density of G(F) in G(𝔸^S)

**API.**

- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite` (constructor): The isomorphism of topological groups for a finite set S.
- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_apply_fst` (simp): (splitFinite S x).1 i = x i for i ∈ S.
- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_apply_snd` (simp): (splitFinite S x).2 i = x i for i ∉ S.
- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_symm_apply` (simp): The inverse glues the two families.
- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_mono` (functoriality): For S ⊆ S' the splittings are compatible with the further splitting of the restricted factor.

**Unit tests.**

- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_empty` (degenerate): For S=∅, splitFinite identifies the restricted product with the one-point finite factor times itself, and the measure becomes the point measure times haarProduct.
- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_univ_finite` (computation): For finite ι and S=univ, splitFinite identifies the full restricted product with ∏_i G_i times the trivial group and transports Haar measure to Measure.pi μ times the point measure.
- `RestrictedProduct.splitFinite_not_infinite` (non-example): Infinitely many factors cannot be split off: a family lying outside B i at infinitely many indices, such as (1/p)_p in ∏_p ℚ_p with B p = ℤ_p, is not an element of the restricted product.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For S = ∅ it is the identity up to the unit factor.
- For the adeles of ℚ with S = {∞} it is 𝔸 = ℝ × 𝔸_f.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.continuous_dom`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.mapAlong_continuous`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.2, p. 7. Splitting the adele group at a set of places.

### Fubini for restricted product measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-split` · theorem.

Under split-finite-factors, ∏ʳ_ι μ i corresponds to (Measure.pi (fun i : S => μ i)).prod (∏ʳ_{ι∖S} μ i). Hence for f integrable on Πʳ i, [G i, B i], ∫ f ∂(∏ʳ μ i) = ∫_{Π_{i∈S} G i} ∫_{Πʳ_{ι∖S}} f(x_S, x^S) dμ^S dμ_S.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; f integrable for ∏ʳ μ i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Both sides are left Haar measures on the same group; compare their values on a single box of positive finite measure (restricted-haar-box) and use uniqueness of Haar measure.
2. Apply MeasureTheory.integral_prod.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the adeles of ℚ and S = {∞}, ∫_𝔸 f = ∫_ℝ ∫_{𝔸_f} f.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/split-finite-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-box`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-is-haar`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.isMulLeftInvariant_eq_smul`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.integral_prod`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. Product decomposition over a finite set of places.

### Integral of a factorizable function

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-factorizable-integral` · theorem.

Let f i : G i → ℂ be integrable, with f i = indicator of B i for all but finitely many i. Then f(x) = ∏ i, f i (x i) is a well-defined integrable function on Πʳ i, [G i, B i] and ∫ f ∂(∏ʳ μ i) = ∏ᶠ i, ∫ f i ∂μ i.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; f i integrable, f i = 1_{B i} cofinitely.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For x in the restricted product, f i (x i) = 1 for all but finitely many i, so the product is finite.
2. Choose S containing the exceptional indices; f is supported in U_S, and on U_S it is a product of a function on Π_{i∈S} G i and the indicator of Π_{i∉S} B i.
3. Apply restricted-haar-split and Fubini for the finite product, using ∫ 1_{B i} dμ i = 1.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For ι = primes, f p = 1_{ℤ_p} for all p gives integral 1.
- Tate's global zeta integral of a factorizable Schwartz–Bruhat function is the product of local zeta integrals in the domain of convergence (AL.1).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-split`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-box`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. Multiplicativity, extended from indicators to factorizable functions. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Restricted products of unimodular groups are unimodular

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-unimodular` · lemma.

If every G i is unimodular (modularCharacter G i = 1), then Πʳ i, [G i, B i] is unimodular. More generally the modular character of the restricted product is x ↦ ∏ᶠ i, Δ_{G i}(x i), a finite product because x_i belongs to B_i at almost every index and those B_i are compact at almost every index.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The modular character is trivial on compact subgroups. Exclude both the finitely many noncompact B_i and the finitely many indices with x_i outside B_i; every remaining factor is 1.
2. Right translation by x scales each factor of ∏ʳ μ i by Δ_{G i}(x i) (restricted-haar-is-haar, right translations of boxes).

**Acceptance checks.**

- The finite adele ring is unimodular as an additive group.
- For G i = B(ℚ_p) the Borel subgroups of GL_2 the modular character is |a/d|_𝔸 (AA.2/modulus-character).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-is-haar`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.modularCharacter`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-box`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. Unimodularity of the adele group is read off from the local factors.

### Functoriality of restricted product measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-map` · lemma.

Let φ i : G i ≃ₜ* G' i be isomorphisms of topological groups with φ i (B i) = B' i for all but finitely many i. The induced isomorphism Φ = RestrictedProduct.map φ of restricted products satisfies Φ_*(∏ʳ μ i) = ∏ʳ (φ i)_* μ i.

**Hypotheses.** ι is a countable index type; each G i is a second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological group; B i ≤ G i is an open subgroup for every i, compact for all but finitely many i; φ i topological group isomorphisms, φ i (B i) = B' i cofinitely.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Φ is a homeomorphism (mapAlong_continuous in both directions).
2. Compare the two Haar measures on one box of positive finite measure using restricted-haar-box and uniqueness of Haar measure.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Multiplication by a finite idele a is a homeomorphism of 𝔸_f scaling ∏ʳ μ_v by ‖a‖.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-box`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-is-haar`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.mapAlong_continuous`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.isMulLeftInvariant_eq_smul`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), Lemma 3.5, p. 26. Pulling restricted product measures back along adelic homeomorphisms.

### Normalized Haar measure on the finite adeles

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/finite-adele-haar` · construction.

For a number field K, the measure μ_f on 𝔸_{K,f} = FiniteAdeleRing (𝓞 K) K is the restricted product of the additive Haar measures μ_v on K_v normalized by μ_v(𝒪_v) = 1, where each K_v is a nonarchimedean local field and 𝒪_v its compact open valuation ring.

**Hypotheses.** K a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. K_v is a nonarchimedean local field, so locally compact with 𝒪_v compact open (Tau Ceti local-field structure on adicCompletion).
2. Normalize μ_v(𝒪_v) = 1 and apply restricted-haar-product with B v = 𝒪_v; local compactness of 𝔸_{K,f} is GlobalNumberFields layer 4.

**Uses.**

- `AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.9/bc-adelic-scaling-measure`: additive finite-adele measure for the Bost–Connes scaling construction (request from AN.8)
- `AA.0/adele-haar`: the finite factor of the adele measure
- `AA.2/compact-open-volume`: volumes of compact open subgroups of G(𝔸_f) in the GL_1 case

**API.**

- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar` (constructor): The normalized Haar measure on 𝔸_{K,f}.
- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar_integers` (simp): finiteAdeleHaar (∏_v 𝒪_v) = 1.
- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar_isAddHaar` (instance): finiteAdeleHaar is an additive Haar measure.
- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar_smul` (relation): For a finite idele a, map (a * ·) finiteAdeleHaar = (∏_v |a_v|_v)⁻¹ • finiteAdeleHaar.

**Unit tests.**

- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar_ideal` (computation): For a nonzero ideal 𝔞 of 𝓞 K, the closure of 𝔞 in ∏_v 𝒪_v has measure (Ideal.absNorm 𝔞)⁻¹.
- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar_rat_twoZ2` (computation): For K = ℚ, the set 2ℤ_2 × ∏_{p≠2} ℤ_p has measure 1/2.
- `NumberField.finiteAdeleHaar_not_finite` (non-example): finiteAdeleHaar is not a finite measure: 𝔸_{K,f} is a disjoint union of infinitely many translates of ∏_v 𝒪_v.

**Acceptance checks.**

- μ_f(∏_v 𝒪_v) = 1.
- For a ∈ 𝔸_{K,f}^× multiplication by a scales μ_f by ∏_v |a_v|_v.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain.FiniteAdeleRing`, `tauceti:IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum.isNonarchimedeanLocalField_adicCompletion`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-4-finite-adeles`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/local-normalized-haar`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The local normalization at finite places. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Normalized Haar measure on the adeles

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/adele-haar` · construction.

For a number field K, the measure μ_𝔸 on 𝔸_K = K_∞ × 𝔸_{K,f} is the product of the measure on K_∞ = ∏_{w|∞} K_w given by Lebesgue measure at real places and twice Lebesgue measure at complex places, and the finite-adele measure finite-adele-haar.

**Hypotheses.** K a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. K_∞ ≃ ℝ^{r₁} × ℂ^{r₂} (InfiniteAdeleRing.ringEquiv_mixedSpace); take 2^{r₂} times the transported mixed-space volume.
2. Take the product with finite-adele-haar; it is an additive Haar measure (product of Haar measures).

**Uses.**

- `AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.0`: the additive measure on 𝔸_K before self-dual renormalization
- `AA.2/tamagawa-number`: vol(𝔸_K/K) for G_a
- `AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient`: idelic and adelic volume comparisons

**API.**

- `NumberField.adeleHaar` (constructor): The normalized Haar measure on 𝔸_K.
- `NumberField.adeleHaar_isAddHaar` (instance): adeleHaar is an additive Haar measure.
- `NumberField.adeleHaar_prod` (characterisation): adeleHaar is the product of the archimedean measure and finiteAdeleHaar.
- `NumberField.adeleHaar_infinite_eq_mixed` (compatibility): The archimedean factor is 2^{r₂} times the transport of the mixed-space volume.

**Unit tests.**

- `NumberField.adeleHaar_box_rat` (computation): For K = ℚ the set [0,1) × ∏_p ℤ_p has measure 1.
- `NumberField.adeleHaar_complex_factor` (computation): For K = ℚ(i), the set ([0,1]²) × ∏_v 𝒪_v has measure 2.
- `NumberField.adeleHaar_not_selfdual` (non-example): For K with |d_K| > 1 the measure is not self-dual for the standard character: the self-dual measure is |d_K|^{-1/2} • adeleHaar (AL.0 owns the self-dual normalization).

**Acceptance checks.**

- The archimedean factor equals 2^{r₂} times the mixed-embedding volume.
- The product [0,1]^{r₁} × ([0,1]²)^{r₂} × ∏_v 𝒪_v has measure 2^{r₂}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/finite-adele-haar`, `mathlib:NumberField.AdeleRing`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/mixed-space-topology`, `mathlib:NumberField.mixedEmbedding.volume_fundamentalDomain_stdBasis`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.prod`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The archimedean normalization. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Normalized Haar measure on the ideles

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/idele-haar` · construction.

For a number field K, the measure d^×x on the idele group 𝔸_K^× is the restricted product, through the topological isomorphism 𝔸_K^× ≅ Πʳ v, [K_v^×, 𝒪_v^×] (finite part) times ∏_{w|∞} K_w^×, of the Haar measures d^×x_v with vol(𝒪_v^×) = 1 at finite places, dx/|x| at real places and 2 dx dy/(x² + y²) at complex places.

**Hypotheses.** K a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use RestrictedProduct.unitsEquiv for the finite part and its homeomorphism property for the units topology (GlobalNumberFields layer 6 proves local compactness and the units topology).
2. Apply restricted-haar-product with B v = 𝒪_v^×.

**Uses.**

- `AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.1/idele-class-volume`: volume of the norm-one idele class group
- `AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient`: the GL_1 quotient measure
- `AA.0/tamagawa-convergence-failure`: comparison with the form measures |dx/x|_v

**API.**

- `NumberField.ideleHaar` (constructor): The normalized Haar measure on 𝔸_K^×.
- `NumberField.ideleHaar_isHaar` (instance): ideleHaar is a Haar measure on the idele group.
- `NumberField.ideleHaar_units` (simp): ideleHaar of ∏_v 𝒪_v^× times a box at infinity is the archimedean volume of the box.
- `NumberField.ideleHaar_invariant_principal` (relation): ideleHaar is invariant under multiplication by K^×.

**Unit tests.**

- `NumberField.ideleHaar_rat_box` (computation): For K = ℚ, ideleHaar (∏_p ℤ_p^× × [1,e]) = 1.
- `NumberField.ideleHaar_neq_restrict_adele` (non-example): ideleHaar is not the restriction of adeleHaar to the units: the units are adeleHaar-null in 𝔸_K.
- `NumberField.ideleHaar_form_factor` (compatibility): At a finite place, the measure |dx/x|_v built from the additive normalization gives 𝒪_v^× volume 1 - q_v⁻¹, so ideleHaar is the product of (1 - q_v⁻¹)⁻¹|dx/x|_v.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The measure of ∏_v 𝒪_v^× × ∏_w {1 ≤ |x_w| ≤ e} is 1 for K = ℚ.
- It is invariant under multiplication by principal ideles.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `mathlib:NumberField.IdeleGroup`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.unitsEquiv`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The finite-place normalization, transported to units. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.
- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. Standard local normalizations.

### Products of form measures need convergence factors

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/tamagawa-convergence-failure` · theorem.

For K = ℚ and ω = dx/x on G_m, the local measures |ω|_p built from the additive normalization μ_p(ℤ_p) = 1 give vol(ℤ_p^×) = 1 - p⁻¹, and ∏_p (1 - p⁻¹) diverges to 0. Hence the family |ω|_p does not satisfy the normalization hypothesis of restricted-haar-product (cofinitely volume 1), and no rescaling by a single constant makes it do so; the Tamagawa measure of G_m uses the convergence factors λ_p = (1 - p⁻¹)⁻¹.

**Hypotheses.** K = ℚ; the analogous statement for a number field K uses the pole of the Dedekind zeta function at s = 1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. ℤ_p^× = ℤ_p ∖ pℤ_p has additive measure 1 - p⁻¹, and |x|_p = 1 on it.
2. ∏_p (1 - p⁻¹) = 0 because ∑_p 1/p diverges (Nat.Primes.not_summable_one_div).
3. So the partial products of the volumes of ∏_p ℤ_p^× tend to 0 and the normalization condition fails at infinitely many places.

**Acceptance checks.**

- This is the reason AA.2/tamagawa-measure inserts λ_v = L_v(X*(G), 1).
- With λ_p = (1 - p⁻¹)⁻¹ the corrected measures are the normalized idele measures of idele-haar.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/idele-haar`, `mathlib:Nat.Primes.not_summable_one_div`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. The failure of the naive product.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. Convergence factors.

### Directed suprema of compatible measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/directed-supremum-measure` · lemma.

Let X be a measurable space, (U_S) a countable directed family of measurable sets covering X and ν_S measures with ν_S supported on U_S and ν_{S'}|_{U_S} = ν_S for S ⊆ S'. Then E ↦ sup_S ν_S(E ∩ U_S) is a measure ν on X with ν|_{U_S} = ν_S for every S, and it is the unique measure with this property.

**Hypotheses.** (U_S) countable, directed and covering; compatibility ν_{S'}|_{U_S} = ν_S.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For a fixed S the map E ↦ ν_{S'}(E ∩ U_{S'}) is monotone in S' and its limit exists in [0, ∞].
2. Countable additivity: for disjoint E_n, exchange the two monotone suprema sup_S Σ_n = Σ_n sup_S (monotone convergence for series).
3. Restriction to U_S: for S' ⊇ S, ν_{S'}(E ∩ U_S) = ν_S(E ∩ U_S) by compatibility.
4. Uniqueness: two measures agreeing on each U_S agree on X = ⋃ U_S by countable additivity over the disjointified pieces.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For X = ℝ, U_n = [−n, n] and ν_n Lebesgue measure on U_n, the supremum is Lebesgue measure.

**Prerequisites:** .

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. The inductive limit of compatible measures.

### Normalized local Haar measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/local-normalized-haar` · lemma.

For a number field K and a finite place v there is a unique additive Haar measure μ_v on K_v with μ_v(𝒪_v) = 1; for a ∈ K_v^×, map (a · ·) μ_v = |a|_v⁻¹ • μ_v with |a|_v = q_v^{−v(a)}.

**Hypotheses.** K a number field; v a finite place.

**Construction or proof.**

1. K_v is a nonarchimedean local field (Tau Ceti), so locally compact with 𝒪_v compact open; normalize Mathlib's Haar measure so that 𝒪_v has volume one.
2. Scaling: multiplication by a uniformizer maps 𝒪_v onto 𝔪_v, of index q_v in 𝒪_v, so its volume is q_v⁻¹ (MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure); units preserve 𝒪_v.

**Acceptance checks.**

- μ_p(pℤ_p) = 1/p for K = ℚ.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum.isNonarchimedeanLocalField_adicCompletion`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.haarMeasure`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The normalization. The notes treat additive adele measures; the group-generic argument here is an adaptation, not a quoted general theorem.

### Topology of the infinite-adele mixed-space comparison

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/mixed-space-topology` · lemma.

The canonical ring equivalence ringEquiv_mixedSpace F and its inverse are continuous for the product topologies, so it upgrades to a ring homeomorphism.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Split the finite place-index set into real and complex subtypes. Use the pinned real and complex completion isometry equivalences coordinatewise, and reindex the products. Their homeomorphisms and the finite product homeomorphism have exactly the underlying ringEquiv_mixedSpace maps.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For ℚ the map is the real completion identification; for ℚ(i) it is the complex completion identification.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:NumberField.InfiniteAdeleRing.ringEquiv_mixedSpace`, `mathlib:NumberField.InfinitePlace.Completion.isometryEquivRealOfIsReal`, `mathlib:NumberField.InfinitePlace.Completion.isometryEquivComplexOfIsComplex`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 11. Supplies the archimedean product carrier. Continuity in this packet is deduced from the pinned local isometry equivalences, rather than attributed to this passage.

### Positive convergent products

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/summable-log-product` · lemma.

For a countable family a_i>0 of real numbers with ∑_i |a_i−1|<∞, the net of finite products has a positive finite limit C. Deleting a finite set S divides C by ∏_{i∈S}a_i; equivalently ∑ log a_i converges absolutely and C=exp(∑ log a_i).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Outside a finite set, |a_i−1|<1/2 and |log a_i|≤2|a_i−1|. Apply the logarithm estimate to the summable tail, and then exponentiate the convergent sum.
2. Split the sum into its finite initial part and its complement to obtain the deletion identity.

**Acceptance checks.**

- a_i=1 gives C=1.
- a_p=1−p⁻² gives a positive product; a_p=1−p⁻¹ fails the hypothesis.

**Prerequisites:** .

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The normalized restricted-product measure motivates this elementary real-analysis extension.

### Convergent restricted products of local Haar measures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/convergent-haar-product` · construction.

Let G_i be countably many second countable locally compact Hausdorff groups, B_i open and compact cofinitely, and μ_i left Haar measures. Choose finite S containing the noncompact B_i. Put a_i=μ_i(B_i)∈(0,∞) for i∉S, suppose ∑_{i∉S}|a_i−1|<∞, and let C_S=∏_{i∉S}a_i>0. Define the convergent product as C_S times the normalized restricted Haar product of μ_i for i∈S and a_i⁻¹μ_i for i∉S. It is independent of S.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The local normalizations have integral volume 1 off S, so the normalized restricted product exists.
2. The positive convergent product supplies a finite nonzero global rescaling.
3. For S⊂T, finite rescaling of the normalized family and C_T=C_S/∏_{T∖S}a_i cancel exactly. Compare arbitrary S,T through their union.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/tamagawa-measure`: constructs the unnormalized convergent product of λ_v|ω|_v without assuming eventual integral volume 1

**API.**

- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct` (constructor): Let G_i be countably many second countable locally compact Hausdorff groups, B_i open and compact cofinitely, and μ_i left Haar measures. Choose finite S containing the noncompact B_i. Put a_i=μ_i(B_i)∈(0,∞) for i∉S, suppose ∑_{i∉S}|a_i−1|<∞, and let C_S=∏_{i∉S}a_i>0. Define the convergent product as C_S times the normalized restricted Haar product of μ_i for i∈S and a_i⁻¹μ_i for i∉S. It is independent of S.
- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct_independent_exceptionalSet` (extensionality): Enlarging the finite exceptional set leaves the measure unchanged.
- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct_isHaarMeasure` (instance): The convergent product is a nonzero Haar measure.
- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct_box` (characterisation): If C_i=B_i off finite T⊃S, its box mass is (∏_{i∈T}μ_i(C_i))·∏_{i∉T}a_i.

**Unit tests.**

- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct_normalized` (compatibility): When every a_i=1 the measure equals haarProduct, including its integral box of mass 1.
- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct_single_rescale` (computation): Changing one local normalized Haar measure by c>0 multiplies the resulting measure by c.
- `RestrictedProduct.convergentHaarProduct_sl2_tail` (non-example): For SL₂ good factors a_v=1−q_v⁻², the integral box mass is their positive infinite product, strictly less than 1 for a nonempty tail; eventual equality to 1 is unnecessary.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For normalized μ_i this is haarProduct.
- For SL_n good-place masses ∏_{j=2}^n(1−q_i⁻ʲ), the convergent tail is retained.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-rescale`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/summable-log-product`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. The local normalized product is the input; the infinite-rescaling construction is supplied here.

### Independence under finite changes of integral subgroups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/convergent-product-independence` · lemma.

Changing compact open B_i at finitely many indices transports the convergent Haar product to the same measure on the canonically identified restricted product.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Include every changed index in S. The normalized families and their convergent tails then coincide. Apply restricted-haar-change-subgroups.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/convergent-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-change-subgroups`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrew V. Sutherland, *18.785 Number theory I, Lecture #23: The ring of adeles, strong approximation*](https://math.mit.edu/classes/18.785/2016fa/LectureNotes23.pdf), §23.3, p. 6. Independence of the finite exceptional set is the required adaptation.

## AA.1 — Adelic points and functoriality

Adelic points are the pinned convolution group of algebra maps, with the affine-points topology requested from RG2.0. Clear denominators in finite Hopf generators, relations and identities to obtain finitely presented integral models with specified generic-fibre Hopf isomorphisms. First prove the point bijection with the restricted product; then prove its topology comparison on principal pieces. Functoriality uses coordinate Hopf maps, and restriction of scalars uses the natural adjunction and continuous adelic tensor comparison.

The finite embedding has archimedean identity point. The GL_n integral model requires unit determinant in O_v. Rational points are discrete in full adeles; finite-adelic discreteness has the separately stated compact-open intersection criterion.

### Adelic points of an affine algebraic group

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points` · definition.

For a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra H over a number field F, G(𝔸_F) is the group of F-algebra maps H → 𝔸_F under convolution (TauCeti.HopfAlgebra.points H 𝔸_F), with the topology of affine points over the topological ring 𝔸_F (weakest topology making every evaluation h ↦ x(h) continuous). Likewise G(𝔸_{F,f}), G(F_∞) = G(F ⊗_ℚ ℝ) and G(F_v). The diagonal ι : G(F) → G(𝔸_F) is mapPoints along F → 𝔸_F and the local projection p_v : G(𝔸_F) → G(F_v) is mapPoints along the projection 𝔸_F → F_v.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Planet:** Adelic points of an algebraic group.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Evaluate the points functor on the F-algebras 𝔸_F, 𝔸_{F,f}, F_v (the adele ring is an F-algebra; the projections are F-algebra maps built from RestrictedProduct.evalRingHom).
2. Give each the affine-points topology supplied by RG2.0 for topological rings; it is a topological group because multiplication is composition with comultiplication and inversion with the antipode.

**Uses.**

- `AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`: identification with the restricted product of local points
- `AA.2/log-height`: domain of H_G
- `AA.3/class-number-finite`: double cosets G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/K
- `ShimuraData:D0`: adelic topological points of Shimura groups
- `MetaplecticAutomorphicForms:MP.4`: model-independent adelic points with local projections (request from MP.0)
- `ExcursionOperatorsAndSpectralAction:ES7:function-field-automorphic`: adelic points of D^× with the diagonal embedding (request from ES7)
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.2/proper-spinor-genus`: finite adelic points of spin groups with their restricted-product topology
- `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII:F4/adelic-class-set`: finite-adelic points of an endomorphism unit group
- `AA.3/component-decomposition`: The right finite level is U.map finiteEmbed, so quotienting by it preserves the infinite factor.

**API.**

- `AdelicPoints` (constructor): AdelicPoints H := WithConv (H →ₐ[F] 𝔸_F), a group under convolution.
- `AdelicPoints.instTopologicalSpace` (instance): The affine-points topology: induced from 𝔸_F^H by evaluation.
- `AdelicPoints.instIsTopologicalGroup` (instance): AdelicPoints H is a topological group.
- `AdelicPoints.diagonal` (data): The diagonal G(F) →* AdelicPoints H, mapPoints along algebraMap F 𝔸_F.
- `AdelicPoints.proj` (projection): For a finite place v, the continuous homomorphism AdelicPoints H →* G(F_v).
- `AdelicPoints.continuous_eval` (simp): For h ∈ H, x ↦ x h is continuous AdelicPoints H → 𝔸_F.
- `AdelicPoints.proj_diagonal` (simp): proj v (diagonal g) is the image of g in G(F_v).
- `AdelicPoints.finiteEmbed` (constructor): The finite-supported homomorphism G(𝔸_f)→G(𝔸), x↦(1,x), under the canonical archimedean/finite splitting. This is an embedding of point groups, not a ring inclusion with archimedean coordinate zero.
- `AdelicPoints.finiteEmbed_finite` (simp): The finite projection of finiteEmbed x equals x.
- `AdelicPoints.finiteEmbed_infinite` (simp): The infinite projection of finiteEmbed x equals 1.

**Unit tests.**

- `AdelicPoints.ga_eq_adeles` (compatibility): For H = F[T] with additive comultiplication, AdelicPoints H ≃ₜ+ 𝔸_F (see ga-adelic).
- `AdelicPoints.trivial_group` (degenerate): For H = F (the trivial group), AdelicPoints H is the one-point group.
- `AdelicPoints.not_product_topology` (non-example): For H = F[T, T⁻¹] the topology is not the subspace topology of 𝔸_F: the inversion map is not continuous for the subspace topology on 𝔸_F^×.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For H the coordinate ring of G_a, G(𝔸_F) is 𝔸_F with its own topology.
- For H the GL_n coordinate ring, G(𝔸_F) is GL_n(𝔸_F) with the units topology.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.HopfAlgebra.points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.HopfAlgebra.mapPoints`, `tauceti:TauCeti.HopfAlgebra.pointsFunctor`, `mathlib:NumberField.AdeleRing`, `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain.FiniteAdeleRing`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.evalRingHom`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-4-finite-adeles`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Proposition 2.1, p. 2. The topology on G(𝔸_F) for affine G.
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 11. The adelic group and its rational points.

### Integral models over S-integers

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model` · definition.

An integral model of H away from a finite set S of places of F (containing the archimedean places) is a finitely presented commutative Hopf algebra 𝓗 over the S-integers 𝒪_{F,S} together with an isomorphism of Hopf algebras F ⊗_{𝒪_{F,S}} 𝓗 ≅ H. For v ∉ S its integral points are 𝓗(𝒪_v) = Hom_{𝒪_{F,S}}(𝓗, 𝒪_v) ⊂ G(F_v).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G); S a finite set of places containing the archimedean ones.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define the structure: the Hopf algebra 𝓗, the base-change isomorphism, and the map 𝓗(𝒪_v) → G(F_v) induced by 𝒪_v → F_v.

**Uses.**

- `AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`: the restricting subgroups 𝓗(𝒪_v)
- `AA.2/tamagawa-convergence`: smooth good models at almost all places
- `AA.3/good-maximal-compact`: K_v = 𝓗(𝒪_v) hyperspecial for almost all v

**API.**

- `IntegralModel` (structure): A finitely presented commutative Hopf algebra over O_{F,S} and a specified Hopf isomorphism of its F-generic fibre with H; S is finite and all infinite places are understood to be included.
- `IntegralModel.localPoints` (data): For v ∉ S, the subgroup 𝓗(𝒪_v) of G(F_v).
- `IntegralModel.enlarge` (functoriality): For S ⊆ S′, the base-changed model over 𝒪_{F,S′}, with localPoints unchanged at v ∉ S′.
- `IntegralModel.localPoints_injective` (characterisation): The map 𝓗(𝒪_v) → G(F_v) induced by the injection 𝒪_v → F_v is injective.

**Unit tests.**

- `IntegralModel.gln_localPoints` (computation): For the standard model of GL_n, localPoints v = GL_n(𝒪_v) (invertible determinant), not all integral matrices with nonzero determinant.
- `IntegralModel.trivial` (degenerate): For the trivial group the localPoints are the trivial subgroup.
- `IntegralModel.monoid_not_model` (non-example): The 𝒪_{F,S}-bialgebra 𝒪_{F,S}[T] with T ↦ T ⊗ T is not an integral model of G_m: its generic fibre F[T] has no antipode, although its F-points contain F^×.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For H = F[GL_n] the model 𝒪_{F,S}[GL_n] has integral points GL_n(𝒪_v).
- For S ⊆ S′, base change gives a model over 𝒪_{F,S′}.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.HopfAlgebra.points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.baseChangePointsMulEquiv`, `mathlib:Set.integer`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Remark 3.5 and §3, p. 6. Spreading out to an S-integral model.

### Spreading out

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model-exists` · lemma.

Every finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra H over F has an integral model away from some finite set S of places.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose finitely many generators and finitely many polynomial relations for H. Over a field finite type is finite presentation.
2. Clear the finitely many denominators in the relations, comultiplication, counit, antipode and their polynomial identities. The resulting finitely presented S-integral Hopf algebra has the specified generic-fibre Hopf isomorphism.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For H = F[GL_n] one may take S the archimedean places.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/hopf-spreading`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.4(1), p. 5. Descent of finitely presented objects through the directed system of S-integers.

### Uniqueness of integral models up to enlarging S

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model-unique` · lemma.

Two integral models 𝓗, 𝓗′ of H (away from S and S′) become isomorphic, compatibly with their identifications with H, after base change to 𝒪_{F,S″} for some finite S″ ⊇ S ∪ S′. Consequently 𝓗(𝒪_v) = 𝓗′(𝒪_v) inside G(F_v) for all but finitely many v.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply Conrad, Theorem 3.4(3) to the two descents of the same finitely presented F-algebra, then to the Hopf structure maps.
2. For v ∉ S″ the isomorphism identifies the integral points inside G(F_v).

**Acceptance checks.**

- The models 𝒪_{F,S}[GL_2] and its conjugate by diag(p, 1) agree away from S ∪ {p}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model-exists`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/hopf-spreading`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.4(3), p. 5. Uniqueness of models.

### Integral points are compact open subgroups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-points-level` · lemma.

For an integral model 𝓗 away from S and a finite place v ∉ S, 𝓗(𝒪_v) is a compact open subgroup of G(F_v).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. 𝒪_v is open and compact in F_v; by RG2.0 (Conrad, Example 2.3 and Corollary 3.7) X(𝒪_v) is open and compact in X(F_v) for X affine of finite type over 𝒪_v.
2. It is a subgroup because the group structure is defined over 𝒪_{F,S}.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_n(ℤ_p) is compact open in GL_n(ℚ_p).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Example 2.3, p. 3. Openness; compactness is Corollary 3.7.

### Adelic points as a restricted product

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison` · theorem.

Let 𝓗 be an integral model of H away from S. The map x ↦ (p_v(x))_v is an isomorphism of topological groups G(𝔸_{F,f}) ≃ₜ* Πʳ v, [G(F_v), B_v], where B_v = 𝓗(𝒪_v) for v ∉ S and B_v = G(F_v) for the finitely many finite v ∈ S; and G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* G(F_∞) × G(𝔸_{F,f}).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G); 𝓗 an integral model away from S.

**Planet:** Restricted-product comparison.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Points over a product of rings are the product of points; 𝔸_F = F_∞ × 𝔸_{F,f}.
2. Set-theoretic bijection: an F-algebra map H → 𝔸_{F,f} factors through 𝒪_{F,S′}-points of 𝓗 with values in 𝔸_{F,S′} for some S′ (H finitely generated, Theorem 3.4 limit argument), i.e. lands in ∏_{v∈S′} G(F_v) × ∏_{v∉S′} 𝓗(𝒪_v); conversely such a family glues (Conrad, Theorem 3.6).
3. Topology: on each 𝔸_{F,S′} the affine-points topology is the product topology (Conrad, Theorem 3.6, affine case), and both sides carry the direct-limit topology over S′ with open transition maps (RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = G_a it is FiniteAdeleRing as a restricted product (definitional up to the identification).
- For G = GL_n it identifies GL_n(𝔸_f) with Πʳ_v [GL_n(F_v), GL_n(𝒪_v)].

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-points-level`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/split-finite-factors`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-adeles-directed-union`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-bijection`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-topology`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.6, p. 6. The comparison on each A_{F,S}, passed to the limit over S.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.2, p. 7. Weil's restricted-product definition of G_A.

### Independence of the model and of the exceptional set

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-model-independence` · theorem.

The topological group structure on Πʳ v, [G(F_v), 𝓗(𝒪_v)] obtained from restricted-product-comparison does not depend on the integral model 𝓗 or on S: for two models the identity of G(𝔸_{F,f}) corresponds to the canonical isomorphism of RestrictedProduct.changeSubgroups, and enlarging S does not change it.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Two models give the same B_v for all but finitely many v (integral-model-unique).
2. Apply restricted-haar-change-subgroups (its topological part); both comparisons are compatible with the projections p_v, which determine the map.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = GL_2 the models 𝒪[GL_2] and its conjugate by diag(p,1) give the same topology on GL_2(𝔸_f).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model-unique`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-change-subgroups`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.6, p. 6. Independence of the finite set; model independence follows from Theorem 3.4(3).

### Adelic groups are locally compact

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-locally-compact` · theorem.

G(𝔸_F), G(𝔸_{F,f}) and G(F_∞) are second countable, locally compact, Hausdorff topological groups.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. By restricted-product-comparison G(𝔸_{F,f}) is a restricted product of locally compact groups G(F_v) with compact open subgroups 𝓗(𝒪_v) for almost all v; apply RestrictedProduct.locallyCompactSpace_of_group and AA.0/second-countable.
2. G(F_∞) is a closed subgroup of GL_n(F ⊗ ℝ) for a faithful representation (RG2.0).
3. Hausdorffness follows from the closed-embedding property of affine points over a Hausdorff ring.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_1(𝔸_ℚ) = 𝔸_ℚ^× is locally compact.
- The full product ∏_p GL_n(ℚ_p) with the product topology is not locally compact; the restricted product is.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.locallyCompactSpace_of_group`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/second-countable`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-5-full-adeles-and-the-additive-quotient`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.2, p. 7. Local compactness of the adele group.

### Rational points are discrete in the full adeles

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete` · theorem.

The diagonal G(F) → G(𝔸_F) is injective, its image is a discrete subgroup, and the image is closed.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Planet:** Discreteness of rational points.

**Construction or proof.**

1. F is discrete and closed in 𝔸_F (Tau Ceti GlobalNumberFields discreteness).
2. For affine X, a discrete subring R ⊂ R′ gives X(R) discrete in X(R′) (Conrad, Example 2.2): choose generators h_1, …, h_n of H; the evaluation embedding sends X(R) into the discrete set R^n.
3. A discrete subgroup of a Hausdorff topological group is closed (Subgroup.isClosed_of_discrete).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = G_a this is discreteness of F in 𝔸_F.
- For G = SL_2 over ℚ, SL_2(ℚ) is discrete in SL_2(𝔸_ℚ).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.discreteTopology_principalSubgroup`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.isClosed_principalSubgroup`, `mathlib:Subgroup.isClosed_of_discrete`, `tauceti:NumberField.AdeleRing.instT2Space`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Example 2.3, p. 3. Exactly the statement.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.2, p. 7. Discreteness of principal adeles.

### Discreteness in the finite adeles alone

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-adelic-discreteness-criterion` · theorem.

G(F) is discrete in G(𝔸_{F,f}) if and only if G(F) ∩ U is finite for one (equivalently every) compact open subgroup U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}). In particular G_a(F) = F is not discrete in 𝔸_{F,f}, SL_2(ℚ) is not discrete in SL_2(𝔸_{ℚ,f}) because SL_2(ℤ) is infinite, and ℚ^× is discrete in 𝔸_{ℚ,f}^× because ℚ^× ∩ ∏_p ℤ_p^× = {±1}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. If G(F) ∩ U is finite then a smaller open neighbourhood of 1 meets G(F) only in 1, since G(𝔸_{F,f}) is Hausdorff.
2. Conversely a discrete subgroup meets the compact U in a finite set.
3. Compact open subgroups are commensurable, so finiteness for one U gives it for all.
4. Examples: ℤ ⊂ ℚ ∩ ∏_p ℤ_p is infinite; SL_2(ℤ) = SL_2(ℚ) ∩ SL_2(ℤ̂) is infinite; ℚ^× ∩ ℤ̂^× = {±1}.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For a definite quaternion algebra D over ℚ, D^× is discrete in (D ⊗ 𝔸_f)^× modulo the centre (AA.5/definite-quaternion-compact).
- Discreteness of G(F) in G(𝔸_F) does not imply discreteness in G(𝔸_{F,f}).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-locally-compact`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.8, p. 9. Discreteness after projecting away a compact factor; the converse direction is the finiteness criterion.

### Splitting off finitely many places

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split` · lemma.

For a finite set S of places, G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* G(F_S) × G(𝔸_F^S), with G(F_S) = ∏_{v∈S} G(F_v) and G(𝔸_F^S) the adelic points away from S; in particular G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* G(F_∞) × G(𝔸_{F,f}). The diagonal G(F) maps to the pair of diagonals.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Combine restricted-product-comparison with split-finite-factors, and points of a product of rings.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For S = {∞} and F = ℚ, G(𝔸) = G(ℝ) × G(𝔸_f).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/split-finite-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/points-product-ring`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.2, p. 7. Exactly this decomposition.

### Functoriality of adelic points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map` · construction.

A homomorphism of affine algebraic groups φ : G → G′ over F (a Hopf algebra map φ* : H′ → H) induces a continuous homomorphism φ_𝔸 : G(𝔸_F) → G′(𝔸_F), x ↦ x ∘ φ*, commuting with the diagonals and with the local projections, with (id)_𝔸 = id and (ψ ∘ φ)_𝔸 = ψ_𝔸 ∘ φ_𝔸. Under restricted-product-comparison it is the restricted product of the local maps φ_v, which send 𝓗(𝒪_v) into 𝓗′(𝒪_v) for almost all v.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G); φ a homomorphism of affine algebraic groups over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Precomposition with φ* is a group homomorphism of convolution groups (TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.pointsFunctor).
2. Continuity: each evaluation of φ_𝔸(x) at h′ is the evaluation of x at φ*(h′).
3. φ* is defined over 𝒪_{F,S} for S large (integral-model-unique), giving the restricted-product description (mapAlong_continuous).

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/log-height`: rational characters χ : G → G_m give χ_𝔸 : G(𝔸) → 𝔸^×
- `AA.4/plus-subgroup`: the image of the simply connected cover
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.2/proper-spinor-genus`: induced spin-cover maps on finite adelic points
- `ShimuraData:D0`: morphisms of Shimura data on adelic points

**API.**

- `AdelicPoints.map` (constructor): AdelicPoints.map φ : AdelicPoints H →* AdelicPoints H′ for a Hopf algebra map H′ → H.
- `AdelicPoints.continuous_map` (functoriality): AdelicPoints.map φ is continuous.
- `AdelicPoints.map_id` (functoriality): AdelicPoints.map (id) = id.
- `AdelicPoints.map_comp` (functoriality): AdelicPoints.map (φ ∘ ψ) = AdelicPoints.map ψ ∘ AdelicPoints.map φ (contravariance on Hopf algebras).
- `AdelicPoints.map_diagonal` (compatibility): map φ (diagonal g) = diagonal (φ g).
- `AdelicPoints.proj_map` (compatibility): proj v ∘ map φ = φ_v ∘ proj v.

**Unit tests.**

- `AdelicPoints.map_det_gl1` (computation): For GL₁, the adelic determinant point map corresponds under the canonical GL₁-to-units identification to the identity on the idele group; explicitly its G_m unit is the one-by-one matrix determinant.
- `AdelicPoints.map_trivial` (degenerate): The map to the trivial group is the constant map.
- `AdelicPoints.map_not_open` (non-example): For G_m over ℚ the image (𝔸_ℚ^×)² is closed and has infinite index. It is not open: every basic identity neighbourhood allows arbitrary units at almost all odd primes, including nonsquare units. Thus the adelic squaring map need not be surjective or open.

**Acceptance checks.**

- det : GL_n → G_m induces det : GL_n(𝔸_F) → 𝔸_F^×.
- The identity homomorphism induces the identity.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.pointsFunctor`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model-unique`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.mapAlong_continuous`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GeneralLinear.determinantCoordinateMap`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GeneralLinear.pointsMulEquiv_determinantPoints`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.3, p. 7. Exactly this functoriality.

### Closed subgroups give closed embeddings

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic` · lemma.

If H′ = H/I for a Hopf ideal I (a closed subgroup G′ ⊂ G), the induced map G′(𝔸_F) → G(𝔸_F) is a closed embedding of topological groups with image quotientPointsSubgroup H I 𝔸_F.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G); I a Hopf ideal of H.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Closed immersions induce closed embeddings on affine points over the Hausdorff ring 𝔸_F (RG2.0, Conrad Proposition 2.1).
2. The image is the set of points vanishing on I (TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.quotientPointsSubgroup).

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_n(𝔸_F) is closed in GL_n(𝔸_F).
- The diagonal torus of GL_n gives a closed subgroup (𝔸_F^×)^n.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.quotientPointsSubgroup`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`, `tauceti:NumberField.AdeleRing.instT2Space`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Proposition 2.1, p. 2. Exactly this property.

### Products of groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/product-adelic` · lemma.

For affine algebraic groups G, G′ over F, (G × G′)(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* G(𝔸_F) × G′(𝔸_F), compatibly with diagonals and local projections.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Points of H ⊗_F H′ are pairs of points (TauCeti.AffineGroup.Product.pointsMulEquiv).
2. The affine-points topology is compatible with fibre products (RG2.0, Conrad Proposition 2.1).

**Acceptance checks.**

- (G_m × G_m)(𝔸) = 𝔸^× × 𝔸^×.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.AffineGroup.Product.pointsMulEquiv`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Proposition 2.1, proof, p. 2. Compatibility with products.

### The centre on adelic points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/center-adelic` · lemma.

Let Z ⊂ G be the centre (centerDefiningIdeal). Then Z(𝔸_F) is a closed subgroup of G(𝔸_F) contained in the centre of G(𝔸_F), and Z(F) = Z(𝔸_F) ∩ G(F).
