**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply closed-subgroup-adelic to the centre Hopf ideal.
2. Points of the centre are universally central points (centerPointsSubgroup_eq_center), hence central in G(𝔸_F).
3. Intersection with G(F): a rational point lies in Z(𝔸_F) iff it vanishes on the defining ideal, i.e. lies in Z(F).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n, Z(𝔸_F) = 𝔸_F^× scalar matrices.
- For SL_2, Z(𝔸_F) = {±1}^{places} ∩ restricted product, an infinite compact group.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.centerDefiningIdeal`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.centerPointsSubgroup_eq_center`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.6, p. 8. Compatibility of the adelic functor with normal subgroups and decompositions, used here for the centre.

### Restriction of scalars on adelic points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic` · construction.

For a finite extension E/F and an affine algebraic group G_E over E, with Res = Res_{E/F} G_E, there is an isomorphism of topological groups Res(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* G_E(𝔸_E), natural in G_E, compatible with Res(F) = G_E(E) on diagonals.

**Hypotheses.** E/F a finite extension of number fields; G_E an affine algebraic group over E; The Weil restriction and the completion/adelic base-change maps are the natural adjunction and canonical tensor-product maps; arbitrary point equivalences are excluded.; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Res(𝔸_F) = G_E(E ⊗_F 𝔸_F) by the defining property of Weil restriction (RG2.0a).
2. E ⊗_F 𝔸_F ≃ 𝔸_E as topological E-algebras (GlobalNumberFields layer 8).
3. Topologies: the module topology on E ⊗_F 𝔸_F is the one used by the affine-points topology (Conrad, Example 4.2).

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/tamagawa-restriction-scalars`: transport of Tamagawa measures
- `AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`: reduction to F = ℚ
- `BorelRegulators:R.6/restriction-scalars-form`: Res_{F/ℚ} of SL_1(D)
- `ShimuraData:D0`: restriction-of-scalars point comparisons

**API.**

- `AdelicPoints.resEquiv` (constructor): The isomorphism of topological groups Res_{E/F}(G_E)(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* G_E(𝔸_E).
- `AdelicPoints.resEquiv_diagonal` (compatibility): resEquiv carries the diagonal of Res(F) to the diagonal of G_E(E).
- `AdelicPoints.resEquiv_natural` (functoriality): resEquiv is natural in homomorphisms G_E → G′_E.
- `AdelicPoints.resEquiv_trans` (functoriality): In a tower F ⊂ E ⊂ L, resEquiv for L/F is the composite of those for L/E and E/F.

**Unit tests.**

- `AdelicPoints.resEquiv_gm` (computation): For G_E = G_m, resEquiv is 𝔸_E^× ≃ (E ⊗ 𝔸_F)^×.
- `AdelicPoints.resEquiv_self` (degenerate): For E = F, resEquiv is the identity.
- `AdelicPoints.res_not_base_change` (non-example): Res_{E/F}(G_E) is not the base change of G_E: for E = ℚ(i), Res G_m(ℚ) = ℚ(i)^× while G_m(ℚ) = ℚ^×.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_E = G_m, Res_{E/F} G_m(𝔸_F) = 𝔸_E^×.
- For E = F it is the identity.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0a`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-8-finite-extensions-of-adeles-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Example 4.2, p. 10. The topological comparison for Weil restriction.
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 11. Reduction to F = ℚ by restriction of scalars.

### Local factors of restriction of scalars

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors` · lemma.

Under base-change-adelic, the projection to F_v corresponds to Res(F_v) ≃ ∏_{w|v} G_E(E_w), and for almost all v the integral points of a model of Res correspond to ∏_{w|v} 𝓗_E(𝒪_w).

**Hypotheses.** E/F finite; G_E affine over E; The Weil restriction and the completion/adelic base-change maps are the natural adjunction and canonical tensor-product maps; arbitrary point equivalences are excluded.; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. E ⊗_F F_v ≃ ∏_{w|v} E_w (GlobalNumberFields layer 8 local decomposition).
2. Points of a product of rings are products of points; Res of a model of G_E is a model of Res for almost all v.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For Res_{ℚ(i)/ℚ} G_m at p ≡ 1 mod 4, Res(ℚ_p) = ℚ_p^× × ℚ_p^×.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-8-finite-extensions-of-adeles-and-ideles`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0a`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.4, p. 8. Restriction of the ground field on rational and adelic points.

### The additive group

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/ga-adelic` · lemma.

For G = G_a (H = F[T] with T primitive), G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ+ 𝔸_F, the diagonal is algebraMap F 𝔸_F and restricted-product-comparison recovers FiniteAdeleRing as a restricted product.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. An F-algebra map F[T] → 𝔸_F is its value at T; the topology induced by evaluation at T is that of 𝔸_F.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The Haar measure of AA.0/adele-haar is a Haar measure on G_a(𝔸_F).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `mathlib:NumberField.AdeleRing`, `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain.FiniteAdeleRing`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 11. The additive case.

### The multiplicative group and the ideles

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic` · lemma.

For G = G_m, G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* NumberField.IdeleGroup (𝓞 F) F = 𝔸_F^× with the units topology, and under restricted-product-comparison the finite part is Πʳ v, [F_v^×, 𝒪_v^×] via RestrictedProduct.unitsEquiv.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. TauCeti.MultiplicativeGroup.pointsMulEquiv identifies the group with 𝔸_F^×.
2. The affine-points topology for F[T, T⁻¹] is induced by x ↦ (x, x⁻¹), which is Mathlib's units topology.
3. GlobalNumberFields layer 6 identifies the units topology with the restricted-product topology.

**Acceptance checks.**

- F^× ⊂ 𝔸_F^× is discrete (rational-points-discrete).
- The idele norm is the composition with the character id ∈ X*(G_m) (AA.2/log-height).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.MultiplicativeGroup.pointsMulEquiv`, `mathlib:NumberField.IdeleGroup`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.unitsEquiv`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.3, p. 7. GL_1(𝔸) is the idele group.

### The general linear group

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gln-adelic` · lemma.

For G = GL_n, G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* GL_n(𝔸_F) = (Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) 𝔸_F)ˣ with the units topology, and its finite part is the restricted product Πʳ v, [GL_n(F_v), GL_n(𝒪_v)].

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; n ≥ 1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. TauCeti.GeneralLinear.pointsMulEquiv.
2. The coordinate ring is generated by the entries and det⁻¹, so the topology is induced by g ↦ (g, g⁻¹).
3. Apply restricted-product-comparison to the standard model over 𝒪_F.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For n = 1 it is gm-adelic.
- GL_n(𝒪_v) is not the set of integral matrices with nonzero determinant.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GeneralLinear.pointsMulEquiv`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 11. The basic example.

### Local unimodularity of reductive groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/local-unimodular-reductive` · lemma.

For a connected reductive group G over a local field E of characteristic 0, the locally compact group G(E) is unimodular.

**Hypotheses.** E a local field of characteristic 0; G connected reductive over E.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The modular character Δ : G(E) → ℝ_{>0} is a continuous homomorphism, trivial on compact subgroups and on the centre Z(E), since Δ(g) is the modulus of conjugation by g.
2. Cartan decomposition G(E) = K M(E) K with M = Z_G(A) for a maximal split torus A (RG2.4 nonarchimedean, LieGroups layer 9 archimedean); so Δ is determined by its restriction to M(E), and M(E)/A(E)·M(E)^1 is finite with M(E)^1 compact, so Δ is determined by Δ|_{A(E)}.
3. For a ∈ A(E), the product ∏_{w∈W} w(a) of the relative Weyl orbit is W-invariant, hence a power of it lies in the split centre; Weyl representatives lie in K, so Δ(w a w⁻¹) = Δ(a), giving Δ(a)^{|W|·m} = 1 and Δ(a) = 1.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_n(ℚ_p) and SL_n(ℝ) are unimodular.
- The Borel subgroup of GL_2(ℚ_p) is not unimodular: Δ(diag(a,d)) = |a/d|_p^{±1}.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.modularCharacter`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/modular-character-trivial-compact-centre`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/weyl-orbit-product-central`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 20. Unimodularity is checked at the local factors.

### Adelic groups of reductive groups are unimodular

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/unimodular-reductive` · theorem.

For a connected reductive group G over a number field F, G(𝔸_F), G(𝔸_{F,f}) and G(F_∞) are unimodular.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G connected reductive over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Each G(F_v) is unimodular (local-unimodular-reductive).
2. A restricted product of unimodular groups is unimodular (AA.0/restricted-unimodular, via restricted-product-comparison).

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_n(𝔸_F) is unimodular; the adelic points of the upper triangular Borel subgroup of GL_2 are not.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/local-unimodular-reductive`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-unimodular`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 20. Borel's sufficient condition; for reductive groups the local argument applies even when X_k(G) ≠ 1.

### Compact open subgroups and product levels

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product` · lemma.

Every compact open subgroup U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}) contains a product subgroup ∏_v U_v with U_v ⊂ G(F_v) compact open and U_v = 𝓗(𝒪_v) for all but finitely many v, and is contained in such a product; any two compact open subgroups are commensurable.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. By restricted-product-comparison and the box basis of the restricted-product topology (AA.0/second-countable), U contains an open box ∏_v U_v with U_v = 𝓗(𝒪_v) cofinitely.
2. U is compact, so its projections p_v(U) are compact and U ⊂ ∏_v p_v(U), with p_v(U) ⊂ 𝓗(𝒪_v) for almost all v (U lies in finitely many translates of ∏ 𝓗(𝒪_v)).
3. Commensurability: U ∩ U′ is open in the compact U, hence of finite index.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_2(ℤ̂) ⊃ K(N) = ker(GL_2(ℤ̂) → GL_2(ℤ/N)) = ∏_p K_p(N) is a product level.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/second-countable`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.7, p. 8. Commensurability of compact opens.

### Conjugation changes a level at finitely many places

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-support-conjugate` · lemma.

For g ∈ G(𝔸_{F,f}) and an integral model 𝓗, g_v ∈ 𝓗(𝒪_v) for all but finitely many v; hence for a product level U = ∏_v U_v, the conjugate gUg⁻¹ = ∏_v g_v U_v g_v⁻¹ agrees with U at all but finitely many v, and the element g can be written as g_B · u with g_B supported on a finite set B of places and u ∈ ∏_v 𝓗(𝒪_v).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; H a finitely generated commutative Hopf algebra over F (the coordinate ring of an affine algebraic group G).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Restricted-product condition (restricted-product-comparison).
2. For v with g_v ∈ 𝓗(𝒪_v) = U_v conjugation preserves U_v.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For g = diag(p, 1) ∈ GL_2(𝔸_f), gGL_2(ℤ̂)g⁻¹ differs from GL_2(ℤ̂) only at p.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.2, p. 7. Finite support of an adelic element modulo integral points.

### Points over a product of rings

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/points-product-ring` · lemma.

For commutative F-algebras R₁, R₂ (and more generally a finite product), WithConv (H →ₐ[F] R₁ × R₂) ≃* WithConv (H →ₐ[F] R₁) × WithConv (H →ₐ[F] R₂), naturally and as topological groups for the affine-points topology.

**Hypotheses.** H a commutative Hopf algebra over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. An algebra map into a product is a pair of algebra maps (AlgHom.prod); convolution is computed componentwise.
2. The affine-points topology is induced from R^H, which is the product of R₁^H and R₂^H.

**Acceptance checks.**

- G(𝔸_F) = G(F_∞) × G(𝔸_{F,f}) because 𝔸_F = F_∞ × 𝔸_{F,f}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `mathlib:AlgHom.prod`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Proposition 2.1, proof, p. 2. Compatibility with products, here applied to a product of coefficient rings.

### Finite adeles as a directed union of S-adeles

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-adeles-directed-union` · lemma.

For finite sets S of finite places, the S-adeles 𝔸_{F,S} = ∏_{v∈S} F_v × ∏_{v∉S} 𝒪_v are open subrings of 𝔸_{F,f} forming a directed union, and every F-algebra map H → 𝔸_{F,f} from a finitely generated H restricts to a map of models 𝓗 → 𝔸_{F,S} for S large: G(𝔸_{F,f}) = ⋃_S 𝓗(𝔸_{F,S}).

**Hypotheses.** H finitely generated; 𝓗 an integral model; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Each generator of H has finitely many denominators, so its image lies in 𝔸_{F,S} for S large.
2. Conrad, Theorem 3.4(1) applied to the directed system 𝒪_{F,S} → 𝔸_{F,S} identifies the points over the limit with the limit of the points (Remark 3.5).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_a: 𝔸_{F,f} = ⋃_S 𝔸_{F,S}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model-exists`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.isOpenEmbedding_inclusion_principal`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Remark 3.5, p. 6. The direct-limit description of adelic points.

### The restricted-product bijection on S-adelic points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-bijection` · lemma.

For a fixed affine finitely presented model over O_{F,S}, the evaluation map from its S′-adelic points to ∏_{v∈S′}G(F_v)×∏_{v∉S′}𝓗(O_v), for finite S′⊃S, is a bijection. This is a set and group statement; the topology is proved separately.

**Hypotheses.** 𝓗 affine over 𝒪_{F,S}; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use Hom(A,∏R_v)=∏Hom(A,R_v) for an affine algebra, which follows by evaluating each generator and each relation coordinatewise.
2. The generic-fibre Hopf isomorphism identifies the exceptional field factors, and the remaining factors are the integral point groups. Hopf compatibility preserves multiplication and inversion.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n: GL_n(𝔸_{F,S}) = ∏_{v∈S} GL_n(F_v) × ∏_{v∉S} GL_n(𝒪_v).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/points-product-ring`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.6, p. 6. Exactly the statement.

### The modular character on compact and central elements

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/modular-character-trivial-compact-centre` · lemma.

For a locally compact group G, the modular character Δ : G → ℝ_{>0} is a continuous homomorphism trivial on every compact subgroup and on the centre of G, and it is invariant under conjugation.

**Hypotheses.** G locally compact.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Δ(g) is the scalar by which conjugation by g rescales Haar measure (Mathlib modularCharacter).
2. A compact subgroup maps to a compact subgroup of ℝ_{>0}, which is trivial.
3. Conjugation by a central element is the identity.
4. Δ is a homomorphism to an abelian group, hence constant on conjugacy classes.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the Borel of GL_2(ℚ_p), Δ is trivial on the compact diagonal units diag(ℤ_p^×, ℤ_p^×).

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.modularCharacter`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 20. Compact quotients do not affect the modular character.

### Weyl orbit products lie in the split centre up to finite index

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/weyl-orbit-product-central` · lemma.

Let A be a maximal split torus of a connected reductive group over a field and W its relative Weyl group. For a ∈ A(E), the product ∏_{w∈W} w(a) lies in (Z(G) ∩ A)(E) up to an element of a finite group; in particular some power of it lies in the split centre.

**Hypotheses.** G connected reductive; A maximal split torus.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The product is W-invariant.
2. The W-invariants of X_*(A) ⊗ ℚ are X_*(Z ∩ A) ⊗ ℚ (W acts without nonzero fixed vectors on the span of the coroots).
3. So the W-invariant subtorus is the identity component of Z ∩ A, and A^W is a finite extension of it.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2 and a = diag(x, y): a · w(a) = diag(xy, xy) is central.

**Prerequisites:** `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 20. The unimodularity this lemma serves.

### Spreading Hopf structure and its identities

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/hopf-spreading` · lemma.

A finitely presented affine F-algebra with Hopf structure descends to a finitely presented Hopf algebra over O_{F,S} after enlarging finite S. A prescribed finite collection of Hopf morphisms and their identities descends simultaneously.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement.; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Represent the structure maps on finite algebra generators. Clear all coefficient denominators in the images and relations.
2. Every Hopf identity is equality of maps from a finitely presented algebra and so is a finite list of polynomial identities; enlarge S until the identities hold.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The antipode is included: a bialgebra alone is insufficient.
- Two inverse Hopf maps spread out as inverse maps.

**Prerequisites:** .

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.4(1)–(2), pp. 5–6; Remark 3.5, p. 6. Finite presentation and finitely many structure maps spread out together.

### Topology of the restricted-product comparison

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-topology` · lemma.

The bijection G(A_{F,f})→∏ʳ_v[G(F_v),B_v] induced by coordinate projections is a homeomorphism. On each S-integral principal piece it is the product homeomorphism of affine points, and the principal pieces are open on both sides.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement.; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. On an affine finite-presentation model, use finitely many evaluation coordinates to identify its topology with the closed solution locus in a finite power of the topological ring.
2. Those coordinates commute with the product decomposition of A_{F,S}. Openness of O_v⊂F_v gives open transition maps. Apply the directed-union topology to the set-theoretic bijection.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-bijection`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-adeles-directed-union`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`, `mathlib:RestrictedProduct.topologicalSpace_eq_iSup`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Theorem 3.6, pp. 7–8; §4, p. 9. The proof separates the point bijection from the topology comparison.

### Naturality of adelic restriction of scalars

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/weil-restriction-naturality` · lemma.

For the Weil-restriction adjunction Res_{E/F}G(R)≃G(E⊗_F R), the adelic comparison commutes with every algebraic group morphism, the diagonal F→A_F, projections to F_v, and the canonical tensor associator for towers E/F/k.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement.; F is a number field and O(G) is a finite-type commutative Hopf F-algebra; integral generic-fibre identifications respect the Hopf structure..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use naturality of the adjunction in R and in G before transporting through E⊗_F A_F≃A_E.
2. The diagonal and local squares are the corresponding value-algebra naturality squares. Tower compatibility follows from the adjunction and tensor associator, whose counits identify the same map.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0a`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-8-finite-extensions-of-adeles-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [Brian Conrad, *Weil and Grothendieck approaches to adelic points*](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/adelictop.pdf), Examples 2.4 and 4.2, pp. 3 and 9–10. The comparisons are canonical and natural in the value algebra.

## AA.2 — Characters, heights and integration

Rational characters are actual group-like elements. Their real dual is Hom_ℤ(X*_F(G),ℝ), and the idele norm gives H_G. For reductive groups the split component identifies with that real dual and gives the norm-one decomposition. For a closed subgroup with matching modular characters, right-Haar fibre averaging, compact quotient lifts and cutoffs produce a positive functional; Riesz–Markov–Kakutani gives the regular quotient measure, and monotone approximation gives Tonelli. Central-character spaces use measurable associated-line sections and a corrected density argument, then a twisted compact-abelian decomposition. Tamagawa normalization uses local gauge measures, the actual Artin factors, discriminants and AA.0’s convergent product.

For nonnormal H, use H\G and right Haar; a normal-extension measure theorem does not apply. A measurable associated line need not be a locally trivial topological line bundle. An integral local basis has Jacobian one; global discriminants enter only after multiplying all basis-sensitive local factors.

### F-rational characters

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters` · definition.

X*_F(G) is the group of homomorphisms G → G_m defined over F, realized as the group-like elements χ ∈ H (Δχ = χ ⊗ χ, ε(χ) = 1) under multiplication; χ acts on points by x ↦ x(χ) ∈ R^×. Equivalently, X*_F(G) is the Galois-fixed subgroup of the geometric character group X*(G_{F̄}).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Group-like elements are units (inverse S(χ)) and form a group.
2. A homomorphism G → G_m is a Hopf map F[T,T⁻¹] → H, determined by the image of T, which is group-like.
3. Galois descent: an F̄-character fixed by Gal(F̄/F) is defined over F.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/log-height`: the coordinates of H_G
- `AA.2/norm-one-subgroup`: the kernels defining G(𝔸)^1
- `AA.3/finite-volume`: finite volume of G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 for connected G
- `AA.2/convergence-factors`: the Galois module X*(G_{F̄}) defining convergence factors

**API.**

- `RationalCharacter` (constructor): RationalCharacter H := GroupLike F H, a commutative group.
- `RationalCharacter.apply` (data): For χ and a point x : H →ₐ[F] R, χ x := x χ ∈ Rˣ.
- `RationalCharacter.apply_mul` (simp): χ (x * y) = χ x * χ y for the convolution product.
- `RationalCharacter.equivHom` (equivalence): RationalCharacter H ≃* (Hopf maps F[T,T⁻¹] → H).
- `RationalCharacter.toGeometric_injective` (compatibility): Base change to an algebraic closure injects rational characters into Tau Ceti’s geometric character group.
- `RationalCharacter.free` (structure): For finite-type smooth geometrically reduced and geometrically connected G, its rational character group is free abelian of finite rank.
- `RationalCharacter.toGeometric_range` (characterisation): Over a number field F, the image of rational characters in the geometric character group is exactly the subgroup fixed by Field.absoluteGaloisGroup F.

**Unit tests.**

- `RationalCharacter.gln_det` (computation): For n≥1, X*_F(GL_n)≃ℤ and the actual generic determinant is sent to 1, so every character is its unique integral power.
- `RationalCharacter.sln_trivial` (degenerate): For SL_n, RationalCharacter is trivial.
- `RationalCharacter.res_norm` (non-example): For E = ℚ(i), the rational characters of Res_{E/ℚ} G_m have rank 1, not the rank 2 of the geometric character group.

**Acceptance checks.**

- X*_F(GL_n) = ℤ·det.
- X*_F(SL_n) = 0.
- For E/F quadratic, X*_F(Res_{E/F} G_m) = ℤ·N_{E/F} while X*(Res G_m ⊗ F̄) = ℤ².

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:GroupLike`, `mathlib:GroupLike.instCommGroup`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.geometricCharacterGroup`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.instGeometricCharacterGroupGaloisAction`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GeneralLinear.determinantGroupLike`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GeneralLinear.pointsMulEquiv_determinantPoints`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. Rational characters; over F in general.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.3, p. 7. The same group.

### Rational characters form a lattice

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters-free` · lemma.

For geometrically connected G, X*_F(G) is a free abelian group of finite rank. For connected reductive G, restriction of F-rational characters to the identity component of the centre is injective with finite cokernel (Borel 5.9).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated; G geometrically connected; G smooth and geometrically reduced (automatic for reduced finite-type characteristic-zero algebraic groups through the requested bridge).

**Construction or proof.**

1. X*(G_{F̄}) is torsion free (Tau Ceti isMulTorsionFree_geometricCharacterGroup) and finitely generated, since characters factor through the torus G/[G,G]·R_u(G) over F̄.
2. A subgroup of a finitely generated free abelian group is free of finite rank.
3. For reductive G, restriction to the radical (connected centre) has finite kernel and cokernel because G is isogenous to Z° × G^der and G^der has no characters (Borel 5.9).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n, restriction of det to scalars is z ↦ zⁿ, with cokernel ℤ/n.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.isMulTorsionFree_geometricCharacterGroup`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`, `tauceti:TauCeti.geometricallyReducedCommHopfAlgProperty`, `tauceti:TauCeti.geometricallyConnectedCommHopfAlgProperty`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Lemma 5.9, p. 22. Finite cokernel of restriction of characters.
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §5, p. 24. Restriction to the split centre.

### The real vector space a_G

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/real-character-space` · definition.

a_G = Hom_ℤ(X*_F(G), ℝ), a finite-dimensional real vector space, with dual a_G^* = X*_F(G) ⊗_ℤ ℝ and complexification a_{G,ℂ}^* = X*_F(G) ⊗ ℂ. A homomorphism G → G′ induces a linear map a_G → a_{G′}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated; G geometrically connected.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define as the dual of the lattice of rational-characters-free; functoriality by pulling back characters.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/log-height`: the target of H_G
- `AA.3/H-P`: a_P = a_{M_P} for parabolics
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: exponents of constant terms in a_{P,ℂ}^*

**API.**

- `RealCharacterSpace` (constructor): RealCharacterSpace H = Hom_ℤ(Additive X*_F(G), ℝ), with scalar multiplication on the codomain and the finite-dimensional topology from rational-characters-free.
- `RealCharacterSpace.pairing` (data): The pairing a_G × X*_F(G) → ℝ.
- `RealCharacterSpace.finrank` (characterisation): finrank ℝ a_G = rank of X*_F(G).
- `RealCharacterSpace.map` (functoriality): A coordinate Hopf map H′→H gives the linear map a_G→a_{G′} by precomposition with character pullback.
- `RealCharacterSpace.map_id` (functoriality): The map induced by the identity coordinate Hopf map is the identity on a_G.
- `RealCharacterSpace.map_comp` (functoriality): For coordinate Hopf maps φ:H′→H and ψ:H″→H′, map(φ∘ψ)=map(ψ)∘map(φ).

**Unit tests.**

- `RealCharacterSpace.gln_finrank` (computation): For GL_n with n≥1, its rational character lattice is ℤ·det and a_G has real dimension 1.
- `RealCharacterSpace.sln_zero` (degenerate): a_{SL_n} = 0.
- `RealCharacterSpace.res_gm_rank` (non-example): For E = ℚ(i) and G = Res_{E/ℚ} G_m, finrank a_G = 1, not 2.

**Acceptance checks.**

- a_{GL_n} ≅ ℝ via ⟨H, det⟩.
- a_{SL_n} = 0.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters-free`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. Exactly this space.

### The Harish-Chandra map H_G

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height` · definition.

H_G : G(𝔸_F) → a_G is the continuous homomorphism with ⟨H_G(x), χ⟩ = log ‖χ(x)‖ for χ ∈ X*_F(G), where χ(x) = χ_𝔸(x) ∈ 𝔸_F^× and ‖·‖ is the idele norm ∏_v |·|_v.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated; G geometrically connected.

**Planet:** Harish-Chandra map H_G.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Each χ gives a continuous homomorphism χ_𝔸 : G(𝔸) → 𝔸^× (AA.1/adelic-map and gm-adelic); compose with log ‖·‖ (GlobalNumberFields layer 6).
2. Additivity in χ makes x ↦ (χ ↦ log ‖χ(x)‖) an element of a_G.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/norm-one-subgroup`: G(𝔸)^1 = ker H_G
- `AA.3/H-P`: H_P(nmk) = H_{M_P}(m)
- `AA.3/siegel-set-adelic`: the chamber condition on H_{P_0}(a)
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: twists π_λ(x) = π(x)e^{λ(H_G(x))}
- `AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.8/cubic-adelic-zeta`: the GL_2 determinant height in adelic zeta integrals

**API.**

- `AdelicPoints.logHeight` (constructor): logHeight : AdelicPoints H →* Multiplicative a_G (an additive map to a_G).
- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_apply` (simp): ⟪logHeight x, χ⟫ = Real.log ‖χ x‖.
- `AdelicPoints.continuous_logHeight` (instance): logHeight is continuous.
- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_diagonal` (simp): logHeight (diagonal g) = 0 (product formula).
- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_map` (functoriality): logHeight ∘ map φ = a(φ) ∘ logHeight.
- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_compact` (characterisation): logHeight vanishes on every compact subgroup.

**Unit tests.**

- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_gm` (compatibility): For G = G_m, logHeight is log of the idele norm.
- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_sln` (degenerate): For SL_n, logHeight is identically zero.
- `AdelicPoints.logHeight_not_infinite_only` (non-example): logHeight is not computed from the archimedean component alone: for F = ℚ, the ideles 1 and (p at the place p, 1 elsewhere) have the same archimedean component but norms 1 and p⁻¹.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = GL_n, ⟨H_G(x), det⟩ = log |det x|.
- H_G is trivial on every compact subgroup, in particular on G(𝒪̂) for a model.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/real-character-space`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.normalizedAbsValue`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. Exactly H_G.

### H_G vanishes on rational points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height-rational` · lemma.

For g ∈ G(F), H_G(g) = 0.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. χ(g) ∈ F^× and ‖a‖ = 1 for a ∈ F^× by the product formula (Tau Ceti finprod_normalizedAbsValue_eq_one, Mathlib prod_abs_eq_one).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2(ℚ), |det γ|_𝔸 = 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.finprod_normalizedAbsValue_eq_one`, `mathlib:NumberField.prod_abs_eq_one`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 17. Vanishing of H_G on rational points.

### The norm-one subgroup G(𝔸)^1

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/norm-one-subgroup` · definition.

G(𝔸_F)^1 = ker H_G = ⋂_{χ ∈ X*_F(G)} ker ‖χ‖, a closed normal subgroup of G(𝔸_F) containing G(F), every compact subgroup and the commutator subgroup.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated; G geometrically connected.

**Planet:** Norm-one subgroup G(A)^1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Kernel of a continuous homomorphism to an abelian Hausdorff group.
2. Containment of G(F): log-height-rational; of compact subgroups: their image in ℝ is a compact subgroup, hence 0.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/finite-volume`: G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 has finite volume
- `AA.3/compactness-criterion`: compactness of G(F)\G(𝔸)^1
- `AA.2/tamagawa-number`: τ(G) = vol(G(F)\G(𝔸)^1)
- `AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient`: GL_1(𝔸)^1 = norm-one ideles

**API.**

- `AdelicPoints.normOne` (constructor): normOne H : Subgroup (AdelicPoints H) := logHeight.ker.
- `AdelicPoints.isClosed_normOne` (instance): normOne is closed.
- `AdelicPoints.normOne_normal` (instance): normOne is normal.
- `AdelicPoints.diagonal_mem_normOne` (simp): diagonal g ∈ normOne.
- `AdelicPoints.mem_normOne_iff` (characterisation): x ∈ normOne iff ‖χ x‖ = 1 for every rational character χ.
- `AdelicPoints.normOne_eq_top_of_no_characters` (example): If X*_F(G) = 0 then normOne = ⊤.

**Unit tests.**

- `AdelicPoints.normOne_gm` (compatibility): For G_m, normOne is the norm-one idele subgroup of GlobalNumberFields layer 6.
- `AdelicPoints.normOne_sl2` (degenerate): For SL_2, normOne = ⊤.
- `AdelicPoints.normOne_not_finite_part` (non-example): normOne is not G(F_∞)^1 × G(𝔸_f): for GL_1(𝔸_ℚ) the idele (p_∞ = p, p_p = p, 1 elsewhere) has norm 1 but its archimedean component has |p|_∞ ≠ 1.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_n(𝔸)^1 = {x : |det x| = 1}.
- For SL_n, SL_n(𝔸)^1 = SL_n(𝔸).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height-rational`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. Definition of G(𝔸)^1.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.8, p. 22. Borel's ¹G_A.

### The split component A_G

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre` · definition.

Let G₁ = Res_{F/ℚ} G. A_G is the largest ℚ-split torus in the centre of G₁, and A_G(ℝ)^0 ⊂ G₁(ℝ) = G(F ⊗ ℝ) = G(F_∞) ⊂ G(𝔸_F) the identity component of its real points, isomorphic to (ℝ_{>0})^k with k = rank X*_F(G).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The centre of G₁ is Res Z; its maximal ℚ-split subtorus exists and is unique.
2. X*_ℚ(G₁) ≅ X*_F(G) (Borel 1.5, Proposition), so dim A_G = rank X*_F(G) by rational-characters-free.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`: G(𝔸) = G(𝔸)^1 × A_G(ℝ)^0
- `AA.3/siegel-set-adelic`: A_0(ℝ)^0 in Siegel sets
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: the quotient G(F)A_G(ℝ)^0\G(𝔸)
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0`: the split-centre quotient in locally symmetric spaces

**API.**

- `SplitComponent` (constructor): The subgroup A_G(ℝ)^0 of AdelicPoints H (supported at the archimedean places).
- `SplitComponent.logHeight_equiv` (equivalence): logHeight restricts to an isomorphism of topological groups A_G(ℝ)^0 ≃ a_G.
- `SplitComponent.central` (characterisation): A_G(ℝ)^0 is central in G(𝔸).
- `SplitComponent.inter_normOne` (simp): A_G(ℝ)^0 ∩ G(𝔸)^1 = {1}.

**Unit tests.**

- `SplitComponent.gln_scalars` (computation): For GL_n, a_G is one-dimensional and SplitComponent (the positive real scalars at ∞ for F = ℚ) is homeomorphic to ℝ.
- `SplitComponent.semisimple_trivial` (degenerate): For semisimple G, SplitComponent is trivial.
- `SplitComponent.gm_number_field` (non-example): For G_m over a number field F, a_G and SplitComponent are one-dimensional, whereas (F ⊗ ℝ)^×_{>0} has dimension r₁ + r₂: for F real quadratic the anti-diagonal direction is not split over ℚ.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n over ℚ, A_G is the scalar G_m and A_G(ℝ)^0 = ℝ_{>0}·I.
- For G_m over F ≠ ℚ, A_G(ℝ)^0 = ℝ_{>0} embedded diagonally in (F ⊗ ℝ)^×, not the whole (F ⊗ ℝ)^×_{>0}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters-free`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/center-adelic`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, pp. 15–16. Over ℚ; over F via restriction of scalars.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.5, Proposition, p. 8. Characters of the restriction of scalars.

### H_G on the split centre

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height-split-centre-iso` · lemma.

The restriction of H_G to A_G(ℝ)^0 is an isomorphism of topological groups onto a_G; in particular H_G is surjective.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Restriction X*_F(G) ≅ X*_ℚ(G₁) → X*_ℚ(A_G) is injective with finite cokernel (rational-characters-free, Arthur (5.1)).
2. On A_G(ℝ)^0 ≅ (ℝ_{>0})^k, H_G is log of the restricted characters, a linear isomorphism after tensoring with ℝ.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n over ℚ, H_G(r·I) = n log r.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters-free`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), (5.1), p. 24. The isomorphism used for P = G.

### G(𝔸) = G(𝔸)^1 × A_G(ℝ)^0

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition` · theorem.

Multiplication G(𝔸_F)^1 × A_G(ℝ)^0 → G(𝔸_F) is an isomorphism of topological groups.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Planet:** Split-centre decomposition.

**Construction or proof.**

1. A_G(ℝ)^0 is central and closed; injectivity from SplitComponent.inter_normOne; surjectivity: for x choose a ∈ A_G(ℝ)^0 with H_G(a) = H_G(x) (log-height-split-centre-iso), then x a⁻¹ ∈ G(𝔸)^1.
2. Continuity of the inverse x ↦ (x a(x)⁻¹, a(x)) with a(x) = (H_G|_{A_G})⁻¹(H_G(x)).

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_n(𝔸) = GL_n(𝔸)^1 × ℝ_{>0}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height-split-centre-iso`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/norm-one-subgroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. Exactly this decomposition.

### Two normalizations of the quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison` · theorem.

The inclusion G(𝔸_F)^1 → G(𝔸_F) induces a homeomorphism G(F)\G(𝔸_F)^1 ≃ G(F)\G(𝔸_F)/A_G(ℝ)^0, equivariant for G(𝔸_F)^1 acting on the right; it is a theorem, not a definitional identity, and it fails if A_G(ℝ)^0 is replaced by a larger central subgroup such as Z(F_∞)^0.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply split-centre-decomposition; G(F) ⊂ G(𝔸)^1 commutes with the central factor.
2. For G_m over a real quadratic field, the anti-diagonal subgroup {(e^t,e^(−t))} lies in G(𝔸)^1 and survives modulo F^×: its intersection with a diagonal rational idele supported trivially at finite places is {1}. It is killed by quotienting by all Z(F∞)^0. A finite narrow class-group statement additionally requires division by the finite integral level.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_1 over ℚ: ℚ^×\𝔸^1 ≃ ℚ^×\𝔸^×/ℝ_{>0} ≃ ℤ̂^×.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §6, p. 30. Used to pass between the two quotients.

### Modulus character of a parabolic

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/modulus-character` · definition.

For an F-parabolic P = M_P N_P of G (or any F-group with a normal unipotent F-subgroup N), δ_P : P(𝔸_F) → ℝ_{>0} is δ_P(p) = ‖det(Ad(p) | Lie N_P)‖, the idele norm of the determinant of the adjoint action on Lie N_P ⊗ 𝔸_F. It factors as ∏_v δ_{P,v}, is trivial on N_P(𝔸) and P(F), and δ_P(p) = e^{⟨2ρ_P, H_P(p)⟩}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; P an F-parabolic with unipotent radical N_P (Tau Ceti dynamic parabolic P(λ) for an F-cocharacter λ).

**Construction or proof.**

1. det ∘ Ad|_{Lie N} is an F-rational character of P; compose with the idele norm (log-height).
2. Trivial on P(F) by the product formula; trivial on N_P since unipotent elements act unipotently.
3. The formula with ρ_P: decompose Lie N_P into A_P-root spaces (Arthur §5).

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/modular-function-parabolic`: the modular function of P(𝔸)
- `AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`: the Iwasawa integration formula ∫_G f = ∫_K ∫_{P} f(pk) δ_P(p)^{-1} d_ℓp dk
- `AutomorphicSpectralTheory:AS.1`: normalized induction from P(𝔸)
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: constant terms along P

**API.**

- `Parabolic.modulus` (constructor): Parabolic.modulus P : P(𝔸) →* ℝ≥0, p ↦ ‖det (Ad p | Lie N_P)‖.
- `Parabolic.modulus_apply_local` (simp): modulus p = ∏ᶠ v, |det(Ad p_v | Lie N_P ⊗ F_v)|_v.
- `Parabolic.modulus_rational` (simp): modulus (diagonal p) = 1 for p ∈ P(F).
- `Parabolic.modulus_unipotent` (simp): modulus n = 1 for n ∈ N_P(𝔸).
- `Parabolic.modulus_eq_exp_rho` (characterisation): modulus p = exp ⟨2ρ_P, H_P p⟩.

**Unit tests.**

- `Parabolic.modulus_borel_gl2` (computation): For the Borel of GL_2, modulus (diag(a,d) * n) = ‖a/d‖.
- `Parabolic.modulus_top` (degenerate): For P = G, modulus = 1.
- `Parabolic.modulus_not_det` (non-example): For the Borel of GL_2, modulus ≠ ‖det‖: at diag(a,1) with ‖a‖ = 2 both equal 2, but at diag(1,d) with ‖d‖ = 2, modulus = 1/2 while ‖det‖ = 2.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the upper triangular Borel B ⊂ GL_2, δ_B(diag(a,d)n) = |a/d|.
- For P = G, δ_G = 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.parabolic`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.unipotent`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.leviDecompositionMulEquiv`, `tauceti:Derivation.adjointPointRepresentation`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §5, p. 25. δ_P = e^{2ρ_P(H_P)} and non-unimodularity.

### Modular function of P(𝔸)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/modular-function-parabolic` · theorem.

For a parabolic P of a connected reductive G over a number field F, Mathlib's modular character (map (·p) μ_l = Δ(p)μ_l) on P(𝔸_F) is δ_P. With mutually inversion-normalized left and right Haar measures, dμ_l(p)=δ_P(p)⁻¹dμ_r(p). For a proper parabolic δ_P is nontrivial; for P=G it is 1.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. P(𝔸) = M_P(𝔸) ⋉ N_P(𝔸) (Tau Ceti Levi decomposition on points, AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic); M_P(𝔸) and N_P(𝔸) are unimodular (AA.1/unimodular-reductive; N_P(𝔸) is unimodular as a restricted product of unipotent groups).
2. For a semidirect product M ⋉ N, right translation by m scales the Haar measure of N by the modulus of conjugation, which on N(F_v) is |det Ad(m)|_{Lie N}|_v.
3. Multiply over places.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the Borel B ⊂ GL_2 over ℚ_p: map (· * diag(a,d)) μ = |a/d|_p μ.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/modulus-character`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/unimodular-reductive`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.leviDecompositionMulEquiv`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.modularCharacter`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §5, p. 25. The modular function of P(𝔸).

### Invariant measure on a coset space

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure` · construction.

Let G be a second countable locally compact group, H ≤ G a closed subgroup with Δ_G|_H = Δ_H, and dg, dh right Haar measures. There is a unique G-invariant Radon measure dġ on the right coset space H\G with ∫_G f(g) dg = ∫_{H\G} ∫_H f(hg) dh dġ for every f ∈ C_c(G).

**Hypotheses.** G second countable locally compact Hausdorff group; H closed subgroup; Δ_G restricted to H equals Δ_H; dg and dh are right Haar measures; invariance means the right G-action on H\G.

**Planet:** Weil quotient integration formula.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply the positive-functional Riesz theorem on the canonical locally compact Hausdorff quotient. Regularity and finiteness on compact sets follow from the construction.
2. The averaging lift proves the stated C_c identity and uniqueness. Invariance follows by comparing compactly supported integrals after right translation.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/automorphic-quotient-measure`: the measure on G(F)\G(𝔸)^1
- `EndoscopicTransferAndUnitaryTraceComparison:ET.1`: measures on centralizer quotients G_γ(𝔸)\G(𝔸) in orbital integrals
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.4/siegel-mean-value`: quotient integration on SL_n(ℝ)/SL_n(ℤ)

**API.**

- `QuotientMeasure.measure` (constructor): The measure on H\G given dg, dh and the modular condition.
- `QuotientMeasure.integral_eq` (characterisation): ∫_G f dg = ∫_{H\G} ∫_H f(hg) dh dġ for f ∈ C_c(G).
- `QuotientMeasure.invariant` (instance): The measure is invariant under the right action of G.
- `QuotientMeasure.unique` (extensionality): Any G-invariant Radon measure on H\G is a scalar multiple.
- `QuotientMeasure.smul_left` (relation): Replacing dh by c • dh replaces dġ by c⁻¹ • dġ.

**Unit tests.**

- `QuotientMeasure.trivial_subgroup` (degenerate): For H=⊥ with its normalized counting measure, the quotient measure transported to G equals dg.
- `QuotientMeasure.z_in_r` (computation): For G = ℝ, H = ℤ with counting measure, ℤ\ℝ has volume 1.
- `QuotientMeasure.borel_no_invariant` (non-example): For G=SL₂(ℝ) and H its upper triangular Borel, Δ_G|H≠Δ_H; the canonical homogeneous quotient ℙ¹(ℝ) has no nonzero invariant Radon measure.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For normal H, compare by inversion with the left-Haar extension construction IsSES.inducedMeasure; that declaration constructs a measure on the middle group from subgroup and quotient measures, and does not itself construct the general nonnormal quotient measure.
- For H = G(F) discrete it is the measure of discrete-quotient-fundamental-domain.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-functional-well-defined`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`, `mathlib:RealRMK.integral_rieszMeasure`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.ext_of_integral_eq_on_compactlySupported`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 7. The discrete case of the invariant quotient measure.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.6, p. 21. Invariant measures on adelic coset spaces.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix B.1, Lemma B.1.3 and Corollary B.1.7, pp. 344–346 and 349–350. The stated general homogeneous-space input and its proof supply this node after inversion from the manuscript’s left-Haar G/H convention to right-Haar H\G.

### Weil's formula for integrable functions

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-integral-integrable` · theorem.

In the setting of quotient-measure, for f ∈ L¹(G), the function h ↦ f(hg) is integrable on H for almost every Hg, the function Hg ↦ ∫_H f(hg) dh is integrable on H\G, and ∫_G f dg = ∫_{H\G} ∫_H f(hg) dh dġ.

**Hypotheses.** as in quotient-measure.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply quotient-tonelli to |f|. Finite integral implies finite fibre integrals almost everywhere.
2. Split real and imaginary parts into positive and negative parts to obtain integrability and the Bochner integral equality. Retain the required a.e. strong measurability when transporting to the pinned unfolding theorem.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For indicator functions of compact sets the formula computes vol(K) through the fibres.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-tonelli`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 9. The Fubini step for quotient measures.

### Quotient measures in stages

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure-transitivity` · lemma.

For closed subgroups H₁ ≤ H₂ ≤ G satisfying the modular conditions, the quotient measure on H₁\G is the product of those on H₂\G and H₁\H₂: ∫_{H₁\G} f = ∫_{H₂\G} ∫_{H₁\H₂} f(hg) dh dg.

**Hypotheses.** G, H₂, H₁ as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply quotient-measure twice and compare on C_c(G) using uniqueness.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For Γ ⊂ G(𝔸)^1 ⊂ G(𝔸): vol(Γ\G(𝔸))-type integrals split as in quotient-norm-one-comparison.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-integral-integrable`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 9. Exactly this lemma.

### Fundamental domains for rational points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/discrete-quotient-fundamental-domain` · lemma.

G(F) acting on G(𝔸_F) (or G(𝔸_F)^1) by left translation admits a Borel fundamental domain; the quotient measure on G(F)\G(𝔸_F) equals the pushforward of the restriction of Haar measure to any measurable fundamental domain, and does not depend on the choice.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G(F) is a countable discrete subgroup (AA.1/rational-points-discrete; F countable) of the second countable locally compact G(𝔸) (AA.1/adelic-points-locally-compact); a Borel fundamental domain exists by choosing a countable cover by open sets injecting into the quotient.
2. Independence: MeasureTheory.IsFundamentalDomain.measure_eq; identification with quotient-measure via QuotientMeasureEqMeasurePreimage and the unfolding lemma.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For ℤ ⊂ ℝ any interval [a, a+1) is a fundamental domain of measure 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-locally-compact`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.IsFundamentalDomain.quotientMeasure_eq`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.QuotientMeasureEqMeasurePreimage`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/left-right-quotient-inversion`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fundamental-domain-exists`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Definition 4.1, p. 17. Fundamental sets; here exact Borel fundamental domains.

### The measure on the automorphic quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/automorphic-quotient-measure` · construction.

For connected reductive G with a Haar measure dx on G(𝔸_F) and Lebesgue measure on a_G (normalized by the lattice dual to X*_F(G)), the measure on [G]^1 = G(F)\G(𝔸_F)^1 is the quotient (quotient-measure) of the measure on G(𝔸)^1 induced via split-centre-decomposition, and the measure on G(F)A_G(ℝ)^0\G(𝔸_F) is its transport by quotient-norm-one-comparison.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Transport dx along G(𝔸) ≅ G(𝔸)^1 × A_G(ℝ)^0 ≅ G(𝔸)^1 × a_G and divide by Lebesgue measure.
2. Take the quotient by the counting measure on G(F) (unimodularity: AA.1/unimodular-reductive and Borel 5.8).

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/finite-volume`: finiteness of vol([G]^1)
- `AA.2/central-character-l2`: the Hilbert space carrier
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: one quotient-measure and Hilbert carrier (RS-04 link AA.2 → AF.3)
- `BorelRegulators:R.1`: one quotient/Tamagawa measure supplier
- `Polylogarithms:P.2`: the generic measure for Bloch–Borel volume identities
- `AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.8/cubic-adelic-zeta`: GL_2 adelic quotient measure for the binary cubic zeta integral

**API.**

- `AutomorphicQuotient.measure` (constructor): The measure on G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 attached to dx.
- `AutomorphicQuotient.measure_split` (equivalence): Its transport to G(F)A_G(ℝ)^0\G(𝔸).
- `AutomorphicQuotient.invariant` (instance): Right G(𝔸)^1-invariance.
- `AutomorphicQuotient.smul_haar` (relation): Scaling dx by c scales the quotient measure by c.

**Unit tests.**

- `AutomorphicQuotient.semisimple` (degenerate): For G without rational characters (for instance semisimple G), G(𝔸)^1 = G(𝔸), so the measure lives on G(F)\G(𝔸).
- `AutomorphicQuotient.gl1_rat` (computation): For GL_1 over ℚ with ideleHaar, the quotient ℚ^×\𝔸^1 ≃ ℤ̂^× has volume 1 (ideleHaar(ℤ̂^×) = 1).
- `AutomorphicQuotient.not_full_quotient` (non-example): For GL_1, the norm-one quotient has finite volume while the split component ℝ_{>0} is not compact, so ℚ^×\𝔸^× has infinite volume.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_1 over ℚ with idele-haar, ℚ^×\𝔸^1 ≅ ℤ̂^× has volume 1 (AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient).
- Changing dx by c changes the quotient measure by c.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/discrete-quotient-fundamental-domain`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/unimodular-reductive`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.8, p. 22. The invariant measure on the norm-one quotient.

### L² space with a unitary central character

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-character-l2` · construction.

Let 𝔛 ⊂ Z(𝔸_F) be a closed subgroup such that 𝔛Z(F) is closed, and ω a continuous unitary character of 𝔛 trivial on 𝔛 ∩ Z(F). L²(G(F)\G(𝔸_F), ω) is the Hilbert space of (classes of) measurable φ on G(𝔸) with φ(γ z g) = ω(z) φ(g) for γ ∈ G(F), z ∈ 𝔛, and ∫_{𝔛G(F)\G(𝔸)} |φ|² < ∞, with right translation R a unitary representation of G(𝔸) on which 𝔛 acts by ω.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; 𝔛, ω as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. central-product-closed proves XΓ is closed from XZ(F) closed. The measurable associated-line and cocycle constructions identify equivariant functions with L² sections on XΓ\G.
2. The completeness node uses the section isometry to scalar L² and quotient invariance. The continuous equivariant-section density input supplies strong continuity of right translation.
3. The central action is multiplication by ω because X is central; the inner product is conjugate-linear in its first variable.

**Uses.**

- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: the Hilbert carrier for cusp forms with central character
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.4/howe-moore-mixing`: L² action normalization (request from GN)
- `AutomorphicSpectralTheory:AS.1`: the spectral decomposition of L²

**API.**

- `CentralCharL2` (constructor): CentralCharL2 𝔛 ω, a Hilbert space.
- `CentralCharL2.rightReg` (data): The unitary representation of G(𝔸) by right translation.
- `CentralCharL2.rightReg_central` (simp): rightReg z = ω z • id for z ∈ 𝔛.
- `CentralCharL2.inner_def` (characterisation): ⟪φ, ψ⟫ = ∫_{𝔛G(F)\G(𝔸)} conj(φ) · ψ, conjugate-linear in the first argument as in Mathlib; the integrand is quotient-invariant.
- `CentralCharL2.continuous_rightReg` (instance): rightReg is strongly continuous.

**Unit tests.**

- `CentralCharL2.trivial_X` (degenerate): For 𝔛 = 1 it is L²(G(F)\G(𝔸)).
- `CentralCharL2.gl1_dim` (computation): For GL_1 and 𝔛 = 𝔸^×, it is one-dimensional iff ω is trivial on F^×.
- `CentralCharL2.nontrivial_on_rational` (non-example): If ω is nontrivial on 𝔛 ∩ Z(F) the space is zero.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For 𝔛 = A_G(ℝ)^0 and ω = 1 it is L²(G(F)\G(𝔸)^1) (central-quotient-change).
- For G = GL_1 and 𝔛 = Z(𝔸) = G(𝔸) it is one-dimensional when ω is trivial on F^×, and zero otherwise.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-integral-integrable`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/center-adelic`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Lp`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/automorphic-quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-l2-completeness`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-product-closed`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 7. Right regular representation on L² of the quotient; here with a central character.

### Change of central quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-quotient-change` · theorem.

Let 𝔛′ ⊂ 𝔛 ⊂ Z(𝔸_F) be as in central-character-l2 with 𝔛′Z(F)\𝔛Z(F) compact, and ω′ a unitary character of 𝔛′. Then L²(G(F)\G(𝔸), ω′) is the Hilbert direct sum of the subspaces L²(G(F)\G(𝔸), ω) over the unitary characters ω of 𝔛 trivial on 𝔛 ∩ Z(F) and restricting to ω′, each being the ω-isotypic part for 𝔛. In particular L²(G(F)A_G(ℝ)^0\G(𝔸)) ≅ L²(G(F)\G(𝔸)^1) unitarily and G(𝔸)^1-equivariantly.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; 𝔛′Z(F) and 𝔛Z(F) closed; their quotient compact; ω′ continuous and trivial on 𝔛′∩Z(F).

**Construction or proof.**

1. Extend ω′ to ω₀ and use the twisted compact quotient action.
2. Apply the supplied compact abelian Peter–Weyl decomposition. The χ-isotypic summand is the space with character ω₀χ, and quotient measure transitivity fixes its isometric normalization.
3. For the split centre with trivial character, transport through quotient-norm-one-comparison.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_1 over ℚ, 𝔛′ = ℝ_{>0}, 𝔛 = 𝔸^×: L²(ℚ^×ℝ_{>0}\𝔸^×) = ⊕ over Dirichlet characters of one-dimensional spaces.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-character-l2`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure-transitivity`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-character-extension-twist`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CompactGroups#layer-5-the-peter-weyl-theorem`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. Passing between G(𝔸) and G(𝔸)^1, the representation-theoretic shadow of the change of central quotient.

### Invariant top-degree forms

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/invariant-top-form` · definition.

For a smooth affine algebraic group G of dimension d over a field k with coordinate Hopf algebra H, the space of left-invariant top-degree differential forms is ω_G = ∧^d_k (𝔪_ε/𝔪_ε²), the top exterior power of the cotangent space at the identity; it is a one-dimensional k-vector space, and a nonzero ω ∈ ω_G is a gauge form. Right translation by g acts on ω_G by det(Ad(g))⁻¹.

**Hypotheses.** k a field; G smooth affine of dimension d.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The cotangent space at the identity is Tau Ceti's Bialgebra.CotangentSpace, of dimension d for smooth G.
2. Use the pinned adjointAction, the scalar tensor-unit equivalence and bidual evaluation to form the transpose of Ad(g⁻¹) on identity cotangents. Its top exterior map multiplies by det(Ad(g))⁻¹. Identifying this algebraic line action with right pullback of invariant differential-form sections is a separate invariant-section comparison.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/local-form-measure`: the local Haar measures |ω|_v
- `AA.2/tamagawa-measure`: the global gauge form
- `BorelRegulators:R.6/local-sl-volume`: canonical differential-form local measures (request from R.6)

**API.**

- `GaugeForm` (constructor): GaugeForm is the top exterior-power line of the cotangent module (ker ε)/(ker ε)² at the identity; a gauge form is a nonzero vector in it.
- `GaugeForm.finrank_eq_one` (characterisation): For smooth G, finrank k (GaugeForm H) = 1.
- `GaugeForm.baseChange` (functoriality): GaugeForm commutes with base change k → k′.
- `GaugeForm.rightTranslate` (relation): Right pullback by g acts on the left-invariant top-form line by det(Ad(g))⁻¹.

**Unit tests.**

- `GaugeForm.gm` (computation): For G_m the cotangent space at 1 is one-dimensional, spanned by the class of T − 1, so GaugeForm is spanned by dT/T.
- `GaugeForm.trivial_group` (degenerate): For the trivial group (d = 0), GaugeForm = k.
- `GaugeForm.borel_not_biinvariant` (non-example): For the upper triangular Borel of GL₂, right pullback by diag(a,d) multiplies a left-invariant gauge form by (a/d)⁻¹. For a/d≠1 it is not invariant.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_m, ω_G = k·dT/T.
- For G_a, ω_G = k·dT.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.Bialgebra.CotangentSpace`, `tauceti:Derivation.adjointPointRepresentation`, `mathlib:Ideal.Cotangent`, `mathlib:Bialgebra.counitAlgHom`, `mathlib:exteriorPower.ιMulti`, `tauceti:Derivation.adjointAction`, `mathlib:Module.evalEquiv`, `mathlib:TensorProduct.lid`, `mathlib:exteriorPower.map`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. Exactly this space.

### The Haar measure |ω|_v of a gauge form

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure` · construction.

For a smooth affine group G over a local field F_v of characteristic 0 with gauge form ω and the standard Haar measure on F_v (𝒪_v of volume 1; Lebesgue on ℝ; twice Lebesgue on ℂ), |ω|_v is the left Haar measure on G(F_v) given in any F_v-analytic chart φ : U → G(F_v), U ⊂ F_v^d open, by |f(x)|_v dx_1 ⋯ dx_d where φ^*ω = f dx_1 ∧ ⋯ ∧ dx_d.

**Hypotheses.** F_v a local field of characteristic 0; G smooth affine over F_v; ω a gauge form.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G(F_v) is an F_v-analytic manifold (inverse function theorem over the complete field F_v) and ω an invariant analytic top form.
2. Charts glue because of the change-of-variables formula |det Dψ|_v for F_v-analytic diffeomorphisms ψ (recorded gap in the nonarchimedean case).
3. Left invariance of ω gives a left Haar measure.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/tamagawa-measure`: the local factors
- `AA.2/weil-volume-formula`: volumes of integral points
- `BorelRegulators:R.6/local-sl-volume`: local SL volumes
- `AA.0/tamagawa-convergence-failure`: the G_m form measures

**API.**

- `GaugeForm.localMeasure` (constructor): The Haar measure |ω|_v on G(F_v).
- `GaugeForm.localMeasure_isHaar` (instance): |ω|_v is a left Haar measure.
- `GaugeForm.localMeasure_smul` (relation): |c ω|_v = |c|_v • |ω|_v.
- `GaugeForm.localMeasure_rightTranslate` (relation): Measure.map (· * g) |ω|_v = |det Ad(g)|_v • |ω|_v. In contrast R_g^*ω = det Ad(g)⁻¹ • ω. This agrees with the pinned Mathlib convention map R_g μ = modularCharacter(g) • μ.

**Unit tests.**

- `GaugeForm.localMeasure_ga` (compatibility): For G_a with coordinate T, the image of localMeasure dT under x ↦ x(T) is the standard Haar measure of F_v (𝒪_v of volume 1).
- `GaugeForm.localMeasure_gm_units` (computation): For G_m at a finite place, localMeasure (dT/T) (𝒪_v^×) = 1 - q_v⁻¹.
- `GaugeForm.localMeasure_not_normalized` (non-example): localMeasure (dT/T) on ℚ_p^× is not the normalized idele measure of AA.0/idele-haar: they differ by the factor 1 − p⁻¹ ≠ 1.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_a and ω = dT, |ω|_v is the standard measure.
- For G_m and ω = dT/T at a finite place, |ω|_v(𝒪_v^×) = 1 - q_v⁻¹.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/invariant-top-form`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. The local measures of a gauge form.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. Local forms.

### Weil's volume formula for integral points

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/weil-volume-formula` · theorem.

Let 𝓗 be a smooth affine group scheme of relative dimension d over 𝒪_v with residue field k_v of order q_v, and ω a gauge form of the generic fibre that extends to a generator of the invariant top forms of 𝓗. Then |ω|_v(𝓗(𝒪_v)) = #𝓗(k_v) · q_v^{-d}.

**Hypotheses.** 𝓗 smooth affine over 𝒪_v; ω extends to a generator over 𝒪_v.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Reduction 𝓗(𝒪_v) → 𝓗(k_v) is surjective by Hensel's lemma for smooth schemes.
2. Each fibre is a coset of the first congruence subgroup, which in coordinates adapted to ω is the polydisc (π_v𝒪_v)^d of measure q_v^{-d}, with |f|_v = 1 there.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n over ℤ_p: vol(GL_n(ℤ_p)) = #GL_n(𝔽_p) p^{-n²} = ∏_{i=1}^n (1 - p^{-i}).
- For G_m: 1 - p⁻¹.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-points-level`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §3, p. 25. The volumes of integral points entering the Tamagawa product.

### Convergence factors from the character module

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors` · construction.

Let X = X*(G_{F̄}) ⊗ ℂ with its continuous finite-image Galois action. For a finite place v, L_v(X, s) = det(1 - q_v^{-s} Frob_v | X^{I_v})⁻¹, and the convergence factors are λ_v = L_v(X, 1) at finite v and λ_v = 1 at infinite v. The partial Euler product L^S(X, s) = ∏_{v∉S} L_v(X, s) converges for Re s > 1, and ρ_G = lim_{s→1⁺} (s - 1)^r L^S(X, s) · ∏_{v∈S, v finite} L_v(X, s) with r = rank X*_F(G), whenever this limit exists and is nonzero.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated; G connected.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The Galois action on the character lattice factors through a finite quotient Gal(E/F), E a splitting field (Tau Ceti geometricCharacterGroup with its continuous Galois action).
2. Frobenius at unramified v (Chebotarev roadmap) gives the local factor.
3. When the action is trivial (G with split characters, e.g. GL_n or split tori), L(X, s) = ζ_F(s)^r and ρ_G = (Res_{s=1} ζ_F)^r (Mathlib dedekindZeta_residue).

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/tamagawa-measure`: the factors λ_v and the normalizing constant ρ_G
- `AA.0/tamagawa-convergence-failure`: λ_p = (1 - p⁻¹)⁻¹ for G_m

**API.**

- `Tamagawa.localFactor` (constructor): localFactor v s := det(1 - q_v^{-s} Frob_v | X^{I_v})⁻¹.
- `Tamagawa.localFactor_trivial` (simp): If Gal acts trivially on X of rank r, localFactor v s = (1 - q_v^{-s})^{-r}.
- `Tamagawa.leadingCoeff` (data): ρ_G, defined when the limit exists.
- `Tamagawa.leadingCoeff_split` (example): For split characters of rank r, ρ_G = (dedekindZeta_residue F)^r.

**Unit tests.**

- `Tamagawa.localFactor_gm` (computation): For G_m, localFactor v 1 = (1 - q_v⁻¹)⁻¹.
- `Tamagawa.localFactor_semisimple` (degenerate): For semisimple G (for instance SL_n), X = 0 and localFactor v s = 1.
- `Tamagawa.localFactor_res_gm` (non-example): For G = Res_{E/ℚ} G_m with E = ℚ(i), localFactor p 1 = (1 - p⁻¹)⁻¹(1 - χ₄(p)p⁻¹)⁻¹, not (1 - p⁻¹)⁻¹: the inert primes see the nontrivial character.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n, λ_v = (1 - q_v⁻¹)⁻¹ and ρ_G = Res_{s=1} ζ_F.
- For SL_n, λ_v = 1 and ρ_G = 1.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.geometricCharacterGroup`, `tauceti:TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.instGeometricCharacterGroupGaloisAction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters`, `mathlib:NumberField.dedekindZeta_residue`, `mathlib:NumberField.dedekindZeta_residue_pos`, `mathlib:NumberField.tendsto_sub_one_mul_dedekindZeta_nhdsGT`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §3, p. 25. The displayed source determinant lacks its inverse (confirmed source issue E10). The planned inverse is required by the subsequent Artin-L pole and corrected-volume statements. The finite-place definition is analogous; the number-field analytic normalization remains a gap.
- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. Why these specific factors.

### Tamagawa measure

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-measure` · construction.

For a smooth connected affine F-group of dimension d for which the Artin leading coefficient ρ_G exists and is positive and the corrected integral volumes converge absolutely, define τ_G=|d_F|⁻ᵈ/²ρ_G⁻¹ times the convergent Haar product of μ_v=λ_v|ω|_v. At finite v, λ_v=L_v(X*(G_Fbar)⊗ℂ,1), including ramified inertia invariants; at infinite v λ_v=1. Use the specified additive normalizations dx, 2dxdy and vol(O_v)=1. Connected reductive groups satisfy the separate convergence target. If the geometric character lattice is zero, λ_v=ρ_G=1, but the gauge integral masses still need the convergent-product construction.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated; G connected; ρ_G exists (convergence-factors); ρ_G is finite and strictly positive; The product of corrected integral volumes is absolutely convergent and nonzero.

**Planet:** Tamagawa measure.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose a finite exceptional set containing the bad models and all noncompact integral factors. At every good place compute a_v=λ_v|ω|_v(𝓗(O_v)) using weil-volume-formula.
2. Apply convergent-haar-product to normalize μ_v by a_v at every good place and then multiply by ∏a_v. Independence of the exceptional set and model follows from the corresponding AA.0 lemma.
3. Multiply by the fixed positive discriminant and Artin-leading-coefficient factors. No arbitrary factor parameters or finite-rescaling replacement enter the definition.

**Uses.**

- `AA.2/tamagawa-number`: τ(G)
- `BorelRegulators:R.6/norm-one-tamagawa`: canonical Tamagawa measure of SL_1(D) with |D_F|^{-dim/2} (request from R.6)
- `BorelRegulators:R.7`: measure normalization when comparing regulator conventions
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.3/adelic-mass-identity`: Siegel–Weil mass via Tamagawa measures

**API.**

- `Tamagawa.measure` (constructor): Tamagawa.measure G : Measure (AdelicPoints H).
- `Tamagawa.measure_isHaar` (instance): Tamagawa.measure is a left Haar measure.
- `Tamagawa.measure_eq_product` (characterisation): On a product of finitely many local sets and almost all 𝓗(𝒪_v) it is |d_F|^{-d/2} ρ_G⁻¹ ∏ λ_v |ω|_v(C_v).
- `Tamagawa.measure_ga` (example): For G_a it is |d_F|^{-1/2} • adeleHaar.
- `Tamagawa.measure_res` (functoriality): Compatibility with restriction of scalars (tamagawa-restriction-scalars).

**Unit tests.**

- `Tamagawa.measure_ga_selfdual` (computation): For G_a over ℚ, Tamagawa.measure (ℚ\𝔸 fundamental domain [0,1) × ℤ̂) = 1.
- `Tamagawa.measure_trivial` (degenerate): For the trivial group it is the Dirac measure of mass 1.
- `Tamagawa.measure_not_naive_product` (non-example): For G_m the naive product ∏ |dT/T|_p is not a measure on 𝔸^×: the volumes 1 − p⁻¹ have product 0 because Σ_p 1/p diverges; Tamagawa.measure uses λ_v = (1 − q_v⁻¹)⁻¹ and ρ = Res ζ_F.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_a, τ = |d_F|^{-1/2} adeleHaar.
- For G_m over ℚ, τ restricted to 𝔸^1 gives vol(ℚ^×\𝔸^1) = 1 (Tate).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/weil-volume-formula`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-rescale`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/tamagawa-convergence-failure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/convergent-haar-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/convergent-product-independence`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §3, p. 25. Function-field analogue only: equation (3.4) also contains (log q)^−r and #coker θ_H factors. This citation does not establish the number-field normalization or restriction-of-scalars formula; those require the recorded normalization gap.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.5, p. 21. Existence of the corrected product measure.
- [Julia Gordon, with an appendix by Matthew Koster, *Orbital integrals and normalizations of measures*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.02391v1), §5.1.1, equation (48), pp. 31–33; Remark 2.1, p. 3. Number-field torus normalization is read directly; the general reductive Artin/convergence extension remains an explicitly listed proof input.

### Independence of the gauge form

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-independent-of-form` · theorem.

τ_G does not depend on the gauge form ω: replacing ω by cω with c ∈ F^× multiplies each |ω|_v by |c|_v, and ∏_v |c|_v = 1.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G = Spec H an affine algebraic group over F, H finitely generated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Local scaling (GaugeForm.localMeasure_smul).
2. Product formula (Tau Ceti finprod_normalizedAbsValue_eq_one); only finitely many |c|_v differ from 1, so AA.0/restricted-haar-rescale applies.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Replacing dT by 2dT on G_a over ℚ leaves τ unchanged.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.finprod_normalizedAbsValue_eq_one`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-rescale`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. Exactly this argument.

### Absolute convergence of the corrected volumes

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence` · theorem.

For connected reductive G with an integral model 𝓗 smooth with connected reductive fibres away from S, the product ∏_{v∉S} λ_v #𝓗(k_v) q_v^{-d} converges absolutely; for semisimple G the factors are 1 + O(q_v^{-2}).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; 𝓗 reductive over 𝒪_{F,S}.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Steinberg's formula #𝓗(k_v) = q_v^{d} ∏_i (1 - ε_i q_v^{-d_i}) for connected reductive groups over finite fields, with exponents d_i ≥ 2 for the semisimple part and d_i = 1 for the torus part, whose contribution is cancelled by λ_v = L_v(X, 1) up to O(q_v^{-2}).
2. ∑_v q_v^{-2} converges.
3. For GL_n and SL_n the convergence is elementary (tamagawa-convergence-gln) and needs no gap.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2: #SL_2(𝔽_q) q⁻³ = 1 - q⁻²; ∏_p (1 - p⁻²) = ζ(2)⁻¹ converges.
- For GL_1 without the factor λ_v the product diverges (AA.0/tamagawa-convergence-failure).
