**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/weil-volume-formula`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence-gln`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-9-pinned-chevalleydemazure-group-schemes-over-ℤ`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §3, p. 25. Absolute convergence of the corrected product.

### Tamagawa number

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-number` · definition.

For connected reductive G, τ(G) = vol(G(F)\G(𝔸_F)^1) for the measure on G(𝔸)^1 induced by τ_G and the Lebesgue measure on a_G normalized by the lattice Hom(X*_F(G), ℤ) (through split-centre-decomposition and log-height-split-centre-iso), with counting measure on G(F), as an element of [0, ∞]. Its finiteness is AA.3/tamagawa-number-finite.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Planet:** Tamagawa number.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Combine tamagawa-measure, automorphic-quotient-measure and the normalization of a_G.

**Uses.**

- `BorelRegulators:R.6/norm-one-tamagawa`: τ(SL_1(D)) in Bloch's reformulation (the value is not computed here)
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.3/adelic-mass-identity`: mass formulas

**API.**

- `Tamagawa.number` (constructor): Tamagawa.number G : ℝ≥0∞.
- `Tamagawa.number_pos` (characterisation): Tamagawa.number G > 0.
- `Tamagawa.number_res` (functoriality): Tamagawa.number (Res_{E/F} G) = Tamagawa.number G.

**Unit tests.**

- `Tamagawa.number_trivial` (degenerate): For the trivial group τ = 1.
- `Tamagawa.number_gm_statement` (computation): τ(G_m) = 1 over any number field (statement; proof in AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors:AL.1 via Tate's thesis).
- `Tamagawa.number_not_full_quotient` (non-example): vol(G_m(F)\G_m(𝔸)) = ∞ because the split component ℝ_{>0} is not compact; τ uses the norm-one quotient.

**Acceptance checks.**

- τ(G_a) = 1 and τ(G_m) = 1 (Tate's thesis, AutomorphicLFunctionsAndLocalFactors).
- τ(SL_2) = 1 (Weil); computing τ for general G is outside this roadmap.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/automorphic-quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height-split-centre-iso`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 3. Exactly this definition.

### Tamagawa measures and restriction of scalars

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-restriction-scalars` · theorem.

Let E/F be a finite extension of number fields and G_E a connected reductive E-group of dimension d. The canonical topological group isomorphism Res_{E/F}G_E(𝔸_F)→G_E(𝔸_E), together with the induced character-space map and its integral-lattice Lebesgue normalization, transports Tamagawa measures and norm-one quotient measures. In particular the Tamagawa numbers agree. The extension to connected nonreductive groups requires the Levi/unipotent integration input recorded separately.

**Hypotheses.** E/F finite extensions of number fields; G_E connected reductive; The Artin leading coefficients and Haar products have the existence/convergence proved in the named inputs..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose an F-basis β of E and the coordinate determinant-line form Ω_β induced from an invariant E-form ω. The local comparison is |Ω_β|_v=j_β(v)^d∏_{w|v}|ω|_w, with j_β(v) defined by the actual Haar Jacobian, not by a bare local discriminant.
2. restriction-global-jacobian gives ∏_v j_β(v)=|d_F|^{[E:F]/2}|d_E|^{-1/2}. Its d-th power cancels |d_F|^{-d[E:F]/2} to give |d_E|^{-d/2}; an F-basis change cancels globally by the product formula.
3. artin-factor-induction identifies all local L-factors, including inertia at ramified places, and the leading coefficient and order of pole. The convergent restricted-Haar construction permits this regrouping.
4. F-rational characters of Res G_E correspond to E-rational characters of G_E; their norm evaluation and the character-lattice Lebesgue measures commute with the isomorphism. Quotient measure transitivity then identifies the norm-one rational quotient measures.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_E = G_m: τ(Res_{E/F} G_m) = 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-0-places-completions-and-the-product-formula`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-4-the-relative-discriminant`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/gauge-form-restriction-discriminant`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/artin-factor-induction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-global-jacobian`.

**Sources.**

- [Julia Gordon, with an appendix by Matthew Koster, *Orbital integrals and normalizations of measures*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.02391v1), §5.1.1, eq. (48), pp. 31–33. The number-field torus normalization supplies the checked special case; the reductive extension is derived from the basis-sensitive and Artin inputs listed here.
- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2; §3, pp. 13–14. This gives function-field compatibility as motivation; it is not used as proof of the number-field Jacobian.

### Volumes of commensurable compact open subgroups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/compact-open-volume` · lemma.

For compact open subgroups U, U′ of G(𝔸_{F,f}) (or of G(F_v)) and a left Haar measure μ, μ(U)/μ(U′) = [U : U ∩ U′]/[U′ : U ∩ U′]; in particular all volumes of compact open subgroups are positive rational multiples of one of them.

**Hypotheses.** G(𝔸_{F,f}) as in AA.1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. U ∩ U′ has finite index in both (AA.1/compact-open-product).
2. MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure applied to U ∩ U′ in U and in U′.

**Acceptance checks.**

- vol(Γ_0(N)-level K_0(N)) = vol(GL_2(ℤ̂)) / [GL_2(ℤ̂) : K_0(N)].

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-is-haar`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §1.7, p. 8. Commensurability, giving the index formula.

### Averaging over a closed subgroup is surjective on compact supports

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/bruhat-section` · lemma.

For a closed subgroup H of a locally compact group G and a right Haar measure dh on H, the map P : C_c(G) → C_c(H\G), (Pf)(Hg) = ∫_H f(hg) dh, is surjective, and every f ≥ 0 in C_c(H\G) is Pφ for some φ ≥ 0.

**Hypotheses.** G locally compact Hausdorff; H closed; dh a right Haar measure on H.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For F∈C_c(H\G), choose a cutoff β with Pβ=1 on support F. Then f(g)=β(g)F(Hg) is compactly supported and Pf=F.
2. If F≥0, use β≥0; the resulting lift is nonnegative.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For H = {1}, P is the identity of C_c(G).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fibre-average-continuous`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/compact-quotient-cutoff`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 7. The construction of the invariant measure, of which this surjectivity is the first step.

### Weil's functional is well defined under the modular condition

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-functional-well-defined` · lemma.

If Δ_G|_H = Δ_H, then for f ∈ C_c(G), Pf = 0 implies ∫_G f dg = 0; hence Pf ↦ ∫_G f dg is a well-defined positive G-invariant functional on C_c(H\G).

**Hypotheses.** G locally compact Hausdorff; H a closed subgroup; dg and dh right Haar measures; Δ_G|_H = Δ_H, using the pinned Mathlib modular-character convention.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For f with Pf=0, take β with Pβ=1 on q(support f). The exchange identity gives ∫f=∫β·Pf=0.
2. A nonnegative quotient function has a nonnegative lift, hence the induced real-linear functional is positive. Right translating its lift proves G-invariance using right Haar dg.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For B ⊂ SL_2(ℝ) the condition fails and the functional is not well defined.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/bruhat-section`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/compact-quotient-cutoff`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/right-haar-exchange`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 9. The product decomposition that the functional realizes.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix B.1, Lemma B.1.3, pp. 344–346. The stated general homogeneous-space input and its proof supply this node after inversion from the manuscript’s left-Haar G/H convention to right-Haar H\G.

### Gauge forms under restriction of scalars

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/gauge-form-restriction-discriminant` · lemma.

Choose an F-basis β of E and an E-basis of the cotangent space dual to the E-gauge ω_E. Let ω_β be the F-top form dual to the ordered F-basis β_j e_i of the restricted cotangent space. At v, transport |ω_β|_v to G_E(∏_{w|v}E_w); it equals j_{β,v}^d∏_{w|v}|ω_E|_w. Here j_{β,v} is defined by (β-coordinates)_*μ_{F_v}^{[E:F]}=j_{β,v}∏_{w|v}μ_{E_w}. It depends on β and is not an unspecified square root of a local discriminant.

**Hypotheses.** E/F finite; ω_E a gauge form.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply the scalar-coordinate change-of-variables calculation in each of the d cotangent directions.
2. Use the finite and infinite scalar Jacobian lemmas. Their global product, not each individual factor, is the absolute-discriminant ratio.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For E=Q(√5), β=(1,(1+√5)/2), the factor at p=5 is 1, while the real determinant contributes 5⁻¹/².
- Replacing β by βM rescales ω_β by det(M)⁻ᵈ; the global measure is unchanged by the product formula.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/invariant-top-form`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-0-places-completions-and-the-product-formula`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-finite-jacobian`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-infinite-jacobian`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 2. The restriction-of-scalars compatibility these factors serve.

### Inductivity of the convergence factors

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/artin-factor-induction` · lemma.

For a finite separable E/F and a finite-image complex Galois representation X over E, the induced representation satisfies L_v(Ind X,s)=∏_{w|v}L_w(X,s) at every finite v, including ramified v with inertia invariants. Consequently their global Euler products coincide for Re(s)>1 and their leading coefficients coincide whenever the stated nonzero limits at s=1 exist.

**Hypotheses.** E/F finite; X with finite image.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Decompose the induced representation into decomposition-group cosets and then take inertia invariants. Frobenius permutes the components in cycles of lengths f_w; the determinant on each cycle is det(1−q_v^{-sf_w}Frob_w|X^{I_w}).
2. Use q_w=q_v^{f_w}. Equality on Re(s)>1 and matching invariant ranks identify the leading-coefficient limits under their explicit existence hypotheses.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For X trivial of rank one, ∏_{w|v} (1 − q_w^{-s})⁻¹ is the local factor of ζ_E.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §3, p. 25. The local factors whose induction formula this is.

### Borel fundamental domains for countable discrete subgroups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fundamental-domain-exists` · lemma.

Let G be a second countable locally compact Hausdorff group and Γ ≤ G a countable discrete subgroup acting by left translation. There is a Borel set D ⊂ G meeting every orbit Γg in exactly one point.

**Hypotheses.** G second countable locally compact Hausdorff; Γ countable and discrete.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose an open neighbourhood V of 1 with VV⁻¹ ∩ Γ = {1}. Each right translate Vg injects into Γ\G: γv₁g=v₂g implies γ=v₂v₁⁻¹=1.
2. Cover G by countably many right translates Vg_n and define D = union over n of (Vg_n minus union over m<n of ΓVg_m). The Γ-saturations are Borel because Γ is countable. Every orbit is retained exactly once in its first such translate.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For ℤ ⊂ ℝ, the construction yields a Borel set such as [0, 1).

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:MeasureTheory.IsFundamentalDomain`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Definition 4.1, p. 17. Fundamental sets; here exact fundamental domains.

### Corrected volumes for GL_n and SL_n

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence-gln` · theorem.

For the standard models of GL_n and SL_n over 𝒪_F and their standard gauge forms, at every finite place v: λ_v · #GL_n(k_v) q_v^{-n²} = ∏_{i=2}^{n} (1 − q_v^{-i}) with λ_v = (1 − q_v^{-1})^{-1}, and #SL_n(k_v) q_v^{-(n²−1)} = ∏_{i=2}^{n} (1 − q_v^{-i}); both products over v converge absolutely.

**Hypotheses.** n ≥ 1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. #GL_n(𝔽_q) = ∏_{i=0}^{n−1} (q^n − q^i) = q^{n²} ∏_{i=1}^{n} (1 − q^{-i}); #SL_n(𝔽_q) = #GL_n(𝔽_q)/(q − 1).
2. Apply weil-volume-formula; Σ_v q_v^{-2} converges because Σ_v q_v^{-s} converges for s > 1 (Dedekind zeta).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2: ∏_p (1 − p⁻²) = ζ(2)⁻¹ = 6/π².

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/weil-volume-formula`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §3, p. 25. The convergence, here checked by counting for GL_n and SL_n.

### Inversion between left and right quotient conventions

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/left-right-quotient-inversion` · lemma.

For a locally compact unimodular G and discrete countable Γ, inversion sends Γg to g⁻¹Γ, giving a homeomorphism Γ\G≃G/Γ that preserves the correspondingly normalized quotient measures. It carries left-orbit unfolding to the pinned right Γ.op-orbit unfolding.

**Hypotheses.** G second countable locally compact Hausdorff and unimodular; Γ a countable discrete subgroup; normalized Haar μ is left and right invariant; Use the fundamental-domain quotient measures and the integrability/measurability hypotheses of the pinned theorem.

**Construction or proof.**

1. If g′=γg then (g′)⁻¹=g⁻¹γ⁻¹, so inversion descends; its inverse is inversion. Quotient continuity follows from continuity of inversion.
2. Inversion preserves a bi-invariant Haar measure; it exchanges left and right fundamental domains. Transport the quotient pushforwards and the integrable automorphization formula, retaining its AEStronglyMeasurable hypothesis.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For ℤ⊂ℝ the bridge is x mod ℤ↦−x mod ℤ and preserves circle volume.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:QuotientGroup.integral_eq_integral_automorphize`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.IsFundamentalDomain.quotientMeasure_eq`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.QuotientMeasureEqMeasurePreimage`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 7. The left-orbit convention used by Arthur; the bridge is an elementary derivation from inversion and the pinned right-orbit theorem.

### Topology of a closed homogeneous quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space` · lemma.

For a second countable locally compact Hausdorff group G and closed H, the left-orbit quotient H\G with its quotient topology is locally compact, Hausdorff and second countable; q:G→H\G is open. Over each compact subset of H\G there is a compact subset of G whose image contains it.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Saturations of open sets are unions of left translates, so q is open. Closedness of H separates distinct orbits.
2. Images of relatively compact neighborhoods give a local compactness basis. Cover a compact quotient subset by finitely many such images and take the union of their compact closures.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** .

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix B.1, Lemma B.1.1, pp. 343–344. The stated general homogeneous-space input and its proof supply this node after inversion from the manuscript’s left-Haar G/H convention to right-Haar H\G.

### Continuity and support of fibre averaging

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fibre-average-continuous` · lemma.

For f∈C_c(G,ℝ) and a right Haar dh on closed H, P f(Hg)=∫_H f(hg)dh is a continuous compactly supported function on H\G, with support contained in q(support f). It preserves positivity.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Right invariance of dh shows representative independence.
2. Near a fixed g, only a compact subset of H can contribute: intersect support f with H times a compact neighborhood of g. Uniform continuity on this compact set gives continuity under the integral.
3. The quotient projection of the compact support is compact; outside it the fibre integrand vanishes.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix B.1, Lemma B.1.2, p. 344. The stated general homogeneous-space input and its proof supply this node after inversion from the manuscript’s left-Haar G/H convention to right-Haar H\G.

### A cutoff over a compact part of the quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/compact-quotient-cutoff` · lemma.

For compact C⊂H\G there is β∈C_c(G,ℝ), β≥0, with Pβ=1 on C.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose finitely many nonnegative compactly supported functions on G whose averages are positive on neighborhoods covering C, using positive Haar mass of nonempty open sets.
2. Their sum ψ has Pψ>0 on a neighborhood of C. Choose a quotient compactly supported bump equal to 1 on C and supported in that neighborhood. Multiply ψ by the pullback of this bump divided by Pψ, extended by zero. Its support stays inside support ψ.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fibre-average-continuous`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix B.1, Lemma B.1.2 and its proof, p. 344. The stated general homogeneous-space input and its proof supply this node after inversion from the manuscript’s left-Haar G/H convention to right-Haar H\G.

### The right-Haar exchange identity

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/right-haar-exchange` · lemma.

Assume Δ_G(h)=Δ_H(h) for h∈H, and dg,dh are right Haar measures. For f,β∈C_c(G), ∫_G β(g)Pf(Hg)dg = ∫_G f(g)Pβ(Hg)dg.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Both iterated integrands have compact contributing subsets in the fibre and quotient, so use Fubini.
2. Substitute x=hg: map(L_h)dg=Δ_G(h)⁻¹dg. The result is ∫_G f(x)∫_H Δ_H(h)⁻¹β(h⁻¹x)dh_r dx. Set k=h⁻¹. Inversion sends dh_r to dh_l=Δ_H(k)⁻¹dh_r; the additional factor becomes Δ_H(k), so the inner integral is ∫_H β(kx)dk_r=Pβ(Hx).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For H=1 this reduces to commutativity of scalar multiplication under the right Haar integral.
- Replacing dh by left Haar destroys the representative-independence step for a nonunimodular H.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fibre-average-continuous`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.modularCharacter`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.integral_prod`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix B.1, Lemma B.1.3, pp. 344–346. The stated general homogeneous-space input and its proof supply this node after inversion from the manuscript’s left-Haar G/H convention to right-Haar H\G.

### Tonelli extension of quotient integration

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-tonelli` · lemma.

For a nonnegative Borel function f on G in the modular-compatible right-Haar setting, fibre integration is measurable on H\G and ∫_G f = ∫_{H\G}∫_H f(hg)dh. The identity is valid in [0,∞].

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. First extend the C_c identity to indicators of open sets by monotone approximation and regularity.
2. Use a monotone-class argument on the Borel σ-algebra, then approximate nonnegative functions by simple functions. The second countable setting supplies countable approximations and σ-finiteness.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/fibre-average-continuous`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.

### The central-character measurable Hermitian line

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-associated-line` · lemma.

With Γ=G(F), closed central X, XΓ closed, and continuous unitary ω trivial on X∩Γ, ξ(γz)=ω(z) defines a continuous unitary character of XΓ. The associated measurable Hermitian line field on XΓ\G has fibres (G×ℂ)/(hg,t)∼(g,ξ(h)⁻¹t). A Borel section of G→XΓ\G trivializes this field measurably; equivariant functions φ(hg)=ξ(h)φ(g) are its sections. Topological local triviality is not asserted for an arbitrary closed subgroup.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Well-definedness of ξ uses triviality on X∩Γ. The quotient X/(X∩Γ)→XΓ/Γ carries the stated quotient topology; continuity needs this topological identification.
2. Form the fibrewise quotient using ξ. The norm is independent of representative because |ξ|=1. The subsequent Borel-section node supplies measurable coordinates; no continuous local section is presumed.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/center-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-product-closed`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, p. 7; §13, p. 70. The quotient representation is the use; the associated bundle is the required precise central-character extension.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix E.1, covariant function construction, pp. 405–407. The manuscript constructs averaging, the quotient measure or induced covariant functions with left Haar measures on G/H. Inversion gives the right-Haar H\G convention used here; the density proof is corrected using subordinate functions as recorded in E11.

### A measurable section and unitary cocycle

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-measurable-section` · lemma.

For closed XΓ in the second countable adelic group there is a Borel section s:XΓ\G→G. With c(y,g)=s(y)g s(yg)⁻¹∈XΓ, right translation on sections is represented on quotient functions by ξ(c(y,g)) times translation y↦yg. Different sections give unitarily equivalent models.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the Borel-section theorem for quotients of Polish groups by closed subgroups, with its exact hypotheses recorded as an analytic input.
2. The cocycle identity is obtained by cancelling consecutive section values. A section change is multiplication by the measurable unit modulus transition function.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-associated-line`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.

### Completeness of central-character L² sections

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-l2-completeness` · lemma.

Square-integrable measurable sections of the associated Hermitian line, modulo equality almost everywhere, form a complex Hilbert space with inner product ∫conj(φ)ψ. Right translation is unitary and strongly continuous.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Trivialize measurably using the section and identify the space isometrically with the genuine L² space on the quotient; completeness is transported from Lp.
2. Quotient measure invariance and the unitary cocycle give unitarity. Approximate by continuous compactly supported local sections to prove strong continuity; this density is an explicit input, not a discrete fundamental-domain argument.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-measurable-section`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Lp`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.
- [Bachir Bekka, Pierre de la Harpe and Alain Valette, *Kazhdan’s Property (T)*](https://perso.univ-rennes1.fr/bachir.bekka/KazhdanTotal.pdf), Appendix E.1, completion construction and Remark E.1.2, pp. 407–408. The manuscript constructs averaging, the quotient measure or induced covariant functions with left Haar measures on G/H. Inversion gives the right-Haar H\G convention used here; the density proof is corrected using subordinate functions as recorded in E11.

### Extension and twisting of a central character

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-character-extension-twist` · lemma.

For X′⊂X with X′Γ\XΓ compact and continuous ω′ trivial on X′∩Γ, extend the resulting character of X′Γ/Γ to a continuous character ω₀ of XΓ/Γ. The operators ω₀(z)⁻¹R(z) define a strongly continuous unitary action of the compact abelian quotient XΓ/X′Γ on CentralCharL2(X′,ω′).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use continuous character extension for a closed subgroup of a locally compact abelian group; its supplier is recorded.
2. For z∈X′Γ the two scalar characters cancel, so the twisted action factors through the compact quotient. Its characters correspond exactly to extensions ω of ω′ via ω=ω₀χ.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-character-l2`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The invariant quotient integrals motivate this generalization. The nonnormal analytic proof is decomposed here; Arthur supplies the discrete/unimodular use, not the general cutoff proof.

### Finite-place scalar Jacobian for a chosen basis

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-finite-jacobian` · lemma.

For β:F_v^n≃∏_{w|v}E_w, let L_{β,v}=∑_j O_vβ_j. With every valuation ring of volume 1, j_{β,v}=vol_{∏E_w}(L_{β,v})⁻¹. In particular j_{β,v}=1 when β is an O_v-basis of ∏_{w|v}O_w.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Both transported measures are additive Haar measures. Evaluate their ratio on the compact open lattice L_{β,v}; its preimage is O_v^n of volume 1.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/local-normalized-haar`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`.

**Sources.**

- [Julia Gordon, with an appendix by Matthew Koster, *Orbital integrals and normalizations of measures*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.02391v1), Remark 2.1, p. 3; §5.1.1, pp. 31–33. Local measures of integral lattices distinguish integral normalization from self-dual different normalization.

### Archimedean scalar Jacobian for a chosen basis

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-infinite-jacobian` · lemma.

If F_v=ℝ and E⊗F_v=ℝ^a×ℂ^b, let D_{β,v} be the real determinant of the basis map in real and imaginary coordinates. Then j_{β,v}=2⁻ᵇ|D_{β,v}|⁻¹. If F_v=ℂ, then j_{β,v}=|det_ℂ β|⁻². The factors use dx at real places and 2dxdy at complex places.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the real linear change-of-variables formula. At a real base place the target measure is 2^b times coordinate Lebesgue measure.
2. At a complex base place the source and target both have 2^n; the real determinant is the squared complex determinant.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/adele-haar`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`.

**Sources.**

- [Julia Gordon, with an appendix by Matthew Koster, *Orbital integrals and normalizations of measures*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.02391v1), §2.2, Examples 2.3–2.4, pp. 5–7; §5.1.1, pp. 31–33. Explicit coordinate and archimedean normalization changes; the displayed general determinant statement is the linear-algebra extension.

### Global scalar Jacobian and absolute discriminants

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-global-jacobian` · lemma.

For an F-basis β of E, the positive factors j_{β,v} equal 1 at almost all finite v and ∏_v j_{β,v}=|d_F|^{[E:F]/2}|d_E|⁻¹/². Thus |d_F|⁻ⁿᵈ/²∏_v j_{β,v}^d=|d_E|⁻ᵈ/².

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Compute finite lattice indices and the archimedean determinant in a pseudo-basis of O_E over O_F.
2. Use the relative-discriminant norm identity from the supplier and the product formula to remove the basis dependence.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-finite-jacobian`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-infinite-jacobian`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-4-the-relative-discriminant`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.finprod_normalizedAbsValue_eq_one`.

**Sources.**

- [Julia Gordon, with an appendix by Matthew Koster, *Orbital integrals and normalizations of measures*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.02391v1), Remark 2.1, p. 3; §5.1.1, pp. 31–33. The local-to-global normalization is motivated here; the relative discriminant determinant calculation is requested from its existing owner.

### Closedness of the central rational product

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-product-closed` · lemma.

Let G be connected reductive over F and X a closed subgroup of Z(𝔸_F). If XZ(F) is closed in Z(𝔸_F), then XG(F) is closed in G(𝔸_F).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Map to G^ad(𝔸_F). The image of G(F) is a subgroup of the closed discrete set G^ad(F), so it is itself closed discrete.
2. Over each point of this discrete image, XG(F) is a translate of XZ(F), a closed subset of the closed fibre Z(𝔸_F). The union is closed because the image is closed discrete: a neighbourhood of any point meets at most one such fibre.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/center-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–9. The closed central quotient is needed for the automorphic Hilbert space; the adjoint-fibre proof makes its central-product hypothesis explicit.

## AA.3 — Reduction theory

The primitive chain is GL_n real reduction, the ℚ finite-adelic PID class calculation, closed-orbit realization, squared-weight bounds and lattice finiteness. It precedes the self-adjoint subgroup reduction and general class-number theorem. Parabolic factorization is separated from its Jacobian and compact-K integration. Root-cone integrals prove finite volume of Siegel sets, while a separate Levi/component argument is needed beyond the connected reductive core. Algebraic heights include inverse coordinates; polynomial coordinate comparison and geometry-of-numbers counting supply their properness and rational counting. The real branch fixes K and its Cartan-stable Siegel tori before applying Orr–Schnell containment and the rational pullback argument.

An adelic Siegel covering is a fundamental set with finite overlap, not a fundamental domain. Reduction of forms uses the full positive cone, with scalar invariance before determinant normalization. The change-of-K identity changes the argument of H_K; incompatible tori cannot be repaired by an unconditional containment assertion.

### Minimal rational parabolics and standard parabolics

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data` · construction.

Fix a minimal F-parabolic P_0 ⊂ G with Levi decomposition P_0 = M_0 N_0, M_0 the centralizer of a maximal F-split torus S_0. A standard parabolic is an F-parabolic P ⊇ P_0; it has a unique Levi component M_P ⊇ M_0, unipotent radical N_P, and split component A_P = A_{M_P}. The standard parabolics are finite in number, correspond to subsets of the simple relative roots Δ_0, and every F-parabolic is G(F)-conjugate to exactly one of them.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Existence and conjugacy of minimal F-parabolics and maximal split tori, relative roots and the standard parabolics (Borel–Tits, requested from RG2.1).
2. Levi decompositions on points: Tau Ceti dynamic parabolics P(λ) = U(λ) ⋊ L(λ) for an F-cocharacter λ of S_0 in the positive chamber realise each standard parabolic.
3. A_P is the split component of M_P (AA.2/split-centre).

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`: P_0, A_0 and Δ_0 in the Siegel set
- `AA.3/compactness-isotropic`: a proper F-parabolic forces noncompactness
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: constant terms along standard parabolics
- `ShimuraVarieties:V0`: rational parabolics for boundary components (RS-04 link AA.3 → V0)

**API.**

- `Reduction.MinimalParabolic` (structure): Structure: P_0, M_0, N_0, S_0 with the Levi decomposition.
- `Reduction.StandardParabolic` (constructor): The finite type of standard parabolics P ⊇ P_0, with M_P, N_P, A_P.
- `Reduction.standardParabolic_equiv_subsets` (equivalence): StandardParabolic ≃ Finset Δ_0.
- `Reduction.exists_unique_standard_conj` (characterisation): Every F-parabolic is G(F)-conjugate to a unique standard parabolic.
- `Reduction.StandardParabolic.le_iff` (relation): P ≤ P′ iff the corresponding subsets satisfy Δ_0^P ⊆ Δ_0^{P′} (with the convention that the subset lists the simple roots of M_P).

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.standardParabolic_gl3_card` (computation): For GL_3 there are 4 standard parabolics.
- `Reduction.standardParabolic_anisotropic` (degenerate): If G is F-anisotropic the only standard parabolic is G.
- `Reduction.standardParabolic_not_all_parabolics` (non-example): For GL_2 over ℚ the lower triangular Borel is a parabolic that is not standard; it is conjugate to the standard one by the Weyl element.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n, P_0 is the upper triangular Borel, standard parabolics are block upper triangular and correspond to compositions of n.
- For an F-anisotropic group P_0 = G and Δ_0 = ∅.

**Prerequisites:** `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.parabolic`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.unipotent`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.leviDecompositionMulEquiv`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 22. Exactly the standard parabolics.

### Relative chambers and the spaces a_P

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/relative-chamber` · construction.

For standard P, a_P = a_{M_P} (AA.2/real-character-space), with a_0 = a_{P_0}. For P_1 ⊆ P_2 there are split exact sequences giving a_{P_1} = a_{P_2} ⊕ a_{P_1}^{P_2} and dually. The roots Φ_P of (P, A_P) lie in (a_P^G)^*, ρ_P = (1/2) ∑_{α∈Φ_P} (dim 𝔫_α) α, the simple roots Δ_P are the restrictions of Δ_0 ∖ Δ_0^P, and the positive chamber is a_P^+ = {H ∈ a_P : α(H) > 0 for α ∈ Δ_P}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Restriction of characters X*_F(M_{P_2}) → X*_F(M_{P_1}) is injective; restriction X*(A_{P_1}) → X*(A_{P_2}) is surjective (Arthur §5); tensor with ℝ.
2. Root space decomposition of 𝔫_P under A_P.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`: the cone condition β(H_0(a) - T_1) > 0
- `AA.2/modulus-character`: δ_P = e^{2ρ_P(H_P)}
- `AutomorphicSpectralTheory:AS.1`: the positive chamber in Eisenstein series

**API.**

- `Reduction.aP` (constructor): The real vector space a_P for a standard parabolic.
- `Reduction.aPProjection` (projection): For P₁⊆P₂ the canonical linear projection a_{P₁}→a_{P₂}, dual to restriction of rational characters; its kernel is the relative space a_{P₁}^{P₂}.
- `Reduction.aP_decomp` (equivalence): For P₁≤P₂, restriction of rational characters induces the canonical projection π:a_{P₁}→a_{P₂}; a_{P₁}≃ₗ a_{P₂}×ker π, with the splitting induced by the split centres. The kernel is 0 for P₁=P₂.
- `Reduction.rho` (data): ρ_P ∈ (a_P^G)^*.
- `Reduction.simpleRoots` (data): Δ_P ⊂ (a_P^G)^*, a basis.
- `Reduction.positiveChamber` (data): a_P^+ = {H | ∀ α ∈ Δ_P, 0 < α H}.

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.rho_gl2` (computation): For the Borel of GL_2, ρ = (1/2)(e_1 - e_2).
- `Reduction.aP_top` (degenerate): For P = G and G without rational characters, a_P = 0; in general a_G^G = 0.
- `Reduction.positiveChamber_not_cone_of_all_roots` (non-example): For GL_3, a_0^+ is cut out by the two simple roots; positivity of e_1 - e_3 alone does not imply membership.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n and P_0, a_0 = ℝ^n, a_G = ℝ (diagonal), Δ_0 = {e_i - e_{i+1}}, ρ_0 = ((n-1)/2, …, (1-n)/2).
- For P=G the relative space a_G^G is 0 and its chamber is a point. The unquotiented a_G^+ has no root inequalities and equals a_G, which can be nonzero.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/real-character-space`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters-free`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §5, p. 24. The decompositions of a_P.
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §5, p. 25. ρ_P.

### Admissible maximal compact subgroup of G(𝔸)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/good-maximal-compact` · definition.

A maximal compact subgroup K = ∏_v K_v of G(𝔸_F) is admissible relative to M_0 if K_v = 𝓗(𝒪_v) is hyperspecial for all but finitely many v, each K_v is a special maximal compact subgroup in good position relative to M_0 at finite v and a maximal compact subgroup of G(F_v) at archimedean v, and G(F_v) = P_0(F_v) K_v for every v.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose an integral model; for almost all v it is reductive and 𝓗(𝒪_v) is hyperspecial and in good position (RG2.3 request).
2. At the remaining finite places choose special maximal compacts in good position (RG2.4 Iwasawa), at archimedean places a maximal compact (LieGroups layer 9).
3. The product is compact by AA.1/restricted-product-comparison and Tychonoff.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`: G(𝔸) = P(𝔸)K
- `AA.3/H-P`: well-definedness of H_P
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.2`: K-finite automorphic forms

**API.**

- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact` (structure): Structure: the local K_v with the hyperspecial, special and Iwasawa conditions.
- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact.toSubgroup` (projection): The compact subgroup ∏_v K_v of G(𝔸).
- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact.isCompact` (characterisation): toSubgroup is compact.
- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact.exists` (constructor): An admissible K exists for every minimal parabolic data.

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact.gln` (computation): For GL_n over ℚ, O(n) × ∏_p GL_n(ℤ_p) is admissible.
- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact.anisotropic` (degenerate): For F-anisotropic G the Iwasawa condition is vacuous (P_0 = G).
- `Reduction.AdmissibleCompact.not_all_places_iwahori` (non-example): The Iwahori subgroup (upper triangular modulo p) is a proper subgroup of GL_2(ℤ_p), so a product of Iwahori subgroups is not maximal compact and not admissible.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n, K = O(n) × ∏_p GL_n(ℤ_p) (Arthur §4).
- The product of the local K_v is compact only because K_v = 𝓗(𝒪_v) for almost all v.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-model`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-points-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.3`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 23. The GL_n example.
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 24. The admissible K.

### Adelic Iwasawa decomposition and integration formula

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa` · theorem.

For admissible K and standard P, G(𝔸_F) = P(𝔸_F)K = N_P(𝔸)M_P(𝔸)^1 A_P(ℝ)^0 K, and for f ∈ L¹(G(𝔸)), ∫_{G(𝔸)} f(x) dx = ∫_K ∫_{M_P(𝔸)} ∫_{N_P(𝔸)} f(nmk) δ_P(m)^{-1} dn dm dk for compatible Haar measures.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; K admissible.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The factorization and the integrable-function formula are separate prerequisites.
2. Apply compact-factor Iwasawa integration first to compactly supported continuous functions, then to nonnegative Borel functions and the positive/negative parts of an integrable function.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n over ℚ this is Gram–Schmidt at ∞ and GL_n(ℚ_p) = P_0(ℚ_p)GL_n(ℤ_p) at p.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa-factorization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/iwasawa-integration-compact`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 24. Exactly the decomposition.

### The map H_P

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/H-P` · definition.

For standard P and admissible K, H_P : G(𝔸_F) → a_P is H_P(nmk) = H_{M_P}(m) for n ∈ N_P(𝔸), m ∈ M_P(𝔸), k ∈ K; write H_0 = H_{P_0}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; K admissible.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Extend each rational character χ of M_P to P by χ(nm)=χ(m). If pK=p′K then p⁻¹p′∈P∩K, and the positive adelic norm of χ is 1 on this compact group.
2. The resulting character logarithms descend along the open surjection P×K→G. This proves well-definedness and continuity without asserting an undefined M_P-height on N_P.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`: Siegel sets via H_0
- `AA.2/modulus-character`: δ_P(p) = e^{2ρ_P(H_P(p))}
- `AutomorphicSpectralTheory:AS.1`: Eisenstein series E(x, φ, λ) built from e^{(λ+ρ_P)(H_P(x))}

**API.**

- `Reduction.HP` (constructor): Reduction.HP P : AdelicPoints H → a_P.
- `Reduction.HP_nmk` (simp): HP (n * m * k) = logHeight_{M_P} m.
- `Reduction.HP_left_P` (relation): HP (p * x) = HP p + HP x for p ∈ P(𝔸).
- `Reduction.continuous_HP` (instance): HP is continuous.
- `Reduction.HP_rational` (simp): HP (diagonal γ * x) = HP x for γ ∈ P(F).

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.HP_gl2_borel` (computation): For x in P(𝔸), the pairing of HP x with each rational character χ of P is log ‖χ(y)‖ for a point y of P; for GL_2 and the Borel, HP (diag(a, d)) = (log ‖a‖, log ‖d‖).
- `Reduction.HP_top` (degenerate): For P = G, HP = logHeight.
- `Reduction.HP_not_homomorphism` (non-example): HP is not a homomorphism on G(𝔸): for GL_2, HP(w) = 0 for the Weyl element w ∈ K, but HP of a product of upper and lower unipotents can be nonzero.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n and P_0, H_0(x) = (log|a_1|, …, log|a_n|) for x = n·diag(a_i)·k.
- H_G = H_P composed with a_P → a_G.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa-factorization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/relative-chamber`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 24. Exactly H_P.

### Adelic Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-siegel-set` · definition.

For T_1 ∈ a_0 and a compact subset ω ⊂ N_0(𝔸)M_0(𝔸)^1, the Siegel set is 𝔖(T_1, ω) = {p a k : p ∈ ω, a ∈ A_0(ℝ)^0, k ∈ K, β(H_0(a) - T_1) > 0 for all β ∈ Δ_0}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; K admissible.

**Planet:** Adelic Siegel set.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define as the image of ω × {a : ...} × K under multiplication; it is invariant under A_G(ℝ)^0.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`: the fundamental-set property
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: rapid decay of cusp forms on Siegel sets
- `MetaplecticAutomorphicForms:MP.5`: convergence of theta integrals estimated on Siegel sets (RT-AREA-automorphic-1/23)
- `AA.3/height-siegel-estimate`: height estimates

**API.**

- `Reduction.siegelSet` (constructor): Reduction.siegelSet T₁ ω : Set (AdelicPoints H).
- `Reduction.mem_siegelSet` (characterisation): x ∈ siegelSet T₁ ω iff x = p a k with the stated conditions.
- `Reduction.siegelSet_mono` (relation): siegelSet is antitone in T₁ (coordinatewise for Δ_0) and monotone in ω.
- `Reduction.siegelSet_mul_K` (simp): siegelSet T₁ ω * K = siegelSet T₁ ω.
- `Reduction.siegelSet_center` (relation): siegelSet is stable under A_G(ℝ)^0.

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.siegelSet_sl2` (computation): For SL_2/ℚ, some Siegel set with compact ω meets every SL_2(ℚ)-orbit in SL_2(𝔸) (at ∞ it contains the standard fundamental domain of SL_2(ℤ)).
- `Reduction.siegelSet_anisotropic` (degenerate): For F-anisotropic G, siegelSet T₁ ω = ω * K.
- `Reduction.siegelSet_not_fundamental_domain` (non-example): For SL₂/ℚ choose the finite factor SL₂(ℤ̂), real N-window [−1,1], and A-parameter y≥1/2. Both the identity and n(1) lie in this Siegel set; their ratio is a nontrivial rational element. Thus this specified Siegel set cannot be a fundamental domain with disjoint rational translates.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2 over ℚ, 𝔖 ∩ SL_2(ℝ) is a classical Siegel set {n a_y k : |x| ≤ c, y > t}.
- For F-anisotropic G, 𝔖 = ω K, a compact set.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/relative-chamber`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/H-P`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/good-maximal-compact`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §8, p. 37. Exactly the definition.

### Siegel sets cover G(F)\G(𝔸)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic` · theorem.

There are T_1 and ω such that G(𝔸_F) = G(F) 𝔖(T_1, ω) (Borel–Harish-Chandra).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; K admissible.

**Planet:** Reduction theory: Siegel covering.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Reduce to F = ℚ by restriction of scalars (AA.1/base-change-adelic).
2. For GL_n: classical reduction (Hermite–Minkowski) for GL_n(ℝ)/GL_n(ℤ) via reduced forms (reduction-siegel-dictionary) together with class-number finiteness for GL_n(𝔸_f) (class-number-finite), Borel 4.4.
3. For reductive G ⊂ GL_n embedded self-adjointly: G(𝔸) ⊂ GL_n(ℚ) 𝔖_{GL_n}, and the finiteness of G(ℚ)-orbits on GL_n(ℚ)·w ∩ closed orbits (Borel 4.5, 5.4) gives finitely many translates x_i𝔖_{GL_n} ∩ G(𝔸) covering; these are contained in finitely many Siegel sets of G.
4. Connected G: G = H ⋉ N with N unipotent (Borel 4.6).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ it reduces to ModularGroup.exists_smul_mem_fd at the archimedean place and SL_2(ℚ)SL_2(ℤ̂) = SL_2(𝔸_f).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduction-siegel-dictionary`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `mathlib:ModularGroup.exists_smul_mem_fd`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-adelic-covering`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/self-adjoint-reduction`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), Theorem 8.1, p. 37. Exactly the statement.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 4.6, p. 18. Existence of fundamental sets, built from Siegel domains.

### Siegel property

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-finiteness-adelic` · theorem.

For a Siegel set 𝔖 = 𝔖(T_1, ω), the set {γ ∈ G(F) : γ𝔖 ∩ 𝔖 ≠ ∅} is finite.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; K admissible.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Reduce to ℚ and the real bounded-denominator Siegel property, using the compact finite part to bound denominators of γ and γ⁻¹.
2. Real overlap then gives finiteness. Discreteness alone does not bound a rational element in the unbounded cusp direction.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ, only finitely many γ ∈ SL_2(ℚ) map the Siegel set of siegelSet_sl2 to itself (essentially ±1, ±S, ±T^{±1}, ±ST, ±TS).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-overlap`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-part-denominator-bound`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Definition 4.1, p. 17. The finiteness condition (F2).
- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(2), p. 9. The archimedean Siegel property.

### Finiteness of class numbers

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite` · theorem.

For every linear algebraic group G over F and every compact open subgroup U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}), the double coset space G(F)\G(𝔸_{F,f})/U is finite; equivalently G(𝔸_F) = ⋃_{i=1}^h G(F) x_i G(F_∞) U for finitely many x_i.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a linear algebraic group over F; U compact open.

**Planet:** Finiteness of class numbers.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For connected reductive G reduce to ℚ by restriction of scalars; self-adjoint-reduction gives a covering fundamental set with compact finite projection (Borel §4.5(3)–(4)).
2. A compact subset meets only finitely many U-cosets. Reduce connected nonreductive G by its characteristic-zero Levi decomposition and the unipotent/semidirect lemmas.
3. For nonconnected G use compactness of G(𝔸)/G°(𝔸) and commensurability of conjugated levels as Borel §5.1. These structural extension inputs are recorded separately; no call to siegel-covering-adelic is made.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/component-decomposition`: finitely many components
- `AA.4/neat-level-exists`: finitely many rational intersections
- `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII:F4/fixed-level-class-bound`: finiteness for unit groups of semisimple endomorphism algebras (request from F4)
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.3/definite-genus-class-finite`: finiteness of classes in a genus
- `ShimuraVarieties:V0`: adelic component finiteness

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = GL_1, h = h_F (class-number-gl1).
- For G = SL_n, h = 1 (strong approximation).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/unipotent-class-number-one`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/semidirect-class-number`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/self-adjoint-reduction`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.1, p. 19. Exactly the statement (Borel's G_A^∞ = G_∞ × ∏_p G_{o_p}).
- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.12, p. 57. The finite-adelic form.

### Unipotent groups have class number one

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/unipotent-class-number-one` · lemma.

For a unipotent group N over F and compact open U ⊂ N(𝔸_f), N(𝔸_f) = N(F)U.

**Hypotheses.** N unipotent over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For G_a this is density of F in 𝔸_{F,f} (GlobalNumberFields layer 6).
2. Induct on a central composition series with G_a quotients (Borel 2.4, 2.5).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the unipotent radical of the Borel of GL_2, N(𝔸_f) = N(ℚ)N(ℤ̂).

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Corollary 2.5, p. 13. Exactly the statement.

### Class numbers of semidirect products

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/semidirect-class-number` · lemma.

If G = H ⋉ N over F with N unipotent, then every double coset G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U meets H(𝔸_f), and G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U is finite if H(F)\H(𝔸_f)/(U ∩ H(𝔸_f)) is finite for all compact open U.

**Hypotheses.** G = H ⋉ N.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Borel 2.4 and 2.7, using N(𝔸_f) = N(F)·(open subgroup) (unipotent-class-number-one) and that N(𝔸) is normal with G(𝔸) = H(𝔸) ⋉ N(𝔸) (Borel 1.6).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the Borel B = T ⋉ N of GL_2, the class number of B is bounded by that of the torus T.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/unipotent-class-number-one`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Proposition 2.7, p. 13. Exactly the statement.

### Arithmetic subgroups attached to a level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level` · definition.

For compact open U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}) and x ∈ G(𝔸_{F,f}), Γ_{x,U} = G(F) ∩ x U x⁻¹, viewed in G(F_∞) through the diagonal. It is a discrete subgroup of G(F_∞); for x, x′ and U, U′ the groups Γ_{x,U} and Γ_{x′,U′} are commensurable after conjugating by a rational element when x′ ∈ G(F) x U.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Discreteness: G(F) is discrete in G(𝔸) (AA.1/rational-points-discrete) and x U x⁻¹ is compact open in G(𝔸_f).
2. Commensurability: compact open subgroups are commensurable (AA.1/compact-open-product).

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/component-decomposition`: the components Γ_i\G(F_∞)
- `AA.4/neat-level`: neatness of all Γ_{x,U}
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0`: explicit stabilizers and component maps
- `ShimuraData:D5`: the arithmetic subgroup attached to a level and a component

**API.**

- `Reduction.levelArithmetic` (constructor): Reduction.levelArithmetic x U : Subgroup (G(F)) := G(F) ∩ x U x⁻¹.
- `Reduction.levelArithmetic_discrete` (instance): Its image in G(F_∞) is discrete.
- `Reduction.levelArithmetic_conj` (relation): levelArithmetic (γ x u) U = γ (levelArithmetic x U) γ⁻¹ for γ ∈ G(F), u ∈ U.
- `Reduction.levelArithmetic_commensurable` (relation): For U′ ≤ U, levelArithmetic x U′ has finite index in levelArithmetic x U.

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.levelArithmetic_gl2` (computation): For GL_2/ℚ, levelArithmetic 1 GL_2(ℤ̂) = GL_2(ℤ).
- `Reduction.levelArithmetic_trivial_group` (degenerate): For the trivial group it is trivial.
- `Reduction.levelArithmetic_not_conj_invariant` (non-example): levelArithmetic x U depends on x and not only on U: for GL_2/ℚ and x = diag(p,1) at the place p, levelArithmetic x GL_2(ℤ̂) = diag(p,1) GL_2(ℤ) diag(p,1)⁻¹ ≠ GL_2(ℤ).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2/ℚ, U = GL_2(ℤ̂), x = 1: Γ = GL_2(ℤ).
- For U = K(N) the principal congruence level, Γ = Γ(N) inside GL_2(ℤ).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.13, p. 57. The arithmetic groups of a level.

### Component decomposition of a level quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition` · theorem.

Let x_1, …, x_h represent G(F)\G(𝔸_{F,f})/U (class-number-finite). Then [g_∞] ↦ [(g_∞, x_i)] induces a homeomorphism ⊔_i Γ_{x_i,U}\G(F_∞) ≃ G(F)\G(𝔸_F)/U, equivariant for the right action of G(F_∞); the archimedean factor and the split centre are retained, and the finite set G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U is in general not the whole quotient.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Every (g_∞, g_f) is G(F)-equivalent to (γ⁻¹g_∞, x_i u) for unique i (Milne 5.13).
2. Injectivity on each piece: (g_∞, x_i) ∼ (g′_∞, x_i) iff g′_∞ = γ g_∞ with γ ∈ Γ_{x_i,U}.
3. Topology: U is open, so G(F)\G(𝔸)/U is a disjoint union of open pieces, each homeomorphic to Γ_{x_i,U}\G(F_∞).

**Uses.**

- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0`: the finite decomposition of X_K into arithmetic components
- `AA.4/level-volume-index`: volumes of level quotients
- `AA.5/gl2-upper-half-plane-component`: the GL_2 comparison
- `ShimuraVarieties:V1`: component input (RS-04 link AA.3 → V1)

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_1/ℚ and U = ℤ̂^×: ℚ^×\𝔸^×/ℤ̂^× ≃ {±1}\ℝ^× ≅ ℝ_{>0}.
- For GL_2/ℚ and K(N), the full rational class set GL_2(ℚ)\GL_2(𝔸_f)/K(N) is (ℤ/N)^×/{±1}; its pieces retain GL_2(ℝ). The φ(N) upper-half-plane components use GL_2(ℚ)^+ instead (AA.5). For N≥3 their number is twice the cardinality of the full rational class set.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-locally-compact`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.13, p. 57. The homeomorphism, here before dividing by K_∞.
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 13. The arithmetic groups of the components.

### Finite volume of G(F)\G(𝔸)^1

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume` · theorem.

For a connected reductive group G/F, G(F)\G(𝔸_F)^1 has finite positive volume for the invariant quotient measure. For a connected nonreductive group the same conclusion follows after supplying its characteristic-zero Levi decomposition, compact unipotent adelic quotient and the associated product integration.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G connected.

**Planet:** Finite volume of G(F)\G(A)^1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The norm-one part of the Siegel covering has finite measure by iwasawa-integration-compact and positive-root-cone-integral.
2. The quotient measure of its image is bounded above by this finite measure; overlap finiteness is unnecessary for this inequality.
3. Positivity follows from a nonempty quotient-open chart. The connected nonreductive extension is a separate Levi/unipotent input.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_1, vol(F^×\𝔸^1) < ∞ (GlobalNumberFields layer 6 compactness).
- For SL_2/ℚ, vol(SL_2(ℚ)\SL_2(𝔸)) = vol(SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ)) vol(SL_2(ℤ̂)) < ∞.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-finiteness-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/automorphic-quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/norm-one-subgroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/modular-function-parabolic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-set-finite-measure`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.8, p. 22. Exactly the statement.

### Tamagawa numbers are finite

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/tamagawa-number-finite` · lemma.

For connected reductive G, the Tamagawa number τ(G) of AA.2/tamagawa-number is finite and positive.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Finite by finite-volume; positive because the quotient measure of a nonempty open set is positive.

**Acceptance checks.**

- τ(G_m) = 1 is finite.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-number`.

**Sources.**

- [Zev Rosengarten, *Tamagawa numbers and other invariants of pseudo-reductive groups over global function fields*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10723v3), §1, p. 3. Finiteness makes τ(G) a real number.

### When G(F)\G(𝔸) has finite volume

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume-criterion` · theorem.

For a linear algebraic group G over F, G(F)\G(𝔸_F) carries a G(𝔸)-invariant measure of finite volume iff X*_F(G°) = 0.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G linear algebraic over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For connected reductive G the zero-character case is finite-volume, since its norm-one group equals the full adelic group.
2. A nonzero rational character gives a noncompact positive-real quotient. The split-centre product integration makes the volume infinite; merely pushing forward a measure does not justify a Haar conclusion.
3. Apply the separately recorded Levi/unipotent and component-quotient reductions for the stated general linear-group scope.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_1(F)\GL_1(𝔸) has infinite volume; SL_n(F)\SL_n(𝔸) has finite volume.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.6(i), p. 21. Exactly the statement.

### Anisotropic groups have compact quotients

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic` · theorem.

For connected reductive G over F whose derived group is F-anisotropic (equivalently, G has no proper F-parabolic subgroup), G(F)\G(𝔸_F)^1 is compact.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; G^der F-anisotropic.

**Planet:** Compactness criterion.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Then P_0 = G, Δ_0 = ∅, and a Siegel set is ω K with ω compact in G(𝔸)^1·A_G(ℝ)^0 modulo A_G(ℝ)^0 (adelic-siegel-set).
2. siegel-covering-adelic gives G(𝔸)^1 = G(F)·(compact set).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For a definite quaternion algebra D over ℚ, D^×(ℚ)\D^×(𝔸)^1 is compact (AA.5/definite-quaternion-compact).
- For F^× this recovers compactness of F^×\𝔸^1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.8, p. 22. Compactness criterion (sufficiency).
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 21. The criterion for reductive groups.

### Isotropic groups have noncompact quotients

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-isotropic` · theorem.

For connected reductive G over F with a proper F-parabolic subgroup, G(F)\G(𝔸_F)^1 is not compact. Precisely: G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 is compact iff G(F) has no nontrivial unipotent element iff G^der is F-anisotropic.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. A proper F-parabolic gives a nontrivial F-split torus S ⊄ Z(G) and a nontrivial unipotent u ∈ N_P(F) (Borel–Tits, RG2.1).
2. Choose a ∈ S(F_v) at an archimedean v with a^n u a^{-n} → 1; the points G(F)·a^{-n} cannot have a convergent subsequence in G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 since otherwise γ_n a^{-n}k_n → x would force a^n u a^{-n} ∈ x⁻¹G(F)x near 1, contradicting discreteness (Mahler-type argument).
3. The equivalences with the unipotent and anisotropy conditions are Borel–Tits (RG2.1).

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℚ)\SL_2(𝔸) is not compact; the sequence diag(n, 1/n) at ∞ escapes.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/norm-one-subgroup`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.8, p. 22. Compactness criterion (necessity).
- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, p. 21. The reductive formulation.

### Cocompact arithmetic groups contain no unipotents

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/cocompact-no-unipotents` · theorem.

Let G be connected semisimple over ℚ and Γ = G(ℚ) ∩ U for a compact open U ⊂ G(𝔸_f). If Γ\G(ℝ) is compact then Γ contains no nontrivial unipotent element.

**Hypotheses.** G connected semisimple over ℚ; U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. By component-decomposition and finite index arguments, Γ\G(ℝ) is compact iff G(ℚ)\G(𝔸) is compact.
2. Apply compactness-isotropic: a nontrivial unipotent in Γ ⊂ G(ℚ) makes G isotropic, hence the quotient noncompact.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ) is noncompact and SL_2(ℤ) contains (1 1; 0 1).
- For a definite quaternion algebra the unit groups are finite and contain no unipotents.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-isotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Remarks 1.4(1), p. 5. The statement, proved here for arithmetic Γ from the compactness criterion.

### S-arithmetic subgroups are lattices

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/s-arithmetic-lattice` · theorem.

Let G be connected semisimple over F, S a finite set of places containing the archimedean ones, and U^S ⊂ G(𝔸_F^S) compact open. Then Γ_S = G(F) ∩ G(F_S)U^S is a lattice in G(F_S) = ∏_{v∈S} G(F_v), cocompact iff G is F-anisotropic.

**Hypotheses.** G connected semisimple over F; S finite ⊇ archimedean places.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G(F)\G(F)G(F_S)U^S is open in G(F)\G(𝔸) and homeomorphic to Γ_S\G(F_S)/... ≃ Γ_S\G(F_S) × U^S/(U^S ∩ ...) up to the compact factor U^S (as in component-decomposition with S in place of the archimedean places).
2. An open subset of a finite-volume space has finite volume (finite-volume); compactness by compactness-anisotropic and compactness-isotropic.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℤ[1/p]) is a lattice in SL_2(ℝ) × SL_2(ℚ_p).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-isotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6, p. 16. S-arithmetic lattices, used in the proof of strong approximation.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Introduction, p. 6; §8, pp. 26–30. The S-arithmetic extension. The quoted sentence is in the Introduction, not §8; the S-arithmetic proof needs the separate recorded reduction inputs.

### Height functions on G(𝔸)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-height` · definition.

Choose a faithful F-algebraic representation r : G → GL_m containing a representation and its dual (and, if needed, a trivial summand), so the resulting height is proper and each local norm is at least 1. Define ‖x‖_r = product over v of ‖r(x)_v‖_v, using the entrywise maximum of normalized absolute values at finite v and the Hilbert–Schmidt norm raised to the archimedean multiplicity [F_v:ℝ] at infinity. The height is submultiplicative, has compact sublevel sets, and satisfies polynomial inverse and rational-point counting bounds, subject to the recorded proper-height and counting inputs.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; r is an algebraic faithful representation chosen with the stated dual/properness condition; arbitrary abstract faithful point representations do not suffice.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the actual closed algebraic embedding augmented by its dual; the normalized local norms are at least one and equal one at almost all finite places.
2. Finite matrix maximum norms and infinite Hilbert–Schmidt norms are submultiplicative. Polynomial coordinate comparison bounds the inverse.
3. Properness is adelic-height-proper. Each rational coordinate projective height is bounded by a power of the group height; rational-coordinate-height-count supplies the polynomial count.

**Uses.**

- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.0`: moderate growth and polynomial comparability (RS-04: AA.3 owns the height comparison)
- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.3`: rapid decay |φ(x)| ≤ C‖x‖^{-N} on Siegel sets
- `AA.3/height-siegel-estimate`: height on Siegel sets

**API.**

- `Reduction.height` (constructor): Reduction.height r : AdelicPoints H → ℝ.
- `Reduction.height_mul_le` (relation): height (x * y) ≤ height x * height y.
- `Reduction.height_inv_le` (relation): ∃ C N, height x⁻¹ ≤ C * height x ^ N.
- `Reduction.isCompact_height_le` (characterisation): {x | height x ≤ t} is compact for a suitable r.
- `Reduction.card_rational_height_le` (relation): #{γ ∈ G(F) | height γ ≤ t} ≤ C t^N.

**Unit tests.**

- `Reduction.height_gl1` (computation): For GL₁/ℚ with r(x)=diag(x,x⁻¹), height x⁻¹=height x; the real local factor is sqrt(x²+x⁻²), and each finite factor is max(|x|_p,|x⁻¹|_p).
- `Reduction.height_one` (degenerate): With Hilbert–Schmidt norms and normalized infinite-place exponents, height 1 = m^([F:ℚ]/2). In particular over ℚ and m=2 this is sqrt 2, not 1.
- `Reduction.height_not_finite_only` (non-example): If G(F_∞) is noncompact the height is unbounded on G(𝔸); a height built from finite places only would be bounded on G(F_∞) and fail the compactness of height balls.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_1/ℚ with r(x)=diag(x,x⁻¹), ‖x‖ = sqrt(|x∞|²+|x∞|⁻²) times product over finite v of max(|x_v|_v,|x_v|_v⁻¹).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-support-conjugate`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gln-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-height-proper`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/rational-coordinate-height-count`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/local-height-polynomial`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §13, p. 70. Exactly the height.

### Comparison of heights

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/height-representation-comparison` · theorem.

For two proper F-algebraic height representations r,r′ satisfying adelic-height, there are C,N>0 with ‖x‖_{r′}≤C‖x‖_r^N for every x∈G(𝔸_F), and conversely. Multiplication on either side by a fixed compact subgroup changes these heights by bounded factors.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; Both representations are algebraic and define proper heights controlling their inverses.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Multiply the local polynomial comparisons; only finitely many c_v differ from one.
2. Use submultiplicativity for bounded height and inverse-height on the compact subgroup. Generic adelic comparison belongs here; AF.1 consumes it.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G_m over ℚ, r=id is not an admissible proper height representation: at ideles x with x∞→0 and x_f=1, its height tends to 0 whereas the height of id⊕dual tends to infinity. Compare id⊕dual with another representation containing its dual instead.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/local-height-polynomial`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §13, p. 70. Polynomial control, the mechanism of the comparison.

### Heights on Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/height-siegel-estimate` · lemma.

On a Siegel set 𝔖(T_1, ω) there are c, C > 0 such that for x = pak, c^{-1} e^{c‖H_0(a)‖} ≤ ‖x‖ ≤ C e^{C‖H_0(a)‖} (any norm on a_0); in particular log‖x‖ and ‖H_0(x)‖ are comparable on 𝔖 ∩ G(𝔸)^1 up to constants.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Write r(pak) = r(p) r(a) r(k): r(p), r(k) lie in compact sets; r(a) is diagonalizable with eigenvalues e^{λ(H_0(a))} for the weights λ of r.
2. Upper bound from the largest weight; lower bound because the weights span a_0^* for faithful r.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ at ∞, ‖n a_y k‖ ≍ y^{1/2} + y^{-1/2}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/H-P`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §13, p. 70. Heights measured on Siegel sets.

### Horospherical decomposition for a fixed maximal compact

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition` · construction.

Let G be connected semisimple over ℚ, G = G(ℝ)^+, K ⊂ G maximal compact with Cartan involution θ, and 𝐏 a ℚ-parabolic with unipotent radical 𝐍_P and Levi quotient 𝐋_P. With S_P the split centre of 𝐋_P, A_P = S_P(ℝ)^0 and M_P the real points of ⋂_{χ∈X*(𝐋_P)} ker χ², there is a unique θ-stable real Levi lift of (𝐋_P)_ℝ, giving P = N_P A_P M_P and the diffeomorphism N_P × A_P × (M_P K) → G. Left multiplication by p_0 = n_0 a_0 m_0 acts by (n, a, m) ↦ (n_0 · (a_0m_0) n (a_0m_0)⁻¹, a_0 a, m_0 m).

**Hypotheses.** G connected semisimple over ℚ; K a maximal compact subgroup of G(ℝ)^+; 𝐏 a ℚ-parabolic.
