**Construction or proof.**

1. θ-stable Levi lift: Borel–Serre 1.9 (cited by BKT).
2. The decomposition G = P K (real Iwasawa decomposition, LieGroups layer 9) and P = N_P ⋊ L_P with L_P = A_P × M_P.
3. The action formula is the conjugation computation; BKT print 'right action', corrected to left action (source issue).
4. For K′=uKu⁻¹ write g=puk u⁻¹, so gu=pu k. Right multiplication of p by u∈N_P leaves its A-coordinate fixed. This proves the stated change-of-basepoint formula.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/real-siegel-set`: coordinates of Siegel sets
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.2`: Borel–Serre corners
- `ArithmeticQuotientDefinability`: definable charts (Part II route of PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20)

**API.**

- `RealSiegel.HoroData` (structure): Structure: N_P, A_P, M_P K, the homeomorphism horoDecomp : G ≃ₜ N_P × A_P × (M_P K) for fixed K and 𝐏, and the simple roots.
- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_left_mul` (simp): The left action formula of p_0 = n_0 a_0 m_0.
- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_conj` (functoriality): Conjugation by g ∈ G(ℚ) carries the decomposition for (𝐏, K) to that for (g𝐏g⁻¹, gKg⁻¹).
- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_change_K` (relation): For K′=uKu⁻¹ with u∈N_P and the Levi lifts identified by conjugation by u, the A-coordinate satisfies a_{K′}(g)=a_K(gu), equivalently H_{P,K′}(g)=H_{P,K}(gu). It need not equal a_K(g).

**Unit tests.**

- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_sl2` (computation): For SL_2(ℝ): (x, a, ±k) ↦ n(x) diag(√a, 1/√a)(±k).
- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_trivial_parabolic` (degenerate): For 𝐏 = 𝐆, N_P = 1, A_P = 1 and the decomposition is G = M_G K.
- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_not_right_action` (non-example): The formula (n_0a_0m_0)(n,a,m) describes left multiplication; read as a right action it fails already for SL_2 with n_0 ≠ 1.
- `RealSiegel.horoDecomp_change_K_sl2` (computation): In SL₂(ℝ), take u=n(1), k=[[0,−1],[1,0]]∈SO(2). The upper-half-plane height of ki is 1 whereas that of k(1+i) is 1/2. Thus a_K(k)=1 but a_{uKu⁻¹}(k)=1/2 under the parameter diag(√a,1/√a).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℝ), K = SO(2), 𝐏 upper triangular: N_P = {n(x)}, A_P = {diag(a^{1/2}, a^{-1/2})}, M_P = {±1}.
- Changing K changes the Levi lift by conjugation by an element of N_P.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.parabolic`, `tauceti:TauCeti.Cocharacter.leviDecompositionMulEquiv`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §2.2, p. 8. The θ-stable Levi lift.
- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), (2.2), p. 8. The horospherical decomposition.

### Simple roots of P and truncated tori

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/positive-root-coordinates` · construction.

Φ(A_P, N_P) is the set of characters of A_P on Lie N_P; Δ(A_P, N_P) = {α_1, …, α_r} is the unique set of dim A_P linearly independent roots of which every root is a nonnegative integral combination (the simple roots). For t > 0, A_{P,t} = {a ∈ A_P : a^α > t for all α ∈ Δ(A_P, N_P)}, and e_P(a) = (a^{-α_1}, …, a^{-α_r}) is a semialgebraic diffeomorphism A_P ≃ (ℝ_{>0})^r with e_P(A_{P,t}) = (0, 1/t)^r.

**Hypotheses.** as in horospherical-decomposition.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Root decomposition of 𝔫_P under A_P; the simple roots come from the relative root system of the Levi (RG2.1 over ℚ).
2. e_P is the composite of the logarithm and the dual basis of Δ.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/real-siegel-set`: the truncation A_{P,t}
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.2`: corner coordinates e_P

**API.**

- `RealSiegel.HoroData.simpleRoots` (data): Δ(A_P, N_P) as a Finset of positive characters of A_P.
- `RealSiegel.truncatedTorus` (data): A_{P,t}.
- `RealSiegel.cornerCoord` (equivalence): e_P : A_P ≃ (Fin r → ℝ_{>0}).
- `RealSiegel.cornerCoord_truncated` (simp): e_P '' A_{P,t} = Set.pi univ (fun _ => Ioo 0 t⁻¹).

**Unit tests.**

- `RealSiegel.simpleRoots_sl2` (computation): For SL_2 and the Borel, Δ = {α} with diag(a, a⁻¹)^α = a².
- `RealSiegel.simpleRoots_minimal_rank` (degenerate): For a maximal parabolic of SL_n, Δ has one element.
- `RealSiegel.simpleRoots_not_all_roots` (non-example): For the Borel of SL_3 the root α_1 + α_2 is positive but not simple; truncating by it alone does not give A_{P,t}.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_3 and the Borel, Δ = {α_1, α_2} and A_{P,t} = {a_1/a_2 > t, a_2/a_3 > t}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §2.2, p. 8. Exactly the simple roots.

### Siegel set for a fixed maximal compact

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set` · definition.

For a ℚ-parabolic 𝐏, a maximal compact K ⊂ G = G(ℝ)^+, t > 0 and bounded (relatively compact open semialgebraic) U ⊂ N_P, W ⊂ M_P K, the Siegel set associated to 𝐏 and K is 𝔖 = U × A_{P,t} × W ⊂ G in horospherical coordinates. For a connected compact M ⊂ K, a Siegel set of G/M associated to K is the image of such a set; K is fixed once and for all (BKT Definition 2.5 as corrected by the 2023 erratum).

**Hypotheses.** as in horospherical-decomposition; K fixed.

**Planet:** Siegel set.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define from horospherical-decomposition and positive-root-coordinates; semialgebraicity from that of the coordinates.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/real-siegel-finite-cover`: finitely many Siegel sets cover Γ\G/M
- `ArithmeticQuotientDefinability`: the definable structure characterised by fixed-K Siegel charts
- `AbelianVarietiesIsogenousToNoJacobian:MZ0/minkowski-domain`: general Siegel-set reduction (request from MZ0)
- `GeometryOfNumbersAndQuadraticArithmetic:GN.4/mahler-compactness`: reduction domains

**API.**

- `RealSiegel.siegelSet` (constructor): RealSiegel.siegelSet 𝐏 K U t W : Set G.
- `RealSiegel.mem_siegelSet` (characterisation): Membership in horospherical coordinates.
- `RealSiegel.siegelSet_mono` (relation): Monotone in U, W and antitone in t.
- `RealSiegel.siegelSet_quotient` (projection): The image in G/M.

**Unit tests.**

- `RealSiegel.siegelSet_sl2` (computation): For SL_2 and K = SO(2), the image in ℍ is {x + iy : x ∈ U, y > t}.
- `RealSiegel.siegelSet_anisotropic` (degenerate): For the improper parabolic P=G the unipotent and relative-root factors are trivial and a Siegel set is the bounded W in the semisimple fixed-K scope. P=G is always allowed; anisotropy means there are no proper rational parabolics.
- `RealSiegel.siegelSet_needs_fixed_K` (non-example): Siegel sets for different K are not interchangeable: for SL_2, P upper triangular and x ≠ i in ℍ, a Siegel set B_N B_A K_x is not contained in finitely many SL_2(ℤ)-translates of Siegel sets for K_i (erratum §1.6.1).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ)/SO(2): {x + iy : |x| < 1, y > t}.
- With K varying between Siegel sets, the definable structure of BKT Theorem 1.1 is not well defined (erratum).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/positive-root-coordinates`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Definition 2.3, p. 8. The definition for one parabolic.
- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Erratum: Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/DefArithErr.pdf), §1.1, p. 1. The corrected fixed-K definition.

### Translating and conjugating Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-translation` · lemma.

For g ∈ G(ℚ), g𝔖g⁻¹ is a Siegel set associated to g𝐏g⁻¹ and gKg⁻¹. For g ∈ G, 𝔖g is a Siegel set for 𝐏 and g⁻¹Kg, and for g ∈ 𝐏(ℝ), g𝔖 is a Siegel set for 𝐏 and K (exactly, for the sets U a A_{>0} W of the erratum; for Definition 2.3's A_{P,t}, up to containment). Consequently, for γ ∈ 𝐆(ℚ)^+, γ𝔖 is contained in a Siegel set associated to γ𝐏γ⁻¹ and the same K.

**Hypotheses.** as in real-siegel-set.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Conjugation transports horospherical coordinates (horoDecomp_conj).
2. Right translation by g changes the base point; left translation by p ∈ P uses the left action formula.
3. For γ ∈ G(ℚ)^+, write γ = p k with p ∈ (γPγ⁻¹)(ℝ) relative to K and combine.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2, γ ∈ SL_2(ℤ) sends {y > t} near the cusp ∞ to a horoball at γ∞.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Lemma 2.4, p. 8. The conjugation statement.
- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Erratum: Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/DefArithErr.pdf), Remark 1.1(1), p. 2. The translation statements.

### Finitely many cusps

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finitely-many-cusps` · theorem.

For an arithmetic subgroup Γ ⊂ 𝐆(ℚ), there are only finitely many Γ-conjugacy classes of ℚ-parabolic subgroups.

**Hypotheses.** G connected semisimple over ℚ; Γ arithmetic.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Every ℚ-parabolic is 𝐆(ℚ)-conjugate to a standard one (minimal-parabolic-data).
2. 𝐆(ℚ) = ⊔_{finite} Γ g_i 𝐏(ℚ) for each standard 𝐏 (Borel 7.3: finiteness of G_ℤ\(G/P)_ℚ, via class-number-finite for the adelic form).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ) there is one cusp; for Γ_0(p), two.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/parabolic-double-cosets-finite`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(1), p. 9. Exactly the statement.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 7.3, p. 26. The finiteness behind it.

### Finitely many fixed-K Siegel sets cover

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-cover` · theorem.

Let 𝐏_1, …, 𝐏_k represent the Γ-conjugacy classes of ℚ-parabolics and K a fixed maximal compact. There are Siegel sets 𝔖_i = U_i × A_{𝐏_i,t_i} × W_i associated to 𝐏_i and the same K whose images cover Γ\G/M.

**Hypotheses.** G connected semisimple over ℚ; Γ arithmetic; K fixed, M ⊂ K compact.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Borel–Ji Proposition 2.5 with base points x_i; the erratum shows the base point can be taken equal to the fixed K for all i, using real-siegel-translation to move each 𝔖_i to K at the cost of enlarging U_i, W_i and shrinking t_i.
2. The adelic covering siegel-covering-adelic projected to the archimedean component gives the same conclusion.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ), one Siegel set {|x| ≤ 1/2, y ≥ √3/2} covers.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finitely-many-cusps`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-translation`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(1), p. 9. As printed with base points x_i.
- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Erratum: Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/DefArithErr.pdf), §1.1, p. 1. The fixed-K correction.

### Finite overlaps of Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-overlap` · theorem.

For Siegel sets 𝔖_1, 𝔖_2 associated to the same K, the set {γ ∈ Γ : γ𝔖_1 ∩ 𝔖_2 ≠ ∅} is finite; the same holds for the relatively compact closures of their unipotent and Levi factors.

**Hypotheses.** as in real-siegel-finite-cover.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the real reductive fundamental-set construction Borel–Harish-Chandra §6.5 and Borel §3.4, based on closed-orbit-lattice-finite and GL_n real overlap.
2. Transfer to the specified parabolic Siegel sets through the fixed-K convention comparison and the rational containment contract. The exact transfer is a remaining refinement, not an appeal back to adelic overlap.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ) and the standard Siegel set, finitely many γ (the identity, S, T^{±1}, ST, TS up to sign).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-translation`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-overlap`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/simultaneous-self-adjointness`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-lattice-finite`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(2), p. 9. Exactly the statement (for one fixed K).

### Inequivalent cusps separate

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/cusp-separation` · theorem.

If 𝐏_1 and 𝐏_2 are not Γ-conjugate, then for fixed U_i, W_i and all sufficiently large t_1, t_2, γ𝔖_1 ∩ 𝔖_2 = ∅ for every γ ∈ Γ.

**Hypotheses.** as in real-siegel-finite-cover.

**Construction or proof.**

1. By real-siegel-finite-overlap only finitely many γ are relevant; for each, γ𝐏_1γ⁻¹ ≠ 𝐏_2, and deep parts of Siegel sets for different parabolics are disjoint (deep-distinct-parabolics).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For Γ_0(p), the Siegel sets at the two cusps ∞ and 0 are disjoint modulo Γ_0(p) once t is large.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-overlap`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/deep-distinct-parabolics`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(3), p. 9. As printed the quantifier on γ is missing; corrected to 'for all γ ∈ Γ' (source issue).

### Deep self-intersections come from the parabolic

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/deep-cusp-stabilizer` · theorem.

For fixed U, W and sufficiently large t, a Siegel set 𝔖 for 𝐏 and K satisfies γ𝔖 ∩ 𝔖 = ∅ for every γ ∈ Γ ∖ Γ_𝐏, where Γ_𝐏 = Γ ∩ 𝐏(ℚ).

**Hypotheses.** as in real-siegel-finite-cover.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Finitely many candidates γ by real-siegel-finite-overlap; for γ ∉ 𝐏, γ𝐏γ⁻¹ ≠ 𝐏 and deep-distinct-parabolics applies to γ𝔖 (a Siegel set for γ𝐏γ⁻¹, real-siegel-translation).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ) and t > 1, only the translations ±T^n map the horoball {y > t} to itself.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-overlap`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-translation`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/deep-distinct-parabolics`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(4), p. 9. Exactly the statement.

### Deep Siegel sets of distinct parabolics are disjoint

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/deep-distinct-parabolics` · theorem.

For distinct ℚ-parabolics 𝐏_1 ≠ 𝐏_2 and fixed bounded U_i, W_i (one K), the Siegel sets 𝔖_1, 𝔖_2 are disjoint once t_1, t_2 are sufficiently large.

**Hypotheses.** as in real-siegel-finite-cover.

**Construction or proof.**

1. In horospherical coordinates of 𝐏_1, points of 𝔖_2 have A_{𝐏_1}-coordinate bounded in the direction of some simple root (Borel–Ji 2.5(5), using the relative Bruhat decomposition of 𝐏_1 and 𝐏_2).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2, the horoballs at two distinct rational cusps are disjoint for large t.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Proposition 2.7(5), p. 9. Exactly the statement.

### Comparison of Siegel-set conventions

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-convention-comparison` · theorem.

Fix K. Every Siegel set U × A_{P,t} × W associated to 𝐏 and K is contained in a Siegel set Ω A_{t′} K in Orr's sense for a Siegel triple (𝐏_0, 𝐒_0, K) with 𝐏_0 ⊂ 𝐏 a minimal ℚ-parabolic, and conversely; every Siegel set for K lies in a 𝐆(ℚ)-translate of one for K and a fixed minimal ℚ-parabolic.

**Hypotheses.** as in real-siegel-set.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Write m ∈ W as n′a′m′k′ in M_P relative to 𝐏_0 ∩ 𝐋_P; A_P is central in L_P, so u a m k = (u n′)(a a′) m′ (k′ k), and the simple roots of 𝐏_0 are bounded below on a a′ because a′ ranges in a compact set.
2. The last statement: all minimal ℚ-parabolics are 𝐆(ℚ)-conjugate (minimal-parabolic-data) and real-siegel-translation.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2 the two conventions coincide.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-translation`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §2.2, p. 8. The two conventions (Borel–Ji and Borel/Orr); the bridge is recorded in the reviewed extraction PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20.

### Containment of subgroup Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orr-schnell-containment` · theorem.

Let 𝐇 ⊂ 𝐆 be reductive ℚ-groups, (𝐏_H, 𝐒_H, K_H) a Siegel triple for 𝐇 and 𝔖_H = Ω A_t K_H a Siegel set. Let K_G ⊂ 𝐆(ℝ) be maximal compact with K_H ⊂ K_G and whose Cartan involution stabilises 𝐒_H. Then there are a Siegel triple (𝐏_G, 𝐒_G, K_G), a Siegel set 𝔖_G for it and a finite C ⊂ 𝐆(ℚ) with 𝔖_H ⊂ C·𝔖_G; moreover R_u(𝐏_H) ⊂ R_u(𝐏_G) and 𝐒_H = 𝐒_G ∩ 𝐇.

**Hypotheses.** 𝐇 ⊂ 𝐆 reductive over ℚ; K_H ⊂ K_G; θ_{K_G} stabilises 𝐒_H.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For h=μak choose the admissible w putting a in wA_{G,t′}w⁻¹.
2. Write w′_Q⁻¹μ=νw_K⁻¹bλ using containment-compact-factors. Since λ∈Z_G(S_H), it commutes with a.
3. The finitely many compact b-factors have all simple-root values bounded below by a common s>0; hence w_K⁻¹ba w_K∈A_{G,t′s}. Thus h∈w′_Q Ω_G A_{G,t′s}K_G. Take the finite rational set of w′_Q, as Orr §4.6 Proposition 4.14, with the 2023 correction.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For 𝐇 = 𝐆 it is trivial with C = {1}.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-finite-root-cones`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-weyl-representatives`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-compact-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-convention-comparison`.

**Sources.**

- [Martin Orr and Christian Schnell, *Correction to the article Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://msp.org/ant/2023/17-6/ant-v17-n6-p04-s.pdf), Theorem 1, pp. 1231–1232; §§A,E. The fixed-K theorem requires Cartan stability of S_H.
- [Martin Orr, *Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1609.01315v2), §4.6, Proposition 4.14, pp. 22–23. The compact-factor calculation completes the finite rational containment.

### Stability of the subgroup under the Cartan involution suffices

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/cartan-subgroup-criterion` · lemma.

In the setting of orr-schnell-containment with K_H ⊂ K_G: if the Cartan involution Θ of 𝐆 for K_G stabilises 𝐇, then Θ|_𝐇 is the Cartan involution of 𝐇 for K_H and Θ stabilises the torus 𝐒_H of every Siegel triple, so the theorem applies. The converse fails for 𝐆 = SL_2, 𝐇 = {(a, db; b, a) : a² − db² = 1} with d a positive non-square rational, K_G = SO_2(ℝ), 𝐒_H = {1}.

**Hypotheses.** as in orr-schnell-containment.

**Construction or proof.**

1. A Cartan involution of 𝐆 stabilising 𝐇 restricts to one of 𝐇 (uniqueness of Cartan involutions up to conjugacy fixing K_H).
2. Counterexample: 𝐇(ℝ) is a split torus not stable under transpose-inverse, but 𝐒_H = {1} is.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The counterexample: transpose-inverse sends (a, db; b, a) to (a, −b; −db, a), not in 𝐇 for d ≠ 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orr-schnell-containment`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Erratum: Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/DefArithErr.pdf), Proof of Theorem 1.2, p. 3. The sufficient condition; the counterexample to the converse is Orr–Schnell Remark 2 as recorded in the reviewed extraction.

### Intersecting Siegel sets with a subgroup

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/rational-siegel-pullback` · theorem.

Let 𝐇 ⊂ 𝐆 be reductive over ℚ, K_H = K_G ∩ 𝐇(ℝ) maximal compact, and assume every K_H-Siegel set lies in finitely many 𝐆(ℚ)-translates of a K_G-Siegel set (the conclusion of orr-schnell-containment). Then for every K_G-Siegel set 𝔖_G there are a K_H-Siegel set 𝔖_H and a finite F ⊂ 𝐇(ℚ) with 𝔖_G ∩ 𝐇(ℝ) ⊂ F 𝔖_H.

**Hypotheses.** as stated, with the forward-containment hypothesis.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose arithmetic groups Γ_H⊂Γ_G compatible with the rational embedding and a finite Siegel cover H(ℝ)=Γ_H C_HΣ_H.
2. The containment hypothesis gives Σ_H⊂AΣ_G with A⊂G(ℚ) finite. Any γ_H C_HΣ_H meeting the given Σ_G determines a Γ_G-overlap between two fixed rational translates of Σ_G.
3. The real Siegel property bounds these overlap elements by a finite set. Retain their H-rational factors to obtain the desired finite H(ℚ)-translates of Σ_H. This is Proposition 28.1, rather than a conclusion of Borel–Harish-Chandra 7.5.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For 𝐇 = 𝐆 it is trivial.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orr-schnell-containment`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-cover`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-overlap`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Thomas W. Grimm, Christian Schnell and Jacob Tsimerman, *Finiteness for self-dual classes in integral variations of Hodge structure*](https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/finiteness.pdf), §28, Proposition 28.1, pp. 14–15. This supplies the rational finite-translate preimage statement.
- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Erratum: Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/DefArithErr.pdf), §1.5, pp. 2–3. The erratum replaces the older real-only citation.

### Compact inclusion alone does not give Siegel containment

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/incompatible-morphism-obstruction` · theorem.

There are inclusions of reductive (even semisimple) ℚ-groups 𝐇 ⊂ 𝐆 with K_H ⊂ K_G for which some 𝐇-Siegel set is not covered by finitely many 𝐆(ℚ)-translates of K_G-Siegel sets; the Cartan compatibility hypothesis of orr-schnell-containment cannot be removed.

**Hypotheses.** The groups and maps are the concrete ones specified in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the explicit split torus H={[[x,x⁻¹−x],[0,x⁻¹]]}⊂SL₂ and K_G=SO₂. Its orbit in the upper half-plane is (1−y)+iy for y>0.
2. A rational translate of a standard Siegel strip is bounded above in imaginary part unless the rational matrix is upper triangular; upper-triangular translates stay in a vertical strip of bounded width. No finite family covers this ray.
3. For the semisimple example use H=ηSO(J)η⁻¹⊂SL₃ from Orr–Schnell §C. The noncompact-intersection root lemma forces the relevant rational translate into the upper parabolic; unipotent boundedness would then imply ηᵀη diagonal, contrary to its displayed matrix. The complete root-intersection lemma is recorded as a refinement.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Any definable-morphism interface built on Siegel sets must carry the Cartan compatibility hypothesis.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orr-schnell-containment`.

**Sources.**

- [Martin Orr and Christian Schnell, *Correction to the article Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://msp.org/ant/2023/17-6/ant-v17-n6-p04-s.pdf), §§B–C, pp. 1232–1234. Both counterexamples have K_H⊂K_G but lack the required Cartan compatibility.

### Preimages of Siegel sets under orbit maps

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orbit-map-siegel-preimage` · theorem.

Let 𝐇 ⊂ SL(V) be reductive over ℚ, x_0 ∈ X = SL(V_ℝ)/SO(b_0) with K_H = Stab_{𝐇(ℝ)}(x_0) and the Cartan involution of x_0 stabilising Lie 𝐇, and ι : 𝐇(ℝ)/K_H → X the orbit map. For every Siegel set 𝔖 ⊂ X, ι⁻¹(𝔖) is contained in finitely many Siegel sets of 𝐇(ℝ)/K_H associated to K_H.

**Hypotheses.** as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. ι⁻¹(𝔖_SL x_0) = (𝔖_SL K_SL ∩ 𝐇(ℝ)) x_0; apply rational-siegel-pullback, whose forward-containment hypothesis is orr-schnell-containment with cartan-subgroup-criterion.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For 𝐇 = SL(V) it is trivial.
- The Hodge-theoretic specialization (period domains D and the map to positive forms) belongs to DegeneratingHodgeStructures.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/rational-siegel-pullback`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orr-schnell-containment`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/cartan-subgroup-criterion`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. Siegel sets of the space X of positive forms; the pullback statement is the extraction's item siegel-preimage-under-hodge-form-map, stated here for a general orbit map.

### Images of Siegel sets under orbit maps

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orbit-map-siegel-image` · theorem.

In the setting of orbit-map-siegel-preimage, every Siegel set of 𝐇(ℝ)/K_H is mapped by ι into finitely many Siegel sets of X.

**Hypotheses.** as in orbit-map-siegel-preimage.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply orr-schnell-containment to 𝐇 ⊂ SL(V); Siegel sets of X do not depend on the choice of maximal compact up to rational translates, and c𝔖 for c ∈ SL(V_ℚ) is a Siegel set (real-siegel-translation).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For 𝐇 = SO(Q) of an indefinite form, Siegel sets of the symmetric space map into finitely many Siegel sets of positive forms.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/orr-schnell-containment`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-translation`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. The ambient Siegel sets; the image statement is the extraction's item siegel-image-under-hodge-form-map, stated for a general orbit map.

### Reduced positive forms

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form` · definition.

Given an ordered basis e = (e_i) of V_ℚ (integral bases of V_ℤ in BKT), C > 0 and a positive definite symmetric form b on V_ℝ, b is (e, C)-reduced if (1) |b(e_i, e_j)| < C b(e_i, e_i) for all i, j; (2) b(e_i, e_i) < C b(e_j, e_j) for i < j; (3) ∏_i b(e_i, e_i) < C det(b), the Gram determinant in e.

**Hypotheses.** V a finite-dimensional ℚ-vector space; e an ordered basis; C > 0.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define as a predicate on Gram matrices.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/reduction-siegel-dictionary`: reduced forms versus Siegel sets
- `AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`: Hermite–Minkowski reduction for GL_n
- `AbelianVarietiesIsogenousToNoJacobian:MZ0/minkowski-domain`: Minkowski-reduced domains

**API.**

- `RealSiegel.IsReduced` (constructor): RealSiegel.IsReduced e C b : Prop for a positive definite matrix b in the basis e.
- `RealSiegel.IsReduced.mono` (relation): IsReduced e C b → C ≤ C′ → IsReduced e C′ b.
- `RealSiegel.IsReduced.smul` (relation): IsReduced e C b ↔ IsReduced e C (λ • b) for λ > 0.
- `RealSiegel.IsReduced.cholesky` (characterisation): For fixed dimension n and C>0 there is a constant R(n,C)>0 such that every C-reduced positive Gram matrix b=Nᵀdiag(d)N with N unit upper triangular and d_i>0 satisfies |N_ij|≤R for i<j and d_i≤R d_{i+1} for 0≤i<n−1. The bound is uniform over b.
- `RealSiegel.IsReduced.of_cholesky_bounds` (characterisation): For fixed n and R>0 there is C(n,R)>1 such that every positive Gram matrix b=Nᵀdiag(d)N with N unit upper triangular, positive pivots d_i, |N_ij|≤R above the diagonal and d_i≤R d_{i+1} for i<n−1 is C-reduced.

**Unit tests.**

- `RealSiegel.IsReduced_identity` (computation): The identity matrix of size n is (std, 2)-reduced.
- `RealSiegel.IsReduced_dim_one` (degenerate): In dimension 1 every positive form is (e, C)-reduced for C > 1.
- `RealSiegel.IsReduced_not_ordered` (non-example): diag(4, 1) is not (std, 2)-reduced (condition (2) fails) although diag(1, 4) is.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The identity form is (e, 2)-reduced for the standard basis.
- Rescaling e_i by integers changes C by bounded factors.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.cholesky`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), Definition 4.11, p. 17. Exactly the definition (extended to rational bases).

### The set T_{e,C} of reduced forms

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form-set` · construction.

T_{e,C} = {b ∈ X : b is (e, C)-reduced} ⊂ X, the space of positive definite forms on V_ℝ; it is semialgebraic and satisfies T_{ge,C} = g·T_{e,C} for g ∈ GL(V_ℚ).

**Hypotheses.** as in reduced-form.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Defined by finitely many polynomial inequalities in the Gram entries.
2. Change of basis transports the inequalities.

**Uses.**

- `AA.3/reduction-siegel-dictionary`: comparison with Siegel sets
- `AA.3/basis-change-reducedness`: covering by finitely many T_{σe,C″}

**API.**

- `RealSiegel.reducedSet` (constructor): RealSiegel.reducedSet e C : Set (PosDefMatrix n).
- `RealSiegel.reducedSet_mono` (relation): Monotone in C.
- `RealSiegel.reducedSet_smul_basis` (functoriality): reducedSet (g • e) C = g • reducedSet e C.

**Unit tests.**

- `RealSiegel.reducedSet_contains_one` (computation): 1 ∈ reducedSet std 2.
- `RealSiegel.reducedSet_dim_one` (degenerate): For n = 1 and C > 1, reducedSet = all positive reals.
- `RealSiegel.reducedSet_not_closed_under_inverse` (non-example): diag(1, 4) ∈ reducedSet std 2 but its inverse diag(1, 1/4) is not.

**Acceptance checks.**

- T_{e,C} increases with C and exhausts X as C → ∞.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form`, `tauceti:TauCeti.cholesky_mul_transpose`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. Exactly the set.

### Reduced forms and Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduction-siegel-dictionary` · theorem.

For an ordered integral basis e and C>0, the set of (e,C)-reduced positive definite forms lies in the image of a GL_n(ℝ) Siegel set in GL_n(ℝ)/O(n), and each such Siegel set has reduced-form image for some C. Restricting to Gram determinant one gives the corresponding statement for SL_n(ℝ)/SO(n); normalize an arbitrary form B by det(B)^−1/n B. Rational bases are handled by the matching rational change of basis.

**Hypotheses.** V_ℤ a lattice in V_ℚ.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Write B=NᵀDN by the pinned Cholesky decomposition; positive definiteness gives positive diagonal pivots.
2. The diagonal-product/determinant inequality bounds the loss between pivots and diagonal entries. The off-diagonal inequalities then bound N, and the ordered diagonal inequalities bound successive pivot ratios.
3. Conversely bounded N and the chamber ratios imply the three reducedness inequalities with a uniform constant. Scalar normalization leaves all three conditions unchanged, and imposes determinant one. The precise pivot estimates remain a refinement.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For n = 2, reduced forms correspond to the standard Siegel set {|x| ≤ c, y ≥ t} in ℍ.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-set`, `tauceti:TauCeti.cholesky`, `tauceti:TauCeti.cholesky_mul_transpose`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form-scalar-invariance`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. Exactly the statement.

### Lower bound by diagonal entries

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gram-diagonal-lower-bound` · lemma.

Let B be a positive definite real symmetric n × n matrix with diagonal d_k = B_kk and D ≥ 1 with ∏_k d_k ≤ D det B. Then for every real vector a and every k, aᵀ B a ≥ a_k² d_k / D.

**Hypotheses.** B positive definite; ∏ d_k ≤ D det B.

**Construction or proof.**

1. By Cramer's rule and Hadamard's inequality applied to the minor, (B⁻¹)_kk ≤ ∏_{j≠k} d_j / det B ≤ D / d_k.
2. Cauchy–Schwarz for the inner product B: a_k² = ⟨a, B⁻¹e_k⟩_B² ≤ (aᵀBa)(B⁻¹)_kk.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For B diagonal and D = 1 it is the trivial bound.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.cholesky_mul_transpose`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. The estimate needed to justify the basis-change sentence; supplied in the reviewed extraction.

### Transferring off-diagonal bounds to another basis

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gram-offdiagonal-transfer` · lemma.

Let B be the Gram matrix in an ordered basis e′ of a positive definite form b with |B_ab| ≤ C′ d_a, d_a ≤ C′ d_b for a < b and ∏ d_a ≤ C′ det B (C′ ≥ 1). For a fixed basis e_i = ∑_a A_ai e′_a put k_i = max{a : A_ai ≠ 0}, m_i = |A_{k_i,i}|, L_i = ∑_a |A_ai|. Then |b(e_i, e_j)| ≤ C′³ L_i L_j m_i⁻² b(e_i, e_i) for all i, j.

**Hypotheses.** as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Expand b(e_i,e_j). For every contributing a≤k_i, |B_ab|≤C′d_a≤C′²d_{k_i}, using the ordering for a<k_i and C′≥1. Thus |b(e_i,e_j)|≤C′²L_iL_jd_{k_i}.
2. Lower-bound b(e_i, e_i) ≥ m_i² d_{k_i}/C′ by gram-diagonal-lower-bound.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For A the identity it reduces to condition (1).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gram-diagonal-lower-bound`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. The off-diagonal half of the basis-change claim.

### Basis change with determinant control

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/basis-change-reducedness` · theorem.

Let e, e′ be bases of V_ℚ (m = dim V) and C, C′ ≥ 1. If b is (e′, C′)-reduced and ∏_i b(e_i, e_i) ≤ C det(b in e), then b is (σe, C″)-reduced for the ordering σ of e that sorts the values b(e_i, e_i), with C″ depending only on C, C′ and the change-of-basis matrix; hence b lies in one of the m! sets T_{σe,C″}. The printed statement with the fixed ordering e is false.

**Hypotheses.** as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Off-diagonal condition (1) from gram-offdiagonal-transfer; (3) is the hypothesis; (2) holds after sorting.
2. Counterexample to the fixed-order version: e′ = (e_1, e_2) standard, e = (e_2, e_1), b = diag(1, 1000).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For e = e′ the statement is trivial with σ = id.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gram-offdiagonal-transfer`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gram-diagonal-lower-bound`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form-set`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §4.5, p. 18 (arXiv v2); JAMS p. 933. Printed version; corrected to allow a permutation of e (source issue).

### Reduction for GL_n(ℝ)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-reduction` · theorem.

There are C > 0 and a standard Siegel set 𝔖 ⊂ GL_n(ℝ) (with respect to O(n) and the upper triangular Borel) such that GL_n(ℝ) = GL_n(ℤ)·𝔖; equivalently every positive definite form is GL_n(ℤ)-equivalent to an (e, C)-reduced form for the standard basis e.

**Hypotheses.** n ≥ 1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Hermite–Minkowski reduction: choose successive minima vectors to build a basis in which the Gram matrix is reduced (Siegel's theorem, cited by Borel 3.3).
2. Translate reducedness into the Siegel-set conditions through reduction-siegel-dictionary.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For n = 2 this is the classical reduction of binary forms, giving ModularGroup.exists_smul_mem_fd.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduction-siegel-dictionary`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form-set`, `mathlib:ModularGroup.exists_smul_mem_fd`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §3.3, p. 15. The GL_n reduction.

### Adelic reduction for GL_n over ℚ

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-adelic-covering` · lemma.

For a standard Siegel domain 𝔖 of GL_n(ℝ), GL_n(𝔸_ℚ) = GL_n(ℚ) · (𝔖 × GL_n(ℤ̂)).

**Hypotheses.** n ≥ 1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. GL_n(𝔸_f) = GL_n(ℚ)GL_n(ℤ̂) (class number one for GL_n over ℚ, Borel 2.2).
2. Then reduce the archimedean component by gln-real-reduction, using GL_n(ℚ) ∩ GL_n(ℤ̂) = GL_n(ℤ).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For n = 1: 𝔸^× = ℚ^×·(ℝ^× × ℤ̂^×).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-reduction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-finite-class-number-one`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Lemma 4.4, p. 17. Exactly the statement.

### Reduction for reductive subgroups of GL_n

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/self-adjoint-reduction` · lemma.

Let G ⊂ GL_n be reductive over ℚ with a(G(ℝ))a⁻¹ self-adjoint for some a ∈ SL_n(ℝ). There are finitely many b_i ∈ GL_n(ℚ) such that ⋃_i (a⁻¹𝔖 GL_n(ℤ̂) b_i ∩ G(𝔸)) is a fundamental set for G(ℚ) in G(𝔸).

**Hypotheses.** G reductive over ℚ; self-adjoint embedding.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Realize G as the stabilizer of a rational point w with closed GL_n-orbit. Replace its infinite component by wρ(a⁻¹), so its real stabilizer is self-adjoint.
2. The compact integral finite factor bounds denominators; closed-orbit-lattice-finite therefore gives finitely many rational orbit points meeting the GL_n adelic fundamental domain.
3. Choose rational b_i for those points and solve equality of orbit images using the stabilizer. This is Borel §4.5, pp. 17–18, and uses no general adelic orbit or class-number theorem.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = SL_n the single b = 1 suffices.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-adelic-covering`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-realization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-lattice-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-part-denominator-bound`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-overlap`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 4.5, p. 17. Exactly the setting.

### Rational points on closed orbits

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-finiteness` · theorem.

Let G be reductive over F, H ⊂ G a reductive F-subgroup and σ : G → H\G. Then σ_𝔸(G(𝔸)) ∩ (H\G)(F) is a finite union of G(F)-orbits.

**Hypotheses.** G, H reductive.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Realize H\G as the closed orbit of w.
2. Choose a simultaneous self-adjoint embedding for G and H. The fundamental set from self-adjoint-reduction then has the closed-orbit finite-image property by the primitive lattice and denominator lemmas.
3. Write each adelic element as a rational translate times this set. Its rational orbit image is among the finite primitive images, proving Borel §5.4 without feeding this theorem into §4.5.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = GL_n acting on quadratic forms, the forms in a genus form finitely many classes.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-realization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/self-adjoint-reduction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/simultaneous-self-adjointness`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-lattice-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-part-denominator-bound`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.4, p. 20. Exactly the statement.

### Siegel sets in G(𝔸)^1 have finite measure

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-set-finite-measure` · lemma.

For an adelic Siegel set 𝔖 = 𝔖(T₁, ω), the measure of 𝔖 ∩ G(𝔸)^1 is finite.

**Hypotheses.** G connected reductive; ω compact.

**Construction or proof.**

1. By the Iwasawa integration formula, the measure of 𝔖 ∩ G(𝔸)^1 is at most vol(ω) vol(K) ∫ e^{−2ρ_0(H_0(a))} da over {a ∈ A_0(ℝ)^0 ∩ G(𝔸)^1 : β(H_0(a) − T₁) > 0}.
2. On the cone shifted by T₁, 2ρ_0 is a positive combination of the simple roots, so the integral of the exponential converges (a product of one-dimensional integrals of e^{−c t} over t > t₀).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ, the classical Siegel set {|x| ≤ 1/2, y > t} has hyperbolic area ∫∫ dx dy/y² < ∞.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-siegel-set`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/modular-function-parabolic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/relative-chamber`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/positive-root-cone-integral`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §8, p. 38. Siegel sets modulo the centre and their volumes.

### Finitely many G(𝒪)-orbits on rational flags

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/parabolic-double-cosets-finite` · theorem.

For connected G and an F-parabolic P, (G/P)(F) is a finite union of orbits of an arithmetic subgroup; equivalently G(F) = ⋃_{i∈I} Γ x_i P(F) with I finite.

**Hypotheses.** G connected; P parabolic over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G/P is projective, so (G/P)(𝔸) is compact and G(𝔸) = C·P(𝔸) for a compact C (Godement, Borel 7.2).
2. Cover C by finitely many translates of an open compact subgroup and use class-number-finite for P.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ) acting on ℙ¹(ℚ) there is one orbit (one cusp).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 7.3, p. 26. Exactly the statement.

### Arithmetic quotients have finite volume

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-quotient-finite-volume` · theorem.

For connected reductive G over F and Γ = G(F) ∩ U with U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}) compact open, Γ\(G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0) has finite invariant volume.

**Hypotheses.** G connected reductive; U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. By component-decomposition, Γ\(G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0) is one of finitely many pieces of G(F)\G(𝔸)/A_G(ℝ)^0 U, which is the quotient of G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 by the compact U.
2. finite-volume gives the finiteness.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ) has finite volume.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.6, p. 21. Passage between the adelic and the arithmetic quotient.

### Division algebras have no nontrivial unipotent units

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/division-algebra-no-unipotent` · lemma.

If D is a division algebra over a field of characteristic 0, then D^× contains no unipotent element other than 1.

**Hypotheses.** D a division algebra.

**Construction or proof.**

1. If u is unipotent then (u − 1)^m = 0 for some m; in a division algebra this forces u − 1 = 0.

**Acceptance checks.**

- In Hamilton's quaternions over ℚ every unit other than 1 has a non-nilpotent u − 1.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:QuaternionAlgebra`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §3, p. 33. Exactly the argument.

### Arithmetic quotients of anisotropic groups are compact

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-quotient-compact` · theorem.

In the setting of arithmetic-quotient-finite-volume, Γ\(G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0) is compact iff G^der is F-anisotropic; for an anisotropic inner form SL_1(D) of a central division algebra D, H(F)\H(𝔸_F) and Γ\H(F_∞) are compact.

**Hypotheses.** G connected reductive.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Same reduction as arithmetic-quotient-finite-volume, now with compactness-anisotropic and compactness-isotropic.
2. SL_1(D) for a division algebra D has no nontrivial unipotent rational points (division-algebra-no-unipotent: a unipotent u ≠ 1 gives the nilpotent u − 1 ≠ 0 in D).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For D definite quaternion over ℚ, Γ is finite modulo the centre and Γ\D^×(ℝ)/ℝ_{>0} is compact.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-quotient-finite-volume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-isotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/division-algebra-no-unipotent`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Theorem 5.6(ii), p. 21. The compactness criterion transported to arithmetic quotients.

### Adelic Iwasawa factorization

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa-factorization` · lemma.

For connected reductive G/F, minimal-parabolic data and an admissible K, multiplication N_P(𝔸)×M_P(𝔸)^1×A_P(ℝ)^0×K→G(𝔸) is surjective and open for each standard P. At almost all finite places it restricts to the integral Iwasawa factorization; this integrality permits assembling local choices into restricted-product elements.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply the local factorization requested from RG2.3/RG2.4 and LieGroups layer 9.
2. At good places factor integral elements using the integral parabolic and K_v, so the chosen n_v,m_v remain integral almost everywhere.
3. Use the split-centre decomposition for the reductive Levi M_P. Openness is supplied by the local quotient-coordinate theorem and finite-level restricted-product charts.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/good-maximal-compact`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.3`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, pp. 21–23. The local/global factorizations under admissibility are the required inputs.

### Parabolic Haar Jacobian

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/parabolic-haar-jacobian` · lemma.

Write P=N⋊M and δ_P(m)=|det(Ad(m)|Lie N)|_𝔸. With left Haar measures dn,dm, the measure δ_P(m)^−1 dn dm in coordinates (n,m) is a left Haar measure of P(𝔸); with the convention d(p x)=Δ_P(p)^−1 dx for right Haar, Δ_P(m)=δ_P(m).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Left multiplication by m₀ sends n to m₀ n m₀^−1 and multiplies dn by δ_P(m₀).
2. The density δ_P(m₀m)^−1 cancels that factor; left N-translations preserve dn.
3. Right multiplication in these coordinates gives the stated right-Haar modular convention.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/modular-function-parabolic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, pp. 21–23. The determinant on the unipotent radical specifies the density.

### Iwasawa integration through the compact factor

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/iwasawa-integration-compact` · lemma.

For admissible K and P=N⋊M, normalize dk to mass one and choose compatible dn,dm. The functional f↦∫_K∫_M∫_N f(nmk)δ_P(m)^−1 dn dm dk on compactly supported continuous f is a positive left-G-invariant functional, hence gives Haar measure on G(𝔸). Equivalently use the compact homogeneous space (P∩K)\K, whose measure is quasi-invariant under G with the parabolic Radon–Nikodym cocycle. No invariant measure on P\G is asserted.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Factorization identifies P\G with (P∩K)\K; changes of local P-K sections act on left Haar of P by the parabolic modular cocycle.
2. The quotient density transforms by the inverse cocycle, as in the local Iwasawa integration contract. Thus the functional is invariant under left translation.
3. Glue over finitely many exceptional places and normalized integral factors; use uniqueness of Haar to fix the remaining positive scalar.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa-factorization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/parabolic-haar-jacobian`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-split`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §4, pp. 21–23. The formula uses the compact factor and parabolic density.

### GLₙ finite class number one over ℚ

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-finite-class-number-one` · lemma.

For n≥1, GL_n(𝔸_{ℚ,f})=GL_n(ℚ)GL_n(ℤ̂). The rational intersection with GL_n(ℤ̂) is GL_n(ℤ).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. For g_f define the ℤ-lattice L=ℚ^n∩g_f ℤ̂^n. A common denominator bounds it between dℤ^n and d^−1ℤ^n.
2. Choose a ℤ-basis of L using the PID property. Its rational matrix γ satisfies L⊗ℤ_p=g_pℤ_p^n for every p, so γ⁻¹g_f lies in GL_n(ℤ̂).
3. The intersection assertion follows entrywise for a matrix and its inverse.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gln-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-points-level`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §2.2, p. 10; §4.4, p. 17. This is the independent class-number-one input, before general class-number finiteness.

### Simultaneous self-adjointness

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/simultaneous-self-adjointness` · lemma.

For a finite nested chain of reductive real algebraic subgroups of GL_n, one a∈SL_n(ℝ) makes every aG_i(ℝ)a⁻¹ stable under transpose.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the Cartan involution restriction/conjugacy theorem for a nested pair from LieGroups layer 9.
2. Induct along the chain, conjugating inside the preceding subgroup so earlier invariance is retained.
3. Represent the final Cartan involution by a positive definite form and rescale its change-of-basis matrix to determinant one.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel and Harish-Chandra, *Arithmetic subgroups of algebraic groups*](https://www.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de/~wienhard/retreat10/references/borel_harishchandra_2.pdf), Theorem 1.9, p. 492. The finite-chain statement is proved by induction from §1.8.

### Reductive homogeneous spaces as closed orbits

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-realization` · lemma.

If H⊂G are reductive algebraic groups over a characteristic-zero field F, then H\G is affine and has a G-equivariant closed immersion into a finite-dimensional rational G-representation, taking the identity coset to w∈V(F) with stabilizer H. The orbit of w is closed.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the affine homogeneous-space criterion for reductive H requested from RG2.1.
2. Choose finitely many generators of its affine coordinate algebra; include them in a finite-dimensional G-stable subspace.
3. Dualize the evaluation embedding. Equivariance identifies the image as the closed orbit, and the stabilizer is H as a group scheme.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel and Harish-Chandra, *Arithmetic subgroups of algebraic groups*](https://www.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de/~wienhard/retreat10/references/borel_harishchandra_2.pdf), §3.8, p. 501; §2.4 cited there. The affine-space realization supplies the primitive representation; it is distinct from adelic orbit finiteness.

### Closed-orbit weight bounds in a real Siegel domain

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-weight-bound` · lemma.

Let GL_n act rationally on V, w have closed orbit and transpose-stable stabilizer, and Γ⊂V(ℚ) be a lattice. For a standard real Siegel domain Σ there is a compact Q⊂GL_n(ℝ) such that wΣ∩Γ⊂wQ. In particular the norms of these lattice points are uniformly bounded.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Diagonalize the rational split-torus action into finitely many weight spaces and use that a nonzero coordinate in the fixed lattice is bounded away from zero.
2. Borel–Harish-Chandra §5.3 applies the square-weight estimate to replace a Siegel-domain representative by one in the stabilizer times a compact set.
3. Transpose stability gives the second compact bound after applying the Cartan involution; bounded unipotent factors stay bounded under the allowed chamber conjugations. The precise squared-weight estimate and proper orbit map are retained as named missing inputs.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/simultaneous-self-adjointness`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-realization`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel and Harish-Chandra, *Arithmetic subgroups of algebraic groups*](https://www.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de/~wienhard/retreat10/references/borel_harishchandra_2.pdf), §5.3–5.4, pp. 504–506. The weight-bound lemma is the primitive step used by lattice finiteness.

### Closed-orbit lattice finiteness

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-lattice-finite` · lemma.

Under closed-orbit-weight-bound, wΣ∩Γ is finite. The same holds after any fixed rational translation of Σ and any rational change of lattice.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The weight-bound puts the intersection in a compact subset of V.
2. The lattice is discrete, so its intersection with that compact set is finite.
3. A rational matrix maps a rational lattice to a rational lattice; conjugate the stabilizer and use simultaneous self-adjointness where required.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-weight-bound`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel and Harish-Chandra, *Arithmetic subgroups of algebraic groups*](https://www.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de/~wienhard/retreat10/references/borel_harishchandra_2.pdf), Lemma 5.4, pp. 505–506. Discreteness completes the independent weight estimate.

### Compact finite parts give bounded denominators

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-part-denominator-bound` · lemma.

For a rational representation ρ:G→GL(V), w∈V(F) and compact C⊂G(𝔸_f), there is a fractional O_F-lattice L⊂V(F) such that wρ(C)∩V(F)⊂L. Likewise a compact C bounds denominators of all entries of g and g⁻¹ for g∈C∩GL_n(F).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Cover the compact set by finitely many restricted-product boxes.
2. Only finitely many places allow denominators, and compactness bounds each valuation below there.
3. Intersect these valuation bounds with V(F); the result is a fractional lattice.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Lemma 4.3, p. 17. The adelic lift of real finiteness uses bounded denominators.

### Real GLₙ Siegel overlap

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-overlap` · lemma.

For a standard GL_n(ℝ) Siegel domain Σ and a fixed integer d≥1, the set of γ∈GL_n(ℚ) with γ,γ⁻¹∈d^−1M_n(ℤ) and γΣ∩Σ≠∅ is finite. The same statement holds for two fixed rational translates of such domains.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the classical real Siegel property for bounded-denominator arithmetic lattices (Borel §3.3 and Borel–Harish-Chandra §4.5).
2. Conjugate fixed rational translates and enlarge the denominator bound.
3. The general-dimensional Siegel estimate is a recorded primitive input; the Mathlib SL₂ modular-domain theorem supplies only a special-case test.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-reduction`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §3.3–3.4, pp. 15–16. This is the independent real overlap input, with the inversion convention adjusted to left quotients.

### Local polynomial comparison for algebraic heights

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/local-height-polynomial` · lemma.

For closed algebraic embeddings σ,τ of an affine group G into general linear groups and dual-augmented norms, there are integers N≥1 and positive c_v, with c_v=1 at almost all finite places, such that ‖τ(g)‖_v≤c_v‖σ(g)‖_v^N. The statement includes inverse coordinates.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The coordinate algebra is generated by the σ matrix and inverse matrix entries, so each τ and inverse τ coefficient is a polynomial in them.
2. At finite places use the ultrametric maximum bound; the polynomial coefficients and integral models are units outside finitely many places.
3. At real/complex places sum the finitely many monomial bounds, respecting the square absolute value at complex places.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/hopf-spreading`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-support-conjugate`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 15. The algebraic-height convention is refined by its coordinate-ring proof.

### Properness of dual-augmented adelic height

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-height-proper` · lemma.

If r contains a closed embedding σ and σ∨, the product height in adelic-height has compact sublevel sets. At infinity it bounds both σ(g) and σ(g)⁻¹; at finite v its value is ≥1 and, when not integral in both directions, is ≥q_v. A height bound therefore allows only finitely many exceptional finite places.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The matrix and inverse-matrix bounds form a compact subset of GL_n(F_v) at each exceptional place.
2. Only the finitely many v with q_v≤R can have local norm>1 on a height-R sublevel set; at all others the point lies in the fixed integral subgroup.
3. Take the resulting finite-level compact product and intersect the closed sublevel set, using the closed embedding topology.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-topology`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/local-height-polynomial`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 15. The dual/inverse coordinates provide the properness that a faithful point homomorphism does not.

### Polynomial count of rational coordinates

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/rational-coordinate-height-count` · lemma.

For a fixed number field F and integer d≥1, the number of a∈F^d with ∏_v max(1,|a₁|_v,…,|a_d|_v)≤R is at most C R^N for R≥1, for constants C,N depending only on F,d. The absolute values are normalized for the product formula.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Clear the denominator ideal and sort vectors by the corresponding ideal norm, which is bounded by a power of R.
2. Choose ideal-class representatives and rescale by units into a bounded logarithmic fundamental domain.
3. The archimedean bounds then give a lattice box of polynomial size, while the number of possible denominator ideals is polynomially bounded. Ideal-class finiteness, the unit fundamental domain, ideal counting and lattice-point bounds are retained as exact missing inputs.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 15. The rational-height assertion needs geometry of numbers beyond discreteness.

### Exponential integrability on the relative chamber

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/positive-root-cone-integral` · lemma.

Let β₁,…,β_r be a basis of (a₀^G)* and let 2ρ=∑c_iβ_i with every c_i>0. For any T the integral of exp(−2ρ(H)) over β_i(H)>β_i(T) is finite. For r=0 the domain is the zero-dimensional point and has the chosen finite Haar mass.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply the linear coordinate isomorphism H↦(β_i(H)).
2. The integral is a positive Jacobian constant times ∏∫_{β_i(T)}^∞exp(−c_i t)dt.
3. Each factor equals exp(−c_iβ_i(T))/c_i; the empty product handles the anisotropic case.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/relative-chamber`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §8, Theorem 8.1, pp. 37–39. Root-cone integration is separated from the group-theoretic covering.

### Scalar invariance of reduced forms

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form-scalar-invariance` · lemma.

For a positive definite Gram matrix B, C>0 and a>0, B is (e,C)-reduced iff aB is (e,C)-reduced. Consequently determinant normalization preserves reducedness.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The first two inequalities scale by a; both sides of the determinant inequality scale by a^n.
2. Positive definiteness is preserved by a>0. Apply a=det(B)^−1/n.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form`.

**Sources.**

- [Benjamin Bakker, Bruno Klingler, Jacob Tsimerman, *Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.04801v2), §3, Definition 3.3 and Proposition 3.4, pp. 13–14. The cone and determinant-one slice are distinguished by scalar normalization.

### Compatible parabolic and torus for a subgroup

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-parabolic-torus` · lemma.

For H⊂G reductive over ℚ, a Siegel triple (P_H,S_H,K_H), and K_G containing K_H with Cartan involution stabilizing S_H, choose Q⊂G with Levi Z_G(S_H) and N_H⊂R_u(Q), then a minimal P_G⊂Q. Its Cartan-stable Siegel torus S_G contains S_H, satisfies S_G∩H=S_H, and N_H⊂N_G.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose an order of the finite restricted roots extending the positive roots of H; RG2.1 constructs the parabolic Q and its centralizer Levi.
2. Cartan stability of S_H implies stability of Z_G(S_H), so K_G∩Z_G(S_H) is maximal compact there.
3. Uniqueness of the Cartan-stable Siegel torus gives S_H⊂S_G. Project to P_G/N_G; maximal splitness in P_H/N_H gives equality S_G∩H=S_H.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [Martin Orr, *Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1609.01315v2), §4.2, Lemmas 4.2–4.6, pp. 15–17. Use the corrected §4.4 Cartan argument; S_H need not be ℚ-defined in its chosen real lift.
- [Martin Orr and Christian Schnell, *Correction to the article Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://msp.org/ant/2023/17-6/ant-v17-n6-p04-s.pdf), §§A,E, pp. 1232, 1236. The corrected Cartan-stability and zero-weight hypotheses are imposed.

### Finite root-cone comparison

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-finite-root-cones` · lemma.

In containment-parabolic-torus, for any t>0 there is t′>0 such that every a∈A_{H,t} belongs to wA_{G,t′}w⁻¹ for some w in the finite Weyl group of S_G satisfying N_H,N_Z⊂wN_Gw⁻¹. Restricted roots equal to zero on S_H impose no inequality.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Choose a maximal root subset Ψ with nonnegative logarithmic values at a whose union with the positive H-roots admits an order.
2. Maximality makes every root positive for this order a nonnegative linear combination of the H-simple roots and Ψ. Finitely many subsets give a uniform bound on coefficient sums, hence t′.
3. Form the corresponding parabolic and use Weyl conjugacy of minimal parabolics; its unipotent radical contains both N_H and N_Z. Roots restricting to zero have value one, as required by the correction.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-parabolic-torus`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [Martin Orr, *Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1609.01315v2), §4.3, Proposition 4.7 and Lemmas 4.8–4.9, pp. 17–20. The finite cone bound and the corrected zero restriction are explicit.
- [Martin Orr and Christian Schnell, *Correction to the article Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://msp.org/ant/2023/17-6/ant-v17-n6-p04-s.pdf), §§A,E, pp. 1232, 1236. The corrected Cartan-stability and zero-weight hypotheses are imposed.

### Rational and compact Weyl representatives

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-weyl-representatives` · lemma.

For each admissible Weyl element w in containment-finite-root-cones, choose a compact representative w_K∈K_G and a representative w_Q=u⁻¹w′_Qu with w′_Q∈G(ℚ), u∈N_Z(ℝ), and w′_Q⁻¹w_Q∈N_G(ℝ). Their quotient can be chosen in the identity component of Z_G(S_G)(ℝ).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Conjugate S_G by u to a rational maximal split torus; use rational relative-Weyl representatives requested from RG2.1.
2. Choose a Cartan-stable maximal real split torus containing S_G. Real Weyl representatives lie in K_G by the LieGroups contract.
3. Admissibility implies u and w_Q⁻¹u⁻¹w_Q lie in N_G; their product is w′_Q⁻¹w_Q. Adjust the compact representative by the centralizer compact subgroup to match components.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-finite-root-cones`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions`.

**Sources.**

- [Martin Orr, *Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1609.01315v2), §4.4, Lemmas 4.10–4.11, pp. 20–21. The real split torus is explicitly Cartan-stable.
- [Martin Orr and Christian Schnell, *Correction to the article Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://msp.org/ant/2023/17-6/ant-v17-n6-p04-s.pdf), §§A,E, pp. 1232, 1236. The corrected Cartan-stability and zero-weight hypotheses are imposed.

### Uniform compact factors for subgroup Siegel sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-compact-factors` · lemma.

With Ω_H⊂N_HM_H compact, choose a compact Ω_G⊂N_GM_G and, for every admissible w, a compact B_w⊂S_G(ℝ)^0 such that w′_Q⁻¹Ω_H⊂Ω_G w_K⁻¹ B_w K_Z. All these choices range over a finite Weyl set.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the unipotent/Levi product homeomorphism to factor Ω_H into compact subsets of N_H and M_H.
2. Apply Iwasawa in Z_G(S_H) to the latter compact set; then Langlands coordinates in its minimal parabolic give compact N_Z,M_G,S_G factors.
3. Conjugate by the representatives from containment-weyl-representatives. The unipotent inclusion and normalization of M_G put the finite union in N_GM_G.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/containment-weyl-representatives`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`.

**Sources.**

- [Martin Orr, *Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1609.01315v2), §4.5, Lemmas 4.12–4.13, pp. 21–22. Compact coordinates are assembled once over the finite set.
- [Martin Orr and Christian Schnell, *Correction to the article Height bounds and the Siegel property*](https://msp.org/ant/2023/17-6/ant-v17-n6-p04-s.pdf), §§A,E, pp. 1232, 1236. The corrected Cartan-stability and zero-weight hypotheses are imposed.

## AA.4 — Approximation, neatness and level maps

Strong approximation separates lattice-projection finite covolume, arithmetic closure in native local Lie algebras, openness of a finite product, and elimination of finite index. The ℚ single-prime argument does not supply the number-field or anisotropic inputs. Neatness uses algebraic coordinate representations, a stable p-adic lattice, root-of-unity distances and eigenvalue bounds. Level quotients carry canonical quotient topology: compact split-centre kernels give finite rational stabilizers and proper action; neatness then gives freeness and coverings. Full and effective stabilizer masses have different central factors. Abelianization and central covers require integral lifts almost everywhere before their local images assemble adelically. Compact residual limits use actual reduced-norm topology and compact-abelian Fourier uniqueness.

At neat level the Hecke degree is the stated subgroup index. A Cartesian change of level needs U′L=U. The canonical U/U′ deck action of a normal covering need not be the full deck group on a disconnected space. Retain rational images in abelianization rather than replacing them by every rational torus point.

### Weak approximation

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-property` · definition.

An affine algebraic group G over F has weak approximation with respect to a finite set S of places if G(F) is dense in G(F_S) = ∏_{v∈S} G(F_v); it has weak approximation if this holds for every finite S.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G affine algebraic over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define with the topology of AA.1 on G(F_S) (AA.1/adelic-points-split).

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/weak-approximation-simply-connected`: the simply connected case
- `HeightsRationalPointsPartIIHomogeneousMassey`: the property (⋆) for homogeneous spaces (Harpaz–Wittenberg route)
- `AA.4/strong-approximation-sufficiency`: approximation at the places of S

**API.**

- `Approximation.HasWeakApproximation` (constructor): HasWeakApproximation G S : Prop := DenseRange (diagonal G(F) → G(F_S)).
- `Approximation.HasWeakApproximation.mono` (relation): Weak approximation for S implies it for every S′ ⊆ S.
- `Approximation.HasWeakApproximation.prod` (functoriality): Weak approximation for G and H gives it for G × H.
- `Approximation.HasWeakApproximation.of_iso` (functoriality): Invariant under isomorphisms of F-groups.

**Unit tests.**

- `Approximation.hasWeakApproximation_ga` (computation): G_a has weak approximation for every finite S (weakApproximation_denseRange).
- `Approximation.hasWeakApproximation_empty` (degenerate): Every G has weak approximation for S = ∅.
- `Approximation.not_hasWeakApproximation_mu2` (non-example): μ_2 over ℚ fails weak approximation for S = {∞, 2}: the diagonal image {(1,1), (−1,−1)} is not dense in {±1}².

**Acceptance checks.**

- G_a has weak approximation (weak approximation for F).
- μ_2 does not: {±1} is not dense in {±1}^S for |S| ≥ 2.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.weakApproximation_denseRange`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.4, p. 13. Approximation properties relative to sets of places; weak approximation is density in the finite product.

### Weak approximation for GL_n, SL_n and split tori

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-gln` · theorem.

GL_n, SL_n, G_a and split tori G_m^r over F have weak approximation.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G_a: weakApproximation_denseRange.
2. GL_n and G_m^r: GL_n(F_S) is open in M_n(F_S) and M_n(F) is dense, so GL_n(F) = M_n(F) ∩ GL_n(F_S) is dense.
3. SL_n: SL_n(F_v) is generated by elementary matrices e_{ij}(t), each root subgroup ≅ G_a has weak approximation, and products of dense subgroups' approximants approximate products.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℚ) is dense in SL_2(ℝ) × SL_2(ℚ_p).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-property`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.weakApproximation_denseRange`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gln-adelic`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-1-weak-approximation-and-multiplicative-congruences`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Lemma 1.2, proof, p. 3. Generation by elementary matrices, the mechanism used here for SL_n at finitely many places.

### Weak approximation for simply connected groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-simply-connected` · theorem.

A connected semisimple simply connected group G over a number field F has weak approximation; more generally (property (⋆)) a torsor under G that has points at all real places has an F-point and satisfies weak approximation.

**Hypotheses.** G semisimple simply connected over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Kneser–Harder–Chernousov and Platonov–Rapinchuk Theorem 7.8 (recorded gap: not decomposed).
2. The torsor statement combines hasse-principle-simply-connected with weak approximation for G.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_n and Sp_{2n} have weak approximation (elementary for SL_n: weak-approximation-gln).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-property`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hasse-principle-simply-connected`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-gln`.

**Sources.**

- [Yonatan Harpaz, Olivier Wittenberg, *Zéro-cycles sur les espaces homogènes et problème de Galois inverse*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.09605v2), Théorème 6.1, proof, p. 21. Density for homogeneous spaces with trivial stabilizer, i.e. torsors under simply connected groups.

### Torsors under an affine group over a field

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/group-torsor` · definition.

For an affine algebraic group G over a field k (Hopf algebra H), a G-torsor is a nonzero finitely generated commutative k-algebra A with a coaction A → A ⊗ H making Spec A a right G-space such that A ⊗_k k̄ ≅ H ⊗_k k̄ as comodule algebras (the isomorphism is of comodule algebras; geometric points alone are insufficient for nonreduced group schemes). It is trivial if X(k) ≠ ∅.

**Hypotheses.** k a field; G affine algebraic over k.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Comodule algebras and base change; triviality iff a k-point exists (then X ≅ G).

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/kneser-local-torsor`: local triviality
