- `AA.4/hasse-principle-simply-connected`: the Hasse principle
- `HeightsRationalPointsPartIIHomogeneousMassey`: fibres of fibrations in Harpaz–Wittenberg's method

**API.**

- `Approximation.Torsor` (structure): Structure: the algebra, the coaction, the geometric trivialization.
- `Approximation.Torsor.IsTrivial` (data): IsTrivial X : Prop := Nonempty (A →ₐ[k] k).
- `Approximation.Torsor.baseChange` (functoriality): Base change along a field extension k → k′.
- `Approximation.Torsor.trivial_iff_iso` (characterisation): X is trivial iff X is isomorphic to G acting on itself.

**Unit tests.**

- `Approximation.Torsor.self_trivial` (degenerate): G acting on itself is trivial.
- `Approximation.Torsor.mu2_sqrt` (computation): For μ_2 over ℚ and a = 2, the torsor ℚ[x]/(x² − 2) is nontrivial over ℚ: 2 is not a rational square (it becomes trivial over ℚ_7).
- `Approximation.Torsor.not_torsor_two_orbits` (non-example): G_m acting on A¹ by scaling is not a torsor: A¹(k̄) has two orbits.

**Acceptance checks.**

- G acting on itself is the trivial torsor.
- For G = μ_2 over ℚ, Spec ℚ[x]/(x² − a) is a torsor, trivial iff a is a square.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCeti.HopfAlgebra.points`.

**Sources.**

- [Yonatan Harpaz, Olivier Wittenberg, *Zéro-cycles sur les espaces homogènes et problème de Galois inverse*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.09605v2), Lemme 6.3, p. 22. Torsors under simply connected groups, the objects of the interface.

### Kneser's theorem: local triviality of torsors

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/kneser-local-torsor` · theorem.

For G connected semisimple simply connected over a nonarchimedean local field F_v of characteristic 0, every G-torsor over F_v is trivial (H¹(F_v, G) = 1).

**Hypotheses.** F_v nonarchimedean of characteristic 0; G semisimple simply connected.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Kneser's theorem (Platonov–Rapinchuk Theorem 6.4; recorded gap).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_1(B) of a quaternion division algebra B over ℚ_p: every element of ℚ_p^× is a reduced norm (Khayutin §2.3).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/group-torsor`, `tauceti:TauCeti.simplyConnectedSemisimpleCommHopfAlgProperty`.

**Sources.**

- [Yonatan Harpaz, Olivier Wittenberg, *Zéro-cycles sur les espaces homogènes et problème de Galois inverse*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.09605v2), §6.2, p. 22. Kneser's local theorem (PR Theorem 6.4).
- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §2.3, arXiv v3 pp. 15–16; Annals p. 162. The quaternion case.

### Hasse principle for simply connected groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hasse-principle-simply-connected` · theorem.

For G connected semisimple simply connected over a number field F, a G-torsor over F is trivial iff it is trivial over F_v for every real place v (H¹(F, G) → ∏_{v real} H¹(F_v, G) is bijective).

**Hypotheses.** G semisimple simply connected over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Kneser–Harder–Chernousov (Platonov–Rapinchuk Theorem 6.6; recorded gap); the nonarchimedean places contribute nothing by kneser-local-torsor.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_1(B) with B definite over ℚ: x ∈ ℚ^× is a reduced norm iff x > 0 (Hasse–Schilling–Maass).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/group-torsor`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/kneser-local-torsor`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §2.3, arXiv v3 pp. 15–16; Annals p. 162. The Hasse principle for the simply connected group B^1.
- [Yonatan Harpaz, Olivier Wittenberg, *Zéro-cycles sur les espaces homogènes et problème de Galois inverse*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.09605v2), Lemme 6.3, p. 22. Property (⋆) for torsors under simply connected groups.

### Strong approximation

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-property` · definition.

An affine algebraic group G over F has strong approximation with respect to a finite set S of places if G(F) is dense in G(𝔸_F^S), the adelic points away from S (equivalently G(F)G(F_S) is dense in G(𝔸_F)).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; S a finite set of places.

**Planet:** Strong approximation.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use AA.1/adelic-points-split to identify G(𝔸) = G(F_S) × G(𝔸^S).

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/strong-approximation-theorem`: the Kneser–Platonov theorem
- `AA.4/class-set-abelianization`: class sets of groups with simply connected derived group
- `BorelRegulators:R.6/norm-one-volume`: H(𝔸_F) = H(F)H_∞U for SL_1(D) (request from R.6)
- `ShimuraVarieties:V0`: the qualified approximation theorem (RS-04 link AA.4 → V0)

**API.**

- `Approximation.HasStrongApproximation` (constructor): HasStrongApproximation G S : Prop := DenseRange (diagonal G(F) → G(𝔸^S)).
- `Approximation.HasStrongApproximation.mul_open` (characterisation): For every open subgroup U ⊂ G(𝔸^S), G(𝔸^S) = G(F)U.
- `Approximation.HasStrongApproximation.mono` (relation): Strong approximation for S implies it for S′ ⊇ S.
- `Approximation.HasStrongApproximation.classNumber_one` (example): If it holds for S = archimedean places then G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U is a point for every compact open U.

**Unit tests.**

- `Approximation.hasStrongApproximation_ga` (compatibility): G_a over F has strong approximation for S = archimedean places (GlobalNumberFields layer 6).
- `Approximation.hasStrongApproximation_sl2_rat` (computation): SL_2 over ℚ with S = {∞}: SL_2(ℤ) → SL_2(ℤ/d) surjective for all d (Tau Ceti), equivalent to density of SL_2(ℚ) in SL_2(𝔸_f).
- `Approximation.not_hasStrongApproximation_gm` (non-example): G_m over ℚ fails for S = {∞}: ℚ^× ∩ ℤ̂^× = {±1}, so ℚ^× is discrete in 𝔸_f^× (AA.1/finite-adelic-discreteness-criterion).

**Acceptance checks.**

- G_a has strong approximation for every nonempty S (density of F in 𝔸_{F,f} for S ⊇ archimedean).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/split-finite-factors`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Theorem 2.3, p. 12. Exactly the definition.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §2.6, p. 13. The case S = archimedean places.

### Strong approximation for unipotent groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/ga-strong-approximation` · lemma.

Every unipotent group over F (in particular G_a) has strong approximation with respect to any nonempty finite S containing the archimedean places.

**Hypotheses.** N unipotent over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G_a: density of F in 𝔸_{F,f} (GlobalNumberFields layer 6).
2. Induction along a central series with G_a quotients, as in AA.3/unipotent-class-number-one.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The upper unitriangular group of GL_n has strong approximation.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-property`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/unipotent-class-number-one`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §2.6, p. 13. Strong approximation for unipotent groups.

### Tori never have strong approximation for finite S

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/torus-strong-approximation-failure` · theorem.

For a nontrivial torus T over F and a finite set S of places, T(F) is not dense in T(𝔸^S); the quotient of T(𝔸^S) by the closure of T(F) has infinite exponent. Weak approximation for tori is asserted only for split tori (weak-approximation-gln).

**Hypotheses.** T a nontrivial F-torus; S finite.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For G_m over ℚ and S = {∞}: ℚ^× ∩ ℤ̂^× = {±1}, so ℚ^× is closed and discrete in 𝔸_f^× ≠ ℚ^×.
2. General T: the m-th power isogenies and Dirichlet's unit theorem with Chebotarev produce characters of T(𝔸^S)/closure(T(F)) of every order (Rapinchuk Proposition 2.1).

**Acceptance checks.**

- G_m over F with S the archimedean places: the closure of F^× in 𝔸_f^× is F^× · (closure of 𝒪_F^×), of infinite index.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-property`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-adelic-discreteness-criterion`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Proposition 2.1, p. 9. Exactly the statement.

### Closures of Zariski-dense subgroups are open

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/zariski-dense-closure-open` · lemma.

Let G be connected absolutely almost simple over ℚ_p, and Γ⊂G(ℚ_p) a Zariski-dense nondiscrete subgroup. Its closure is open in G(ℚ_p), subject to the recorded p-adic analytic closed-subgroup input. This statement does not extend to arbitrary E/ℚ_p with E-Zariski density alone.

**Hypotheses.** G connected absolutely almost simple over ℚ_p; Γ⊂G(ℚ_p) Zariski dense and nondiscrete.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The closure Δ is a p-adic analytic subgroup of positive dimension (Cartan's closed-subgroup theorem; recorded gap).
2. Its Lie algebra is Ad Γ-invariant, hence Ad G-invariant by Zariski density, hence everything by irreducibility of the adjoint representation of an absolutely almost simple group.
3. An analytic subgroup with full Lie algebra is open.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ) ⊂ SL_2(ℤ_p), the closure is SL_2(ℤ_p).
- For a proper finite extension E/ℚ_p, SL_2(ℚ_p)⊂SL_2(E) is E-Zariski dense, closed and nondiscrete but not open. Its analytic dimension is 3 rather than 3[E:ℚ_p].

**Prerequisites:** `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Lemma 2.7, p. 16. The statement (for subgroups of G(ℤ); the nondiscreteness is automatic there).

### Borel density for S-arithmetic groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/borel-density` · theorem.

For G connected absolutely almost simple over F and S a finite set of places containing the archimedean ones with G_S noncompact, the S-arithmetic group G(𝒪_{F,S}) is infinite and Zariski dense in G.

**Hypotheses.** G absolutely almost simple over F; G_S noncompact.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G(𝒪(S)) is an S-arithmetic lattice in G_S (AA.3/s-arithmetic-lattice). A projection to a noncompact factor need not be nondiscrete; Borel density is a separate recorded input.
2. Borel's density theorem (Platonov–Rapinchuk Theorem 4.10; recorded gap).

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℤ) is Zariski dense in SL_2.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/s-arithmetic-lattice`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Theorem 2.3, remark after it, p. 12. Exactly the statement.

### Open subgroups of finite covolume have finite index

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/open-finite-covolume-finite-index` · lemma.

If Δ is an open subgroup of a locally compact group H such that H/Δ carries a nonzero finite H-invariant Radon measure, then Δ has finite index in H.

**Hypotheses.** H locally compact; Δ open; The quotient measure is nonzero, finite and Radon.

**Construction or proof.**

1. H/Δ is discrete; an invariant measure on a discrete homogeneous space gives every point the same mass, positive; finiteness forces finitely many points.

**Acceptance checks.**

- ℤ_p ⊂ ℚ_p is open but ℚ_p/ℤ_p has infinite invariant measure.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6, p. 16. Exactly the step.

### Strong approximation: sufficiency

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-sufficiency` · theorem.

Let G be connected, absolutely almost simple and simply connected over a number field F, and S a finite set of places containing the archimedean ones with G_S = ∏_{v∈S} G(F_v) noncompact. Then G has strong approximation with respect to S.

**Hypotheses.** G absolutely almost simple simply connected over F; S ⊇ archimedean places; G_S noncompact.

**Planet:** Strong approximation theorem.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply the finite-place density criterion.
2. The arithmetic finite-product openness lemma and its finite-covolume measure give a finite-index closure.
3. Arithmetic finite-index elimination gives the full product. Its native-field, independent-factor and anisotropic arithmetic inputs are explicitly recorded gaps; the single-prime proof alone is not presented as a proof of the general theorem.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_n over ℚ with S = {∞}: SL_n(ℤ) is dense in SL_n(ℤ̂), i.e. SL_n(ℤ) → SL_n(ℤ/N) is surjective.
- For SL_1(B), B definite quaternion over ℚ, S = {∞}: G_S is compact and the theorem does not apply.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-property`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-finite-places`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-finite-product-openness`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-finite-index-elimination`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Theorem 2.3, p. 12. The theorem (sufficiency half).
- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), Remark 1 after Theorem 2.3, p. 12. Where simple connectedness enters (Kneser–Tits).

### Strong approximation: necessity

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-necessity` · theorem.

In the setting of strong-approximation-sufficiency without the hypotheses: if G has strong approximation with respect to S then G_S is noncompact and G is simply connected.

**Hypotheses.** G connected absolutely almost simple over F; S finite.

**Construction or proof.**

1. If G_S is compact, G(F) is discrete in G(𝔸) and G_S compact, so G(F) is discrete in G(𝔸^S), which is not discrete; so G(F) is not dense.
2. If G is not simply connected, let π : G̃ → G be the simply connected cover with kernel a nontrivial finite group scheme; Chebotarev produces infinitely many places v with G(F_v)/π(G̃(F_v)) nontrivial, and the image of G(F) cannot be dense in the corresponding product (Rapinchuk §2.3, Proposition 2.2).

**Acceptance checks.**

- PGL_2 over ℚ fails strong approximation for S = {∞}: det modulo squares separates classes.
- SL_1(B) with B definite and S = {∞} fails: G_∞ compact.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-property`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev`, `tauceti:TauCeti.simplyConnectedSemisimpleCommHopfAlgProperty`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.3, p. 11. Both necessary conditions.

### Strong approximation for semisimple groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-theorem` · theorem.

Let G be connected semisimple simply connected over F and S ⊇ archimedean places finite such that G′(F_S) is noncompact for every F-simple factor G′ of G. Then G(𝔸_F) = G(F)·G(F_S)·U for every compact open U ⊂ G(𝔸_F^S); in particular G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U is a single point when S is the set of archimedean places.

**Hypotheses.** G semisimple simply connected; each F-simple factor noncompact at S.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G = ∏ Res_{F_i/F} G_i with G_i absolutely almost simple simply connected (restriction of scalars, AA.1/base-change-adelic).
2. Apply strong-approximation-sufficiency to each G_i over F_i with the places above S; noncompactness of Res G_i at S is noncompactness of G_i at the places above S.
3. Density gives G(𝔸^S) = G(F)U.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2 over a number field F, S archimedean: class number one for every level.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-sufficiency`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), Theorem 2.1(a), p. 12. Exactly the statement over ℚ.

### Class sets of groups with simply connected derived group

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/class-set-abelianization` · theorem.

Let G be connected reductive over F with G^der simply connected and G^der(F_∞) noncompact on each F-simple factor, ν : G → D = G/G^der. For compact open U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}), ν induces a bijection G(F)\G(𝔸_{F,f})/U ≃ ν(G(F))\D(𝔸_{F,f})/ν(U).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; G^der simply connected; G^der(F_∞) noncompact on each simple factor.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Finite adelic surjectivity of ν proves surjectivity on the displayed class sets; the rational image remains ν(G(F)), without replacing it by D(F).
2. If ν(y)=ν(γ)ν(x)ν(u), then y=γxhu with h∈G^der(𝔸_f).
3. Strong approximation for G^der applied to the conjugated compact open x(U∩G^der)x⁻¹ puts h into a rational-derived translate of U∩G^der, proving injectivity.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n: GL_n(F)\GL_n(𝔸_f)/GL_n(Ô) ≅ F^×\𝔸_f^×/Ô^× = Cl(F).
- For B^× with B definite over ℚ, G^der = SL_1(B) is compact at ∞ and the hypothesis fails; indeed class numbers of definite quaternion orders exceed the narrow class number in general.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-theorem`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/kneser-local-torsor`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/abelianization-adelic-surjective`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2.2, (21), p. 14. The reduced-norm bijection on class sets, here for any G with simply connected noncompact derived group.

### Neat elements

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-element` · definition.

An automorphism α of a finite-dimensional vector space over a subfield of ℂ is neat if its eigenvalues in ℂ generate a torsion-free subgroup of ℂ^×. An element g ∈ G(F) of a linear algebraic group over a number field F is neat if ρ(g) is neat for one faithful F-representation ρ; a subgroup of G(F) is neat if all its elements are.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field (G over ℚ via restriction of scalars); G linear algebraic; The group representation is algebraic and faithful, rather than merely injective as a map on F-rational points. In Hopf coordinates it is induced by a surjective GL_n-coordinate morphism O(GL_n)→O(G)..

**Construction or proof.**

1. First define neatness relative to a specified faithful closed algebraic representation, using its eigenvalue subgroup.
2. The theorem neat-representation-independence subsequently proves that this predicate is independent of the chosen representation; it is not a prerequisite of the definition.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/neat-level`: neat compact open levels
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0`: freeness at neat level (RT-AREA-automorphic-1/28)
- `ShimuraData:D5`: D5 imports neatness from AA.4
- `ShimuraVarieties:V0`: existence of neat congruence subgroups imported from AA.4

**API.**

- `Neat.IsNeatAut` (constructor): IsNeatAut α : Prop for α ∈ GL(V), V over a subfield of ℂ.
- `Neat.IsNeat` (constructor): IsNeat g : Prop for g ∈ G(F), via a chosen faithful representation.
- `Neat.IsNeatSubgroup` (constructor): A subgroup all of whose elements are neat.
- `Neat.IsNeat.pow` (relation): If g is neat then so is g^n.
- `Neat.IsNeat.torsion_eq_one` (characterisation): A neat element of finite order is 1.
- `Neat.algebraicPointMap` (constructor): For a GL_n coordinate bialgebra morphism r:O(GL_n)→O(G) and an embedding F→ℂ, evaluate r on F-points through Tau Ceti GeneralLinear.pointsMulEquiv and extend matrix entries to ℂ.
- `Neat.IsAlgebraicPointHom` (data): The point homomorphism is induced by such an algebraic coordinate morphism, using the specified coefficient embedding.
- `Neat.IsFaithfulAlgebraicPointHom` (data): The point homomorphism is induced by a surjective coordinate morphism, hence a closed algebraic immersion; this is stronger than injectivity on rational points.

**Unit tests.**

- `Neat.isNeat_diag` (computation): diag(2, 1/2) ∈ SL_2(ℚ) is neat.
- `Neat.isNeat_one` (degenerate): 1 is neat.
- `Neat.not_isNeat_rotation` (non-example): The order-3 element (0 −1; 1 −1) of SL_2(ℤ) is not neat; nor is a torsion-free element whose eigenvalue group contains ζ_3, such as (0 −1; 1 1)·(scalar 2) in GL_2(ℚ).

**Acceptance checks.**

- No nontrivial element of finite order is neat.
- diag(2, 1/2) ∈ SL_2(ℚ) is neat; diag(−1, −1) is not.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §3, p. 34. Exactly the definition.

### Neatness does not depend on the representation

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-representation-independence` · theorem.

If ρ(g) is neat for one faithful representation ρ of G then σ(g) is neat for every representation σ of G defined over a subfield of ℂ.

**Hypotheses.** G a finite-type linear algebraic group over the number field F; ρ is a faithful algebraic representation (a closed immersion), σ is an algebraic representation, and the compared complex point actions use the same embedding F→ℂ. Arbitrary homomorphisms of the abstract rational-point group are excluded..

**Construction or proof.**

1. By algebraic-tensor-eigenvalues the eigenvalue subgroup for σ(g) is a subgroup of that for the chosen faithful algebraic ρ(g).
2. A subgroup of a torsion-free group is torsion free. Base-field extension does not change the abstract multiplicative relations between eigenvalues.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n the determinant of a neat element is neat.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-element`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/algebraic-tensor-eigenvalues`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §3, p. 34. Exactly the argument.

### Neatness is stable under subgroups, conjugation and homomorphisms

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-stability` · lemma.

Subgroups of neat subgroups are neat; conjugates of neat subgroups by elements of G(F) are neat; and for a homomorphism φ : G → G′ of linear algebraic groups the image φ(Γ) of a neat subgroup Γ is neat.

**Hypotheses.** G, G′ linear algebraic over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Subgroups: by definition.
2. Conjugation preserves eigenvalues.
3. Homomorphisms: σ ∘ φ is a representation of G; apply neat-representation-independence.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The image of a neat subgroup of SL_2(ℚ) in PGL_2(ℚ) is neat.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-representation-independence`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §3, p. 34. Neat subgroups; stability follows from the representation independence.

### Neat groups are torsion free

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-torsion-free` · lemma.

A neat subgroup of G(F) is torsion free.

**Hypotheses.** G linear algebraic.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The eigenvalues of an element of finite order are roots of unity, so they generate a finite subgroup, which must be trivial; a diagonalizable (finite-order) element with all eigenvalues 1 is the identity.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Γ(N) ⊂ SL_2(ℤ) is torsion free for N ≥ 3.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-element`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §3, p. 34. The key observation.

### Neat compact open levels

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-level` · definition.

A compact open subgroup U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}) is neat if G(F) ∩ x U x⁻¹ is neat for every x ∈ G(𝔸_{F,f}) (convention: all rational intersections, not every element of U).

**Hypotheses.** G linear algebraic over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Define via the arithmetic groups of AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/level-covering-map`: free actions at neat level
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0`: freeness of the action at neat level
- `ShimuraData:D5`: neat levels imported from AA.4
- `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII:F4/conditional-orbit-bound`: level maps without assuming neatness

**API.**

- `Neat.IsNeatLevel` (constructor): IsNeatLevel U : Prop := ∀ x, IsNeatSubgroup (levelArithmetic x U).
- `Neat.IsNeatLevel.mono` (relation): A compact open subgroup of a neat level is neat.
- `Neat.IsNeatLevel.conj` (relation): Conjugates of neat levels are neat.
- `Neat.IsNeatLevel.torsionFree` (characterisation): All levelArithmetic x U are torsion free.

**Unit tests.**

- `Neat.isNeatLevel_U3` (computation): The arithmetic group Γ(3) = SL_2(ℤ) ∩ U(3) of the level U(3) ⊂ GL_2(ℤ̂) is neat.
- `Neat.isNeatLevel_trivial_group` (degenerate): For the trivial group every level is neat.
- `Neat.not_isNeatLevel_GL2Zhat` (non-example): GL_2(ℤ̂) is not neat: it contains −1 ∈ GL_2(ℤ).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_n over ℚ, U(N) = ker(GL_n(ℤ̂) → GL_n(ℤ/N)) is neat for N ≥ 3 (neat-criterion-one-prime).
- GL_n(ℤ̂) is not neat.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-element`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §5, p. 58. Neat levels through all the arithmetic groups Γ_g.

### A one-prime criterion for neatness

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-criterion-one-prime` · theorem.

Let ρ : G ↪ GL_n be a faithful representation over ℚ (after restriction of scalars) and U ⊂ G(𝔸_f) compact open. If for some prime p ≥ 3 the projection of ρ(U) to GL_n(ℚ_p) lies in 1 + p M_n(ℤ_p) (for p = 2, in 1 + 4M_n(ℤ_2)), then U is neat.

**Hypotheses.** ρ faithful; U compact open; p ≥ 3 (or p = 2 with level 4).

**Construction or proof.**

1. For γ ∈ G(ℚ) ∩ xUx⁻¹, ρ(γ) is conjugate in GL_n(ℚ_p) to an element of x_p ρ(U_p) x_p⁻¹, whose eigenvalues λ in ℚ̄_p satisfy |λ − 1|_p ≤ p⁻¹ (resp. 4⁻¹) because they are eigenvalues of conjugates of elements of 1 + pM_n(ℤ_p).
2. The multiplicative group {λ : |λ − 1|_p < p^{-1/(p−1)}} of ℚ̄_p contains no nontrivial root of unity, and it contains the group generated by such eigenvalues.
3. Embedding the eigenvalue group (inside ℚ̄^×) in both ℂ^× and ℚ̄_p^× gives the same abstract group, so it is torsion free.

**Acceptance checks.**

- U(3) ⊂ GL_2(ℤ̂) is neat; U(2) is not (it contains −1).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-representation-independence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/padic-ball-torsion-free`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Proposition 3.5, p. 34. Neat congruence subgroups; the p-adic eigenvalue criterion is the standard proof (Borel 17.4, not read).

### Neat levels exist

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-level-exists` · theorem.

Every compact open U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}) contains a neat normal open subgroup of finite index; every arithmetic subgroup of G(F) contains a neat subgroup of finite index defined by congruence conditions (Borel).

**Hypotheses.** G linear algebraic over F.

**Planet:** Existence of neat levels.

**Construction or proof.**

1. After restriction of scalars take a faithful algebraic ℚ-representation and one prime p≥3. The projection of U is compact and has a stable ℤ_p-lattice.
2. The inverse image of the identity modulo p on this lattice is normal and open in U, with finite index by compactness.
3. The one-prime criterion makes it neat for every rational conjugate intersection. Intersect an arithmetic subgroup with the resulting congruence level.

**Acceptance checks.**

- GL_2(ℤ̂) ⊃ U(3) neat of index #GL_2(ℤ/3) = 48.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-criterion-one-prime`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/compact-stable-padic-lattice`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Proposition 3.5, p. 34. Exactly the arithmetic-subgroup statement; the adelic level statement is derived here (RT-AREA-automorphic-1/28).

### Nested-level map on double cosets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-map` · construction.

For a group G, H ≤ G and K′ ≤ K ≤ G, π : H\G/K′ → H\G/K, [g] ↦ [g], is well defined and surjective, and for each g the map K/K′ → π⁻¹([g]), kK′ ↦ [gk], is a surjection; no normality is assumed.

**Hypotheses.** G a group; H, K′ ≤ K subgroups.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Well defined by DoubleCoset.eq.
2. Surjectivity onto the fibre: an element of the fibre is [x] with x = h g k, and [x] = [g k] at the finer level.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/double-coset-level-cardinality`: the finite-index bound
- `AA.4/level-covering-map`: the underlying map of level quotients
- `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuliPartII:F4/conditional-orbit-bound`: nested-level double-coset fibre surjection (request from F4)

**API.**

- `LevelMaps.levelMap` (constructor): levelMap H hK : DoubleCoset.Quotient H K′ → DoubleCoset.Quotient H K.
- `LevelMaps.levelMap_mk` (simp): levelMap (mk g) = mk g.
- `LevelMaps.levelMap_surjective` (characterisation): levelMap is surjective.
- `LevelMaps.fibreSurj` (data): fibreSurj g : K ⧸ K′.subgroupOf K → levelMap ⁻¹' {mk g}, kK′ ↦ mk (g k), surjective.
- `LevelMaps.levelMap_comp` (functoriality): levelMap for K″ ≤ K′ ≤ K composes.

**Unit tests.**

- `LevelMaps.levelMap_refl` (degenerate): For K′ = K, levelMap is the identity.
- `LevelMaps.levelMap_trivial_H` (computation): For H = ⊥ and finite index, each fibre has exactly [K : K′] elements.
- `LevelMaps.levelMap_fibre_not_index` (non-example): For H = G and [K : K′] = 2 there is one fine class, so the fibre size 1 is not the index 2.

**Acceptance checks.**

- K′ = K gives the identity with singleton fibres.
- H = 1: fibres are exactly K/K′.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:DoubleCoset.Quotient`, `mathlib:DoubleCoset.eq`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2, (15), p. 11. Double-coset counts at varying levels, whose comparison uses the nested-level map.

### Finite-index bound for level changes

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-cardinality` · theorem.

For K′ ≤ K of finite index N, every fibre of H\G/K′ → H\G/K has at most N elements; if H\G/K is finite of cardinality h then H\G/K′ is finite of cardinality at most Nh. No normality, freeness or neatness is assumed.

**Hypotheses.** K′ ≤ K, [K : K′] = N finite.

**Construction or proof.**

1. fibreSurj is a surjection from a set of size N.
2. The fine quotient is the disjoint union of its fibres over a finite base.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For H = 1 equality holds; for H = G and N > 1 it is strict.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-map`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2.3, p. 16. The level-index step combined with the class-number bound.

### Conjugate levels give equivalent double-coset sets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-conjugate-level` · theorem.

For H, K ≤ G and a ∈ G, [g] ↦ [ga] is a bijection H\G/(aKa⁻¹) ≃ H\G/K with inverse [x] ↦ [xa⁻¹]; a need not normalize H.

**Hypotheses.** G a group.

**Construction or proof.**

1. If g′ = h g (a k a⁻¹) then g′a = h (g a) k; the inverse similarly.

**Acceptance checks.**

- a = 1 gives the identity.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:DoubleCoset.eq`, `mathlib:DoubleCoset.Quotient`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2. Stabilizers of different lattices are conjugate levels, compared by right translation.

### Index of product subgroups with finite exceptional support

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/finite-support-product-index` · theorem.

For groups H_v with subgroups S_v ≤ H_v equal to H_v outside a finite set B and of finite index for v ∈ B, (∏ H_v)/(∏ S_v) ≃ ∏_{v∈B} H_v/S_v and [∏ H_v : ∏ S_v] = ∏_{v∈B} [H_v : S_v]; in particular for compact open product levels in G(𝔸_f), [∏ K_v : ∏ K′_v] = ∏_v [K_v : K′_v].

**Hypotheses.** S_v = H_v outside finite B.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Map a coset to its components; coordinatewise membership; lift finitely many representatives with identity elsewhere.

**Acceptance checks.**

- [GL_2(ℤ̂) : K_0(N)] = ∏_{p | N} [GL_2(ℤ_p) : K_0(p^{v_p(N)})] = N ∏_{p|N}(1 + 1/p).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2.1, (20), p. 14. The local-to-global index step.

### Level quotients

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient` · definition.

For compact open U ⊂ G(𝔸_{F,f}) and a closed subgroup K_∞ ⊂ G(F_∞) (for example a maximal compact subgroup times A_G(ℝ)^0, or trivial), the level quotient is X_U = G(F)\G(𝔸_F)/K_∞U with the quotient topology, together with the right action of G(𝔸_f) by Hecke translation X_{gUg⁻¹} ≃ X_U, [x] ↦ [xg].

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U compact open; K_∞ closed.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Quotient of G(𝔸) by the left action of G(F) and right action of K_∞U.
2. Right translation by g carries the K_∞(gUg⁻¹)-orbits to K_∞U-orbits.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/level-covering-map`: covering maps between level quotients
- `AA.4/hecke-cartesian`: Hecke correspondences
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0`: X_K for the symmetric space
- `ShimuraVarieties:V1`: level maps before complex structures (RS-04 link AA.4 → V1)

**API.**

- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient` (constructor): LevelQuotient U K∞ : Type, the double quotient with its topology.
- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient.mk` (constructor): The projection G(𝔸) → LevelQuotient U K∞.
- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient.rightTranslate` (functoriality): rightTranslate g : LevelQuotient (g U g⁻¹) K∞ ≃ₜ LevelQuotient U K∞.
- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient.mk_rational` (simp): mk (diagonal γ * x) = mk x.

**Unit tests.**

- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient.gl1_rat` (computation): For GL_1/ℚ, U = ℤ̂^× (the maximal compact open subgroup) and K∞ = ℝ^×: LevelQuotient is a point.
- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient.trivial_group` (degenerate): For the trivial group it is a point.
- `LevelMaps.LevelQuotient.not_finite_adelic_only` (non-example): For SL_2/ℚ with K∞ = 1, LevelQuotient is SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ) (strong approximation), not the one-point set SL_2(ℚ)\SL_2(𝔸_f)/U.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For K_∞ = 1, X_U ≃ ⊔ Γ_i\G(F_∞) (AA.3/component-decomposition).
- For GL_2/ℚ and principal K(N), K∞=ℝ^×SO(2) gives φ(N) copies of Γ(N)\ℍ. With K∞=ℝ^×O(2), the negative-determinant involution identifies the pair of half-plane components, giving determinant classes (ℤ/N)^×/{±1}, hence φ(N)/2 components for N≥3.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §5, footnote 40, p. 57. Level quotients G(F)\G(𝔸)/K_∞K.

### Covering maps between neat levels

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-covering-map` · theorem.

Let U′⊂U be compact open with U neat, and K∞ containing A_G(ℝ)^0 and compact modulo it. Then X_{U′}→X_U is a finite covering of degree [U:U′]. If U′ is normal in U, the canonical U/U′ action is free and transitive on each fibre, so this is a principal U/U′ covering. The rational stabilizer is trivial in this scope. Equality of U/U′ with the full deck-transformation group additionally requires the usual connectedness hypotheses (in particular connected total space).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U neat; U′ ⊂ U compact open; K∞ contains A_G(ℝ)^0 and is compact modulo it.

**Planet:** Level covering maps.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The fibre over [g] is the orbit set of the finite stabilizer group G(F) ∩ g K_∞U g⁻¹ acting on the cosets of U′ in U (double-coset-level-map with H the stabilizer).
2. Under the compact-modulo-A_G hypotheses the full rational stabilizer is finite; neatness makes it torsion free and therefore trivial.
3. For normal U′ the finite U/U′ action is free and properly discontinuous. Identify its orbit quotient with X_U with the actual quotient topology and apply the pinned quotient-covering theorem; use the Tau Ceti intermediate-cover result for nonnormal U′.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2/ℚ with K_∞ = ℝ^×O(2), U = U(3) and U′ = U(9), the covering has degree [U(3) : U(9)] = 3⁴ = 81; the central correction is trivial because −1 ∉ U(3).
- Without neatness the map SL_2(ℤ)\ℍ ← Γ(2)\ℍ is not a covering map: it ramifies over the elliptic points i and ρ.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-torsion-free`, `mathlib:isQuotientCoveringMap_quotientMk_of_properlyDiscontinuousSMul`, `tauceti:IsQuotientCoveringMap.isCoveringMap_of_comp`, `mathlib:ProperlyDiscontinuousSMul`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-action-free-at-neat`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient-hausdorff`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §5, p. 58. Level maps at small (neat) level.

### Fibre mass of a level map with stabilizers

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-map-fibre-mass` · theorem.

Without neatness, for U′ ⊂ U and x ∈ X_U with finite stabilizer group Γ_x = (G(F) ∩ g K_∞U g⁻¹)/(Z(F) ∩ K_∞U), the fibre of X_{U′} → X_U over x satisfies ∑_{y ↦ x} 1/|Γ_y| = [U : U′]/(|Γ_x|·[Z(F) ∩ K_∞U : Z(F) ∩ K_∞U′]).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U′ ⊂ U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Apply level-full-stabilizer-mass.
2. Put Z_U=Z(F)∩K∞U and Z_U′=Z(F)∩K∞U′. These finite groups are the central kernels at every point of the respective level.
3. Since |Γ_x|=|A_x|/|Z_U| and |Γ_y|=|A_y|/|Z_U′|, substitute into the full-stabilizer identity to obtain the displayed factor [Z_U:Z_U′].

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2(ℤ)\ℍ ← Γ_0(2)\ℍ over the elliptic point i (stabilizer of order 2 mod ±1) the masses add to 3/2 = 3/|Γ_i|.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-cardinality`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-full-stabilizer-mass`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-stabilizer-finite`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2. Stabilizer-sensitive counts at non-neat level.

### Quotient groupoids at non-neat level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient-groupoid` · construction.

For compact open U and closed K∞, 𝒳_U is the action groupoid of G(F) on G(𝔸)/K∞U, with automorphism group G(F)∩xK∞Ux⁻¹ and isomorphism classes X_U. If K∞ contains A_G(ℝ)^0 and is compact modulo it, these automorphism groups are finite; at neat U they are trivial. For an arbitrary closed K∞ they need not be finite (real quadratic unit stabilizers when K∞ is the full archimedean centre).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Construct the action category for the diagonal G(F)-action on the canonical right homogeneous quotient.
2. Its automorphisms are exactly the full rational stabilizer A_x.
3. The finite-stabilizer lemma applies under the stated compact-modulo-A_G hypotheses; neatness then makes this finite group trivial.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/level-map-fibre-mass`: masses 1/|Aut|
- `AlgebraicModuliForArithmeticGeometry`: stack-theoretic quotients [Γ\X] (stack language imported from that roadmap)
- `AA.5/definite-quaternion-mass`: Eichler mass formula

**API.**

- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid` (constructor): The action groupoid of G(F) on G(𝔸)/K∞U.
- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid_aut` (characterisation): Aut of the object x is G(F) ∩ x K∞U x⁻¹.
- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid_isoClasses` (equivalence): Isomorphism classes ≃ LevelQuotient U K∞.
- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid_finite_aut` (characterisation): Every automorphism group is finite if U is compact open and K∞ contains A_G(ℝ)^0 and is compact modulo it; no finiteness assertion is made for general closed K∞.

**Unit tests.**

- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid_sl2_i` (computation): For SL_2/ℚ, U = SL_2(ℤ̂), the automorphism group of the object over i has order 4.
- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid_neat` (degenerate): Under the compact-modulo-A_G hypotheses, neat level has trivial actual automorphism groups, by finiteness and torsion-freeness.
- `LevelMaps.levelGroupoid_not_space` (non-example): The groupoid is not determined by X_U: Y(1) and the coarse space of the groupoid agree, but the groupoid remembers the stabilizers of orders 4 and 6.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ and U = SL_2(ℤ̂), the automorphism group of the object over i is ⟨S⟩ of order 4.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `mathlib:CategoryTheory.ActionCategory`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-stabilizer-finite`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §5, p. 58. Quotient by a finite group action at non-neat level.

### Hecke correspondences

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hecke-correspondence` · construction.

For g ∈ G(𝔸_{F,f}) and compact open U, put U_g = U ∩ gUg⁻¹. The Hecke correspondence T_g is X_U ←p₁ X_{U_g} →p₂ X_U with p₁[x] = [x] and p₂[x] = [xg]; it depends only on UgU.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U compact open; g ∈ G(𝔸_f).

**Construction or proof.**

1. p₁ is the level map for U_g ⊂ U; p₂ is the level map for U_g ⊂ gUg⁻¹ followed by right translation by g (LevelQuotient.rightTranslate).

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/hecke-cartesian`: Cartesian squares
- `ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.3`: Hecke action on cohomology
- `AA.5/gl1-hecke-action`: GL_1 Hecke operators

**API.**

- `LevelMaps.hecke` (constructor): The pair of maps X_{U_g} → X_U.
- `LevelMaps.hecke_fst` (simp): (hecke g).1 (mk x) = mk x.
- `LevelMaps.hecke_snd` (simp): (hecke g).2 (mk x) = mk (x * g).
- `LevelMaps.hecke_degree` (characterisation): At neat level the degree of p₁ is [U : U_g] = degree of the double coset UgU (HeckeCoset.degree_eq_relIndex).

**Unit tests.**

- `LevelMaps.hecke_one` (degenerate): For g = 1 both maps are the identity.
- `LevelMaps.hecke_Tp_degree` (computation): For GL_2/ℚ, U = GL_2(ℤ̂), g = diag(p,1): [U : U_g] = p + 1.
- `LevelMaps.hecke_not_symmetric` (non-example): T_g and T_{g⁻¹} are transposes, not equal in general: for GL_2 with g = diag(p,1), T_{g⁻¹} is T_g composed with translation by the central idele p⁻¹ at p.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2/ℚ and g = diag(p,1) at p: T_g is the classical T_p correspondence on Y_0-type curves, of degree p + 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hecke-degree-double-coset`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 13. Hecke operators from double cosets.

### Cartesian squares of level maps

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hecke-cartesian` · theorem.

Let U′,L⊂U be compact open with U neat, K∞ containing A_G(ℝ)^0 and compact modulo it, and U′L=U. Then X_{U′∩L}→X_L, X_{U′∩L}→X_{U′}, X_{U′}→X_U, X_L→X_U is Cartesian. For L=U_g this identifies this one leg of a Hecke pullback only when the product condition holds. U′ normal does not imply that condition, and U′∩U_g is generally different from U′∩gU′g⁻¹.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U neat; U′L = U; K∞ contains A_G(ℝ)^0 and is compact modulo it.

**Construction or proof.**

1. At neat level all maps are coverings with fibres U/U′, L/(U′ ∩ L) (level-covering-map).
2. The fibre-product's fibre over a point of X_L is U/U′; the natural map from L/(U′ ∩ L) is bijective iff U′L = U.
3. A bijective map of coverings over X_L is a homeomorphism.

**Acceptance checks.**

- If U′L ≠ U the square is not Cartesian: degrees differ.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-covering-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hecke-correspondence`, `tauceti:HeckeCoset.degree_eq_relIndex`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §5, p. 58. The Hecke action on the tower of level quotients.

### Volume of a level quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quotient-volume-decomposition` · theorem.

With a Haar measure dg_f on G(𝔸_f), dg_∞ on G(F_∞) and the product measure on G(𝔸) (AA.0/restricted-haar-split), vol(G(F)\G(𝔸)^1) = vol(U) ∑_{i} vol(Γ_i\G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0) for representatives x_i of G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U, where Γ_i = G(F) ∩ x_iUx_i⁻¹, the measure on G(𝔸)^1 is transported from G(𝔸)/A_G(ℝ)^0 (AA.2/split-centre-decomposition) and G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0 carries the quotient measure. (G(𝔸)^1 is not G(F_∞)^1 × G(𝔸_f): finite ideles have nontrivial norms.)

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Transport G(F)\G(𝔸)^1 to G(F)\G(𝔸)/A_G(ℝ)^0 with the compatible product measure.
2. Component-decomposition separates the finitely many pieces Γ_i\(G(F∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0×U). This is a diagonal Γ_i-quotient, not a product with a literal U-fibre when stabilizers are present.
3. Apply the Weil integration formula with counting measure on Γ_i; integration over the compact U-factor gives vol(U) times the corresponding archimedean quotient volume.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ: vol(SL_2(ℚ)\SL_2(𝔸)) = vol(SL_2(ℤ̂)) vol(SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ)).
- For GL_1/ℚ with the idele measure and U = ℤ̂^×: vol(ℚ^×\𝔸^1) = 1 · vol({±1}\ℝ^×/ℝ_{>0}) = 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/automorphic-quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure-transitivity`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/restricted-haar-split`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), (2.1), p. 13. The component decomposition at the level of L²; the volume identity is its measure-theoretic shadow.

### Volumes under finite-index level change

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-volume-index` · theorem.

For compact open U′ ⊂ U, ∑_j vol(Γ′_j\G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0) = [U : U′] ∑_i vol(Γ_i\G(F_∞)/A_G(ℝ)^0).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F; U′ ⊂ U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Both sides times vol(U′), resp. vol(U), equal vol(G(F)\G(𝔸)^1) (quotient-volume-decomposition); vol(U) = [U : U′] vol(U′) (MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure).

**Acceptance checks.**

- [SL_2(ℤ) : Γ(N)] = vol(Γ(N)\SL_2(ℝ))/vol(SL_2(ℤ)\SL_2(ℝ)) when SL_2(ℚ)\SL_2(𝔸_f)/U(N) is one point.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quotient-volume-decomposition`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Subgroup.index_mul_measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/compact-open-volume`.

**Sources.**

- [Michael Lipnowski, Jacob Tsimerman, *How large is A_g(F_q)?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.02212v1), §3.2.3, p. 16. Level index changes; here the exact volume identity.

### The subgroup G(𝔸)^+ from the simply connected cover

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/plus-subgroup` · definition.

For G connected reductive over F with simply connected cover ρ : G̃ → G^der ⊂ G of the derived group, G(𝔸_F)^+ = ρ(G̃(𝔸_F)), a normal subgroup of G(𝔸_F) containing the commutator subgroup; likewise G(R)^+ for any F-algebra R. For G = PB^× (B a quaternion algebra over ℚ), G̃ = B^(1) is the norm-one group.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Take the image of the adelic homomorphism induced by the canonical simply connected derived cover.
2. The lifted conjugation and commutator morphisms requested from RG2.1 prove normality and commutator containment.
3. Integral compatibility is cover-integral-image; the baseline simply-connected predicate alone neither constructs this cover nor proves those morphisms.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/residual-quotient`: G_res = G(F)\G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+
- `GeometryOfNumbersPartIIToralJoinings`: invariance subgroups of joinings (Part II route of PAPER-KHAYUTIN-19)

**API.**

- `Approximation.plusSubgroup` (constructor): plusSubgroup G : Subgroup (AdelicPoints H) := range of the simply connected cover.
- `Approximation.plusSubgroup_normal` (instance): plusSubgroup is normal.
- `Approximation.commutator_le_plusSubgroup` (characterisation): ⁅G(𝔸), G(𝔸)⁆ ≤ plusSubgroup.
- `Approximation.plusSubgroup_gln` (example): For GL_n, plusSubgroup = SL_n(𝔸).

**Unit tests.**

- `Approximation.plusSubgroup_sln` (degenerate): For simply connected G, plusSubgroup = ⊤.
- `Approximation.plusSubgroup_pgl2_quotient` (computation): For PGL_2/ℚ, det induces G(𝔸)/plusSubgroup ≃ 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}.
- `Approximation.plusSubgroup_not_derived_points` (non-example): For PGL_2, plusSubgroup ≠ G^der(𝔸) = PGL_2(𝔸): the image of SL_2(ℚ_p) in PGL_2(ℚ_p) has index 4 for odd p.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = GL_n, G(𝔸)^+ = SL_n(𝔸).
- For G = PGL_2, G(𝔸)^+ is the image of SL_2(𝔸), and G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+ ≃ 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} via det.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`, `tauceti:TauCeti.simplyConnectedSemisimpleCommHopfAlgProperty`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/cover-integral-image`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §2.3, arXiv v3 pp. 15–16; Annals p. 162. Exactly the definition for G = PB^×.

### The residual quotient G_res

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/residual-quotient` · definition.

For connected reductive G/F, define G_res=G(𝔸_F)/(G(F)G(𝔸_F)^+), an abelian topological group with its canonical quotient topology, and π⁺:[G(𝔸)]→G_res. The composite G(𝔸)→G_res is a continuous surjective homomorphism. Hausdorffness or compactness requires closedness of the denominator subgroup; for PB×/ℚ these follow from reduced-norm-components and idele-class-square-compact.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; G a connected reductive group over F.

**Planet:** Residual quotient G_res.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The plus subgroup contains commutators, so G(F)G(𝔸)^+ is a normal subgroup and its abstract quotient is abelian.
2. Use the quotient topology and its induced continuous group operations.
3. For PB× the independent norm and idele-class lemmas below provide the closed compact quotient; no compactness of the raw local quotient is inferred.

**Uses.**

- `AA.4/residual-joint-limit`: limits of pushforwards to G_res × G_res
- `GeometryOfNumbersPartIIToralJoinings`: the residual spectrum in the mixing conjecture
- `AA.3/component-decomposition`: for G with G^der simply connected and noncompact, G_res mod G(F_∞) indexes connected components

**API.**

- `Approximation.residualQuotient` (constructor): residualQuotient G : Type, the quotient group G(𝔸) ⧸ (G(F) ⊔ plusSubgroup).
- `Approximation.residualQuotient.commGroup` (instance): residualQuotient is a commutative topological group.
- `Approximation.residualQuotient.piPlus` (projection): piPlus : G(𝔸) →* residualQuotient, continuous and surjective.
- `Approximation.residualQuotient.piPlus_rational` (simp): piPlus (diagonal γ) = 1.

**Unit tests.**

- `Approximation.residualQuotient_sl2` (degenerate): For SL_2 the residual quotient is trivial.
- `Approximation.residualQuotient_pgl2` (computation): For PGL_2/ℚ, residualQuotient ≃ ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}.
- `Approximation.residualQuotient_not_G_mod_plus` (non-example): G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+ itself is not compact for PGL_2: it is 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}, only locally compact (Khayutin E40).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For G = SL_2, G_res is trivial.
- For G = PGL_2 over ℚ, G_res ≃ ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} ≅ ∏_p ℤ_p^×/ℤ_p^{×2} (an infinite compact group of exponent 2).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/plus-subgroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Definition 3.1, arXiv v3 p. 22; Annals p. 169. Exactly the definition.

### Reduced norms of a quaternion algebra over ℚ

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quaternion-reduced-norm-image` · lemma.

For a quaternion algebra B over ℚ: Nrd(B_p^×) = ℚ_p^× for every prime p; Nrd(B_∞^×) = ℝ^× if B is split at ∞ and ℝ_{>0} otherwise; and Nrd(B^×) = ℚ^× if B is split at ∞, ℚ_{>0} otherwise (Hasse–Schilling–Maass).

**Hypotheses.** B a quaternion algebra over ℚ.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Locally the split case uses determinant. In the division case the unramified quadratic subfield supplies every unit norm and norms of even valuation; a uniformizer of the quaternion division algebra has reduced norm of valuation 1, supplying odd valuation. Equivalently use the Kneser local torsor theorem with its recorded gap.
2. Global: x is a reduced norm iff the quinary form Nrd ⊥ ⟨−x⟩ has an isotropic vector with nonzero last coordinate; for x > 0 (or B split at ∞) it is indefinite, hence isotropic by Hasse–Minkowski (GlobalQuadraticForms layer 5), and if the isotropic vector has last coordinate 0 then B is split and det represents x anyway.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For B = (−1, −1)_ℚ (Hamilton), every positive rational is a sum of four squares.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:QuaternionAlgebra.normForm`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalQuadraticForms#layer-5-hasseminkowski-isotropy`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/kneser-local-torsor`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §2.3, arXiv v3 pp. 15–16; Annals p. 162. Exactly the global statement.

### The reduced norm on the residual quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/reduced-norm-components` · theorem.

For G = PB^× with B a quaternion algebra over ℚ, the reduced norm induces an injective continuous homomorphism of locally compact groups Nrd : G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+ → 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}, and a bijection (indeed an isomorphism of compact groups) G_res ≃ ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} whether or not B is split at ∞. Neither G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+ nor 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} is compact.

**Hypotheses.** B a quaternion algebra over ℚ; G = PB^×.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Locally the norm-square-class kernel is the image of the actual norm-one cover; use cover-integral-image to assemble these identifications in the restricted product.
2. The local norm maps are open onto their images; at good places the integral norm image is the unit square-class subgroup. These give the topological image identification.
3. Globally divide by the reduced norm of B×. In the definite case a rational −1 changes the infinite sign, identifying the positive-sign image quotient with ℚ×\𝔸×/𝔸×².
4. The idele-class-square-compact lemma proves closedness and compactness after the rational quotient. Local norm openness and good integral norm images remain exact supplier refinements.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For B = M_2(ℚ), G_res ≃ ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} via det.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/residual-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quaternion-reduced-norm-image`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/plus-subgroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/cover-integral-image`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/idele-class-square-compact`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §2.3, arXiv v3 pp. 15–16; Annals p. 162. As printed ('compact', and image of index 2 in the definite case); corrected per Khayutin E39/E40 recorded in the reviewed extraction.

### Images of tori in the residual quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/torus-image-residual` · theorem.

For a quadratic field E ⊂ B and the torus T = E^×/ℚ^× ⊂ G = PB^×, π^+([T(𝔸)]) is a closed subgroup of G_res and Nrd ∘ π^+([T(𝔸)]) = ker χ_E, where χ_E : ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} → {±1} is the quadratic character of E/ℚ.

**Hypotheses.** B quaternion over ℚ; E ⊂ B quadratic.

**Construction or proof.**

1. T is ℚ-anisotropic, so [T(𝔸)] is a compact abelian group and its image is closed (Khayutin E10 corrects 'isotropic').
2. Nrd restricted to E^× is the field norm N_{E/ℚ}; its image modulo ℚ^×𝔸^{×2} is ℚ^× N(𝔸_E^×)/…, which is ker χ_E by class field theory (ClassFieldTheory layers 11–12).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For E = ℚ(i) ⊂ (−1,−1)_ℚ, the image is the kernel of the character of ℚ(i)/ℚ.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/reduced-norm-components`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-12-separate-arithmetic-global-existence-the-norm-index-and-the-global-correspondence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/homogeneous-measure-pushforward`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Proof of Proposition 3.6, arXiv v3 p. 25; Annals pp. 172–173. Exactly the statement.

### Limit behaviour of the residual spectrum

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/residual-joint-limit` · theorem.

Let B be a quaternion algebra over ℚ, G = PB^×, and for each i let T_i = E_i^×/ℚ^× ⊂ G be the torus of a quadratic field E_i ⊂ B, g_i, s_i ∈ G(𝔸), and μ_i the pushforward to [G(𝔸)] × [G(𝔸)] of the Haar probability measure of [T_i(𝔸)] under t ↦ ([t g_i], [t s_i g_i]). Suppose either the E_i are pairwise distinct (put H = G_res) or all equal one field E_0 (put H = ker(χ_{E_0} ∘ Nrd) < G_res). Then every weak-* limit point of (π^+ × π^+)_* μ_i is an H^Δ-invariant probability measure supported on a single coset of H^Δ; in general (π^+ × π^+)_* μ_i need not converge.

**Hypotheses.** as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The homogeneous pushforward is Haar on a coset of ker χ_{E_i}^Δ by the actual reduced-norm and torus-norm identification.
2. Distinct quadratic fields give distinct nontrivial characters by the class-field supplier. Apply diagonal-coset-limit with H=C=G_res.
3. For a constant field the subgroup H is fixed, and the same coset-limit lemma applies. No convergence of the moving translating cosets is presumed.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For E_i = E_0 fixed, limits are supported on a coset of ker(χ_{E_0} ∘ Nrd)^Δ.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/torus-image-residual`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/residual-quotient`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-12-separate-arithmetic-global-existence-the-norm-index-and-the-global-correspondence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/homogeneous-measure-pushforward`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/chabauty-limit-kernels`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/diagonal-coset-limit`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/idele-class-square-compact`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Proposition 3.6, arXiv v3 p. 24; Annals p. 172. Exactly the conclusion.
- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Remark 3.7, arXiv v3 p. 25; Annals p. 172. The non-convergence.

### Strong approximation through finitely many places

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-finite-places` · lemma.

For S containing the archimedean places, G has strong approximation with respect to S iff for every finite set S₁ of places disjoint from S, the S ∪ S₁-arithmetic group G(𝒪(S ∪ S₁)) is dense in G_{S₁} = ∏_{v∈S₁} G(F_v).

**Hypotheses.** S ⊇ archimedean places.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Basic open sets of G(𝔸^S) are products of opens at finitely many places S₁ and integral points elsewhere (AA.1/compact-open-product).
2. Density of G(F) in such a set is density of the elements integral outside S ∪ S₁ in the open of G_{S₁}.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For SL_2/ℚ and S = {∞}: density of SL_2(ℤ[1/p]) in SL_2(ℚ_p) for every p.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-property`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6, p. 16. Exactly the reformulation.

### S-arithmetic groups are not discrete at an extra place

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/s-arithmetic-nondiscrete` · lemma.

If G is absolutely almost simple, G_S is noncompact and S₁ is finite, nonempty and disjoint from S, then the image of G(𝒪(S ∪ S₁)) in G_{S₁} is not discrete and is infinite.

**Hypotheses.** G_S noncompact; F a number field; G connected absolutely almost simple; S finite containing the archimedean places; S₁ finite, nonempty and disjoint from S.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G(𝒪(S ∪ S₁)) is a lattice in G_{S∪S₁} (AA.3/s-arithmetic-lattice) while G(𝒪(S)) is a lattice in G_S.
2. If its image in G_{S₁} were discrete, G(𝒪(S ∪ S₁)) ∩ (G_S × open compact) would be a lattice in G_S of covolume tending to infinity as the compact shrinks, contradicting finiteness of covolume (Platonov–Rapinchuk Lemma 3.17).

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℤ[1/p]) is dense, not discrete, in SL_2(ℚ_p).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/s-arithmetic-lattice`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6, p. 16. Exactly the step.

### Principal units of small radius are torsion free

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/padic-ball-torsion-free` · lemma.

In an algebraic closure of ℚ_p, the multiplicative group {λ : |λ − 1|_p < p^{−1/(p−1)}} contains no root of unity other than 1; in particular eigenvalues of elements of 1 + pM_n(ℤ_p) (p ≥ 3) or 1 + 4M_n(ℤ_2) generate a torsion-free group.

**Hypotheses.** p prime.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The ultrametric ball around one is a subgroup, including inverses.
2. The root-of-unity distance lemma excludes every nontrivial torsion element from this strict ball.
3. Use congruence-matrix-eigenvalue-bound and the strict inequality p^−1<p^−1/(p−1) for p≥3, or 2^−2<2^−1 for p=2.

**Acceptance checks.**

- −1 ∈ 1 + 2ℤ_2 but −1 ∉ 1 + 4ℤ_2: the level 4 is needed at p = 2.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/padic-root-unity-distance`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/congruence-matrix-eigenvalue-bound`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Proposition 3.5, p. 34. The neatness criterion, whose p-adic input this lemma is.

### Free action of finite level groups at neat level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-action-free-at-neat` · lemma.

For neat compact open U, normal open U′⊂U, and K∞ containing A_G(ℝ)^0 and compact modulo it, the full group U/U′ acts freely and properly discontinuously on X_{U′}. Rational stabilizers are finite, and neatness makes them trivial; the central rational kernel is trivial in this scope.

**Hypotheses.** U neat; U′ normal in U; K∞ contains A_G(ℝ)^0 and is compact modulo it.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The rational stabilizer embeds in the conjugate finite-level arithmetic subgroup and is finite by rational-stabilizer-finite.
2. Neatness makes it torsion free, hence trivial. This identifies every canonical level-group stabilizer with the identity.
3. The finite group action on the Hausdorff level space is properly discontinuous.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2/ℚ at U(3), U/U(9) acts freely on Y(9).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/neat-torsion-free`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-stabilizer-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-action-proper`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §5, p. 58. Free quotients at small level.

### Pushforward of a homogeneous measure to a compact abelian quotient

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/homogeneous-measure-pushforward` · lemma.

Let C be a compact abelian group, π : G → C a continuous surjective homomorphism, T ≤ G a closed subgroup with Λ ≤ T discrete and Λ\T compact (as for [T(𝔸)] with T anisotropic modulo the centre), and μ the T-invariant probability measure on Λ\T g. If π(Λ) = 1, then π_*μ is the Haar probability measure of the coset π(T)·π(g) of the closed subgroup π(T).

**Hypotheses.** C compact abelian; π continuous surjective homomorphism; Λ\T compact.

**Construction or proof.**

1. π_*μ is invariant under π(T).
2. A probability measure on a coset invariant under the subgroup is its Haar measure (uniqueness of Haar measure on the compact group π(T)).
3. π(T) is closed: it is the image of the compact Λ\T under the continuous map induced by π.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For T = G, π_*μ is the Haar probability measure of C.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/residual-quotient`, `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.isMulLeftInvariant_eq_smul`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Definition 3.1, arXiv v3 p. 22; Annals p. 169. The pushforward principle, here for homogeneous toral measures.

### Kernels of distinct characters converge to the whole group

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/chabauty-limit-kernels` · lemma.

Let C be a compact abelian group and (χ_i) a sequence of pairwise distinct continuous characters C → {±1}. Then the closed subgroups ker χ_i converge to C in the Chabauty topology; consequently any weak-* limit of probability measures invariant under ker χ_i is C-invariant.

**Hypotheses.** C compact abelian; χ_i pairwise distinct.

**Construction or proof.**

1. For every nonempty open subset of C choose a nonnegative continuous function supported there with positive Haar integral. Kernel Haar convergence makes the open set meet ker χ_i eventually; this is the Chabauty lower condition, and the upper condition is automatic inside C.
2. For arbitrary ker χ_i-invariant probability measures, a fixed nontrivial Fourier coefficient vanishes eventually by the same annihilator computation.
3. Every weak limit therefore has all nontrivial Fourier coefficients zero; Fourier uniqueness makes it C-invariant.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For C = ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}, distinct quadratic characters χ_{E_i} have kernels converging to C.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quadratic-kernel-fourier`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CompactGroups#layer-5-the-peter-weyl-theorem`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Proof of Proposition 3.6, arXiv v3 p. 25; Annals pp. 172–173. Exactly the argument.

### Level quotients are Hausdorff

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient-hausdorff` · lemma.

For compact open U and K_∞ compact modulo A_G(ℝ)^0 containing A_G(ℝ)^0, the level quotient X_U is Hausdorff and locally compact, and G(F) acts properly discontinuously on G(𝔸)/K_∞U.

**Hypotheses.** U compact open; K_∞ as stated.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The right homogeneous quotient is locally compact and Hausdorff because K∞U is closed.
2. Apply rational-action-proper to the canonical topology.
3. A proper discrete-group quotient of a locally compact Hausdorff space is Hausdorff and locally compact. The exact topological quotient theorem remains an interface refinement.

**Acceptance checks.**

- SL_2(ℤ)\ℍ is Hausdorff.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-action-proper`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`, `mathlib:ProperlyDiscontinuousSMul`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.13, p. 57. The topology of level quotients.

### Degree of a Hecke correspondence

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hecke-degree-double-coset` · lemma.

For compact open U and g∈G(𝔸_f), UgU is the disjoint union of [U:U∩gUg⁻¹] right U-cosets, represented by u g. In the compact-modulo-A_G neat scope, p₁:X_{U∩gUg⁻¹}→X_U is a covering of exactly that degree; the rational central kernel is trivial.

**Hypotheses.** U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The orbit map u↦u g U has stabilizer U∩gUg⁻¹, giving the right-coset count and the pinned HeckeCoset relative-index formula.
2. Apply level-covering-map. No central correction is needed at neat level under the specified finite-stabilizer hypotheses.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For GL_2/ℚ, U = GL_2(ℤ̂), g = diag(p,1): p + 1.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:HeckeCoset.degree_eq_relIndex`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-covering-map`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 13. Hecke operators from double cosets, whose degree this lemma computes.

### Finite covolume of a projection closure

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/projection-finite-covolume` · lemma.

Let Γ be a lattice in locally compact second-countable groups A×B, and Δ the closure of its B-projection. Then Δ\B carries a nonzero finite B-invariant Radon measure.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The map Γ\(A×B)→Δ\B induced by projection is continuous, surjective and B-equivariant.
2. Push forward the normalized finite invariant measure on the lattice quotient.
3. Finite regularity on the countably generated locally compact quotient gives a Radon probability measure; invariance and nonzeroness are preserved.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6, proof following Theorem 2.3. The finite-covolume projection step is separated from openness.

### Native-field Lie algebra of an arithmetic closure

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-native-lie-closure` · lemma.

For absolutely almost simple G/F, S containing all infinite places with G(F_S) noncompact, and finite nonempty S₁ disjoint from S, the closure of G(O_{F,S∪S₁}) in ∏_{v∈S₁}G(F_v) has full native F_v Lie algebra in every factor and no proper graph Lie subalgebra linking distinct places. This is an arithmetic statement requiring the full S-integral subgroup, rather than mere F_v-Zariski density.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The projection-finite-covolume and S-arithmetic nondiscreteness inputs provide a positive-dimensional closed analytic subgroup.
2. For ℚ with one additional isotropic prime use Rapinchuk §2.6 and the ℚ_p closed-subgroup argument.
3. For general F and multiple places the restriction-of-scalars trace-field and independent-factor argument is a recorded input. Without it SL₂(ℚ_p) inside SL₂(E) is a counterexample to native-E openness.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/projection-finite-covolume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/s-arithmetic-nondiscrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/zariski-dense-closure-open`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/borel-density`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §§2.6–2.7, proof of Theorem 2.3 and reference to Platonov–Rapinchuk §7.4. The surveyed proof treats the single-prime ℚ branch; the general arithmetic input remains explicit.

### Openness in a finite product of completions

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-finite-product-openness` · lemma.

Given arithmetic-native-lie-closure and the closed analytic subgroup theorem for ∏_{v∈S₁}G(F_v), the closure Δ of G(O_{F,S∪S₁}) is open in that finite product. Its index is finite by projection-finite-covolume.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. A closed analytic subgroup with the full product Lie algebra has the same dimension and contains an identity neighbourhood by the inverse function theorem.
2. Apply open-finite-covolume-finite-index to the nonzero finite invariant quotient measure. Full projections alone would not exclude graph subgroups.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-native-lie-closure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/projection-finite-covolume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/open-finite-covolume-finite-index`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6. The product Lie hypothesis is essential.

### Elimination of arithmetic finite-index closures

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-finite-index-elimination` · lemma.

For G/F absolutely almost simple simply connected, S containing infinity with G(F_S) noncompact, and S₁ finite disjoint from S, an open finite-index closure of G(O_{F,S∪S₁}) in ∏_{v∈S₁}G(F_v) is the whole product. At isotropic factors this follows from the local Kneser–Tits/Tits finite-index theorem; anisotropic factors need the separate global arithmetic congruence argument.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Take the finite normal core of the closure.
2. At every isotropic factor the precise RG2.4 finite-index theorem makes that factor trivial in the finite quotient.
3. For anisotropic compact factors the finite quotient can be nontrivial for an arbitrary open subgroup. Eliminating it for this arithmetic subgroup is a distinct recorded global input; weak approximation does not impose integrality away from S∪S₁.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-finite-product-openness`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2.6 and the reference to Platonov–Rapinchuk §7.4. The isotropic proof does not silently cover compact anisotropic factors.

### Eigenvalues of algebraic tensor subquotients

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/algebraic-tensor-eigenvalues` · lemma.

For a closed faithful algebraic representation ρ of a finite-type affine group G over a characteristic-zero field and any algebraic representation σ, every eigenvalue of σ(g) over an algebraic closure lies in the multiplicative subgroup generated by the eigenvalues of ρ(g).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Request the algebraic comodule theorem that σ is a subquotient of a finite direct sum of tensor words in ρ and ρ∨.
2. Triangularize the action of g over the algebraic closure. Tensor eigenvalues are products and dual eigenvalues are inverses.
3. Invariant subspaces and quotient spaces take subsets of the triangular diagonal eigenvalues.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8. The representation-independent neat condition uses algebraic tensor subquotients.

### Stable lattices for compact p-adic matrix groups

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/compact-stable-padic-lattice` · lemma.

Every compact subgroup C⊂GL_n(ℚ_p) preserves a full ℤ_p-lattice Λ⊂ℚ_p^n. It is conjugate into GL_n(ℤ_p).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Compactness bounds all entries of C, so Cℤ_p^n lies in p^−Mℤ_p^n for one M.
2. Let Λ be the ℤ_p-span of Cℤ_p^n. It contains ℤ_p^n, is contained in p^−Mℤ_p^n, and is C-stable.
3. Noetherianity and the PID property give a free rank-n lattice; choose its basis.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/compact-open-product`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8. This makes the congruence-neat construction valid for an arbitrary compact level.

### Distance of p-adic roots of unity from one

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/padic-root-unity-distance` · lemma.

If ζ≠1 is a root of unity in an algebraic closure of ℚ_p, then |ζ−1|_p≥p^{−1/(p−1)}. More precisely a primitive p^k-th root has distance p^{−1/(p^{k−1}(p−1))}, while a root of order prime to p has distance one.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Reduction on prime-to-p roots of unity is injective: Hensel uniqueness for T^m−1 and its unit derivative gives distance one for a nontrivial such root.
2. For a primitive p^k-root, evaluate the cyclotomic polynomial at one to obtain p=∏(1−ζ^a), over a prime to p.
3. The conjugates have the same extended p-adic absolute value, so each factor has absolute value p^−1/φ(p^k). Combine the prime-to-p and p-primary parts.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-0-places-completions-and-the-product-formula`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8. The exact distance bound justifies the one-prime neatness criterion.

### Eigenvalue bound for congruence matrices

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/congruence-matrix-eigenvalue-bound` · lemma.
