If M∈1+p^a M_n(ℤ_p), every eigenvalue λ of M in ℚ̄_p satisfies |λ−1|_p≤p^−a. The eigenvalues and their inverses then lie in the multiplicative open ball used by padic-ball-torsion-free when p≥3,a≥1 or p=2,a≥2.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. An eigenvalue μ of an integral matrix has |μ|_p≤1, by its monic integral characteristic polynomial.
2. Apply this to (M−1)/p^a; λ=1+p^aμ.
3. Ultrametric multiplication and inversion preserve the ball about one.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/padic-root-unity-distance`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8. The matrix bound is independent of choice of conjugating lattice.

### Compact kernel of height on an archimedean level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/compact-kernel-split-centre` · lemma.

For connected reductive G/F let K∞⊂G(F∞) be closed, contain A_G(ℝ)^0, and be compact modulo it. Then K∞∩ker H_{G,∞} is compact, and multiplication gives K∞≃A_G(ℝ)^0×(K∞∩ker H_{G,∞}).

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The archimedean height restricts to a topological isomorphism on A_G(ℝ)^0.
2. Map k to (a(H∞(k)),a(H∞(k))⁻¹k); centrality makes this a group and topological product decomposition.
3. Its kernel factor is homeomorphic to K∞/A_G(ℝ)^0, hence compact.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height-split-centre-iso`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, pp. 13–15. The compact kernel removes the noncompact split-centre direction.

### Finite full rational stabilizers

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-stabilizer-finite` · lemma.

Under compact-kernel-split-centre and for compact open U⊂G(𝔸_f), A_x=G(F)∩gK∞Ug⁻¹ is finite. Here A_x is the full stabilizer, before division by any rational central subgroup.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Every rational character has total adelic norm one on a rational point by the product formula.
2. Its finite component lies in g_fUg_f⁻¹, a compact group; every positive-real character norm is one there. Its archimedean height is therefore zero.
3. Conjugate the archimedean point into the compact kernel of K∞. The full adelic stabilizer lies in a compact product, whose intersection with the closed discrete rational subgroup is finite.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/compact-kernel-split-centre`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-split`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8; §2, pp. 13–15. The proof uses the full product formula, not an arbitrary noncompact K∞.

### Proper arithmetic action on the level space

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-action-proper` · lemma.

Under rational-stabilizer-finite, the discrete group G(F) acts properly discontinuously on G(𝔸)/K∞U with its canonical quotient topology: for compact C,D only finitely many γ satisfy γC∩D≠∅.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the split-centre quotient homeomorphism G(𝔸)/A_G(ℝ)^0≃G(𝔸)^1. The residual right subgroup is compact by compact-kernel-split-centre and compactness of U.
2. The quotient by this compact subgroup has compact lifts for compact subsets, using finite covers by relatively compact quotient neighbourhoods.
3. If γC∩D is nonempty, γ lies in the compact product of the lifts and the compact right subgroup. The rational subgroup is closed discrete, so only finitely many such γ occur.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/compact-kernel-split-centre`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/closed-homogeneous-space`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8. Canonical quotient topology and compact lifts precede the covering theorem.

### Fibre mass for full stabilizers

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-full-stabilizer-mass` · lemma.

Let U′⊂U be compact open and let A_x=G(F)∩gK∞Ug⁻¹ be finite. For the full stabilizers A_y at the points above x, ∑_{y↦x}1/|A_y|=[U:U′]/|A_x|.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The finite group A_x acts on U/U′ through the finite component of g⁻¹A_xg.
2. The fibre is its orbit set and each orbit stabilizer is the corresponding full A_y.
3. For each orbit |orbit|=|A_x|/|A_y|. Sum these equalities over the finite coset set.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/double-coset-level-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/rational-stabilizer-finite`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §1, pp. 7–8. This is the full action-groupoid convention before the central correction.

### Integral lifting for reductive abelianization

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/abelianization-integral-lifts` · lemma.

For connected reductive G/F with simply connected derived group and ν:G→D=G/G^der, there is a finite set B such that smooth reductive models over O_{F,B} extend ν and ν:G(O_v)→D(O_v) is surjective for every finite v∉B.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Spread the quotient and its simply connected derived kernel to smooth models with geometrically connected fibres, as requested from RG2.3.
2. A fibre over d∈D(O_v) is a smooth torsor under the connected derived model. Lang vanishing over the finite residue field supplies a residue point.
3. Hensel lifting supplies an O_v-point of that fibre, which is the required integral lift.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/hopf-spreading`, `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.3`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-9-pinned-chevalleydemazure-group-schemes-over-ℤ`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2, discussion of simply connected groups. This is the extra restricted-product input beyond local H¹ vanishing.

### Surjectivity of finite adelic abelianization

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/abelianization-adelic-surjective` · lemma.

Under abelianization-integral-lifts and local simply connected H¹ vanishing, ν:G(𝔸_f)→D(𝔸_f) is surjective with kernel G^der(𝔸_f). It is the actual restricted-product homomorphism induced by ν.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. At each exceptional finite place use the torsor fibre and kneser-local-torsor to lift the local point.
2. At almost all places lift the integral point integrally by abelianization-integral-lifts.
3. These lifts assemble into a restricted-product element. The kernel is identified placewise with the derived group, with the same integral compatibility.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/abelianization-integral-lifts`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/kneser-local-torsor`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`.

**Sources.**

- [Andrei S. Rapinchuk, *On strong approximation for algebraic groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4425), §2, simply connected local and global approximation. Pointwise surjectivity is supplemented by integral lifts.

### Integral compatibility of a central derived cover

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/cover-integral-image` · lemma.

For the actual simply connected central cover ρ:G̃→G^der, at almost every finite place v one has ρ(G̃(F_v))∩G^der(O_v)=ρ(G̃(O_v)). Consequently the adelic image is exactly the restricted product of the local images with these integral image subgroups.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Extend ρ as a finite morphism of integral models away from finitely many places.
2. A local lift over F_v of an integral target point extends over O_v by the valuative criterion for properness of a finite morphism.
3. Choose such integral lifts almost everywhere and assemble them adelically.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.3`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §3.1, discussion of G(𝔸)^+. The actual algebraic cover, rather than an arbitrary point homomorphism, defines the plus subgroup.

### Compact idele classes modulo squares

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/idele-class-square-compact` · lemma.

Let C_F=F×\𝔸_F× be the idele class group of a number field. Then C_F/C_F² is compact Hausdorff: the norm decomposition C_F≃ℝ_{>0}×C_F¹ identifies it with the quotient of compact C_F¹ by its square image.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The norm-one idele class group is compact by the GlobalNumberFields layer-6 contract.
2. Squaring is surjective on ℝ_{>0}. The image of squaring on the compact norm-one factor is compact and hence closed.
3. The resulting quotient is compact Hausdorff. The local idele square-class group itself remains noncompact.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), §3.1 and Remark 3.7. Compactness appears only after quotienting by rational points.

### Fourier convergence of quadratic-character kernels

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quadratic-kernel-fourier` · lemma.

Let C be a compact Hausdorff abelian group and χ_i:C→{±1} pairwise distinct nontrivial continuous characters. For each fixed character ψ of C, the Haar integral of ψ over ker χ_i is zero unless ψ=1 or ψ=χ_i, and hence these kernel Haar probabilities converge weakly to Haar probability on C.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. A character restricted to a compact subgroup has integral zero unless trivial, by Haar translation invariance.
2. A character trivial on ker χ_i factors through C/ker χ_i≃{±1}, so it is 1 or χ_i.
3. For each fixed nontrivial ψ there is at most one exceptional i. Character separation and Stone–Weierstrass, supplied by CompactGroups layer 5, extend convergence to all continuous functions.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/compact-open-volume`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CompactGroups#layer-5-the-peter-weyl-theorem`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Proposition 3.6 and Remark 3.7. Distinct quadratic characters give the Fourier limit without presuming a full Chabauty–Pontryagin theorem.

### Limits of Haar measures on diagonal cosets

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/diagonal-coset-limit` · lemma.

For compact abelian C, closed H_i≤C with H_i→H through the quadratic-kernel setting, and points z_i∈C×C, every weak limit of Haar probabilities on z_iH_i^Δ is H^Δ-invariant and supported on one coset of H^Δ. The analogous statement holds when H_i=H is fixed.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. For a character (ψ₁,ψ₂) of C×C, the integral along H_i^Δ is zero unless ψ₁ψ₂ is trivial on H_i.
2. The quadratic-kernel Fourier computation gives invariance under H^Δ for every weak limit.
3. The quotient by C^Δ is constant on each moving coset when H=C; its pushforward is a Dirac probability, and the Dirac image of a compact group is closed. For fixed H use the quotient by H^Δ instead. Thus the limit remains supported on one coset.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quadratic-kernel-fourier`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/homogeneous-measure-pushforward`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CompactGroups#layer-5-the-peter-weyl-theorem`.

**Sources.**

- [Ilya Khayutin, *Joint equidistribution of CM points*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.04557v3), Proposition 3.6 and Remark 3.7. The translations may move; only limit points are asserted.

## AA.5 — Validation at specified groups and levels

The GL₁ checks use idele classes and the full logarithmic unit lattice. Divide out complex compact circles before counting torus dimensions, and separate component permutation from translation on torus cohomology. For GL₂/ℚ, use the raw action on both half-planes with ℝ×SO(2), and the folded Mathlib action with ℝ×O(2); principal level N gives the two stated determinant component quotients. Definite quaternion checks use reduced-norm-one rational stabilizers, then the full/effective mass formulas.

At N=3 the SO(2) and O(2) component counts are respectively two and one. Hurwitz class number one, the 24 unit enumeration and the Eichler index four are explicit validation inputs requiring their own computation; abstract compactness does not prove them.

### The GL_1 quotient is the idele class group

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient` · theorem.

For G = G_m over a number field F: G(F)\G(𝔸_F) ≃ₜ* IdeleClassGroup F; G(𝔸_F)^1 is the group 𝔸_F^1 of norm-one ideles; A_G(ℝ)^0 = ℝ_{>0} embedded diagonally at the archimedean places; 𝔸_F^× = 𝔸_F^1 × ℝ_{>0}; and F^×\𝔸_F^1 is compact while F^×\𝔸_F^× is not.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Planet:** GL1 check: idele class group.

**Construction or proof.**

1. AA.1/gm-adelic identifies the groups; the quotient topologies agree.
2. X*_F(G_m) = ℤ·id, so H_G = log ‖·‖ and G(𝔸)^1 = 𝔸^1 (AA.2/norm-one-subgroup).
3. A_G = G_m ⊂ Res_{F/ℚ} G_m, so A_G(ℝ)^0 = ℝ_{>0}; split-centre-decomposition.
4. Compactness: GlobalNumberFields layer 6 (or AA.3/compactness-anisotropic, G_m^der = 1).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F = ℚ: ℚ^×\𝔸^1 ≃ ℤ̂^×, of ideleHaar volume 1.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic`, `mathlib:NumberField.IdeleClassGroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/norm-one-subgroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/split-centre-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/quotient-norm-one-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §3, p. 16. The GL_1 case n = 1.
- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), §5.8, p. 22. Exactly the compactness check.

### GL_1 class number

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-class-number` · lemma.

For G = G_m over F and U = Ô^× = ∏_v 𝒪_v^×, G(F)\G(𝔸_f)/U ≃ Cl(𝒪_F), so its cardinality is the class number of F.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The map from finite ideles to fractional ideals, x ↦ ∏ 𝔭_v^{v(x_v)}, is surjective with kernel Ô^× and sends F^× to principal ideals (Borel 2.2; GlobalNumberFields layer 4 finite-idele description of the class group).
2. Compare with AA.3/class-number-finite.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F = ℚ(√−5) the double coset space has 2 elements.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `mathlib:NumberField.classNumber`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-4-finite-adeles`.

**Sources.**

- [Armand Borel, *Some finiteness properties of adele groups over number fields*](http://www.numdam.org/item/PMIHES_1963__16__5_0.pdf), Proposition 2.2, p. 11. Exactly the statement.

### Geometry of the GL_1 arithmetic quotients X_Q

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-XQ-components` · theorem.

For a finite set Q of finite places with N(v) ≡ 1 mod p^n, let U_Q = K_∞ × ∏_v U_{Q,v}, where K_∞ ≅ (S¹)^{r₂} is the identity component of the maximal compact subgroup of (F ⊗ ℝ)^×, U_{Q,v} = 𝒪_v^× for v ∉ Q and the index-p^n subgroup of 𝒪_v^× for v ∈ Q, and X_Q = F^×\𝔸_F^×/U_Q A_∞^0 with A_∞^0 = A_{G_m}(ℝ)^0. Each connected component of X_Q is a compact torus (S¹)^{r₁+r₂−1}, and π_0(X_Q) = F^×\𝔸_F^×/U_Q (F ⊗ ℝ)^{×,0}, an extension of the narrow class group of F by a quotient of ∏_{v∈Q} 𝒪_v^×/𝒪_v^{×p^n} (not, in general, the maximal exponent-p^n quotient of the ray class group).

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; Q, p, n as stated.

**Planet:** GL1 check: tori X_Q.

**Construction or proof.**

1. AA.3/component-decomposition for G_m with level U_Q: components are Γ\(F ⊗ ℝ)^×/ℝ_{>0} with Γ = F^× ∩ U_Q a finite-index subgroup of the units.
2. After dividing by K∞=(S¹)^{r₂}, the archimedean identity component modulo A∞^0 is ℝ^{r₁+r₂−1} in logarithmic coordinates. The positive congruence-unit subgroup is a full lattice of that rank (gl1-units-lattice). Each component is therefore a torus of dimension r₁+r₂−1; the complex circles were already divided out.
3. π_0 is the quotient by the identity component (F ⊗ ℝ)^{×,0} (correcting Calegari–Geraghty's description, E160 of the reviewed extraction).

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F = ℚ, X_Q is finite (r₁ + r₂ − 1 = 0) and π_0(X_Q) = ℚ^×\𝔸^×/U_Q ℝ_{>0} ≅ ℤ̂^×/U_Q.
- For F real quadratic and Q = ∅, X_Q has h⁺_F components, each a circle.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`, `mathlib:NumberField.Units.rank`, `mathlib:NumberField.Units.unitLattice_rank`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-units-lattice`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-logarithmic-torus`.

**Sources.**

- [Frank Calegari, David Geraghty, *Modularity lifting beyond the Taylor–Wiles method*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4224v2), §8.2, p. 79. The components; the component group is corrected (E160 of the reviewed extraction).

### The invariant l0 for GL_1

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-component-dimension` · lemma.

Every connected component of X_Q has dimension r₁ + r₂ − 1 = NumberField.Units.rank F, the value of Calegari–Geraghty's invariant l0 for G = GL_1/F; in particular its cohomology vanishes above degree r₁ + r₂ − 1.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Dimension of the torus of gl1-XQ-components; r₁ + r₂ = #InfinitePlace F (Mathlib), so the dimension is Units.rank F.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F imaginary quadratic, l0 = 0.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-XQ-components`, `mathlib:NumberField.Units.rank`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AlgebraicTopology#stage-6-cohomology-products-and-manifold-duality`.

**Sources.**

- [Frank Calegari, David Geraghty, *Modularity lifting beyond the Taylor–Wiles method*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4224v2), §8.2, p. 77. Exactly the value.

### Degree-zero cohomology of X_Q

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-H0` · lemma.

The ℤ_p-module of locally constant functions X_Q → ℤ_p is free on π_0(X_Q): H^0(X_Q, ℤ_p) ≅ ℤ_p[π_0(X_Q)].

**Hypotheses.** as in gl1-XQ-components.

**Construction or proof.**

1. X_Q is a finite disjoint union of its connected components (finitely many, by AA.3/class-number-finite), each connected; locally constant functions are constant on components.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F = ℚ and Q = ∅, H^0(X, ℤ_p) = ℤ_p (one component).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-XQ-components`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`.

**Sources.**

- [Frank Calegari, David Geraghty, *Modularity lifting beyond the Taylor–Wiles method*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4224v2), §8.2, p. 79. H^0 is the group ring of the component group (here with the corrected component group).

### Hecke and diamond operators for GL_1

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-hecke-action` · lemma.

For v∉Q the GL₁ Hecke operator of the finite idele π_v is right translation, and for v∈Q a unit α gives the diamond translation. Each permutes π₀(X_Q) by its finite idele class. Under the natural exterior-power identification of torus cohomology, translation induces the identity within the torus factor and the indicated permutation of the component factors in every degree.

**Hypotheses.** as in gl1-XQ-components.

**Construction or proof.**

1. The abelian group has one right U-coset in each Hecke double coset, so the correspondence is a single translation.
2. A translation on a connected real torus is homotopic to the identity, using a lifted path in its real vector space.
3. Import the torus exterior-power cohomology/product calculation and combine it with the component permutation. This does not assert that the operator is the identity on H⁰ when it moves components.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F = ℚ and Q = ∅, T_p acts on π_0 = point trivially.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hecke-correspondence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-XQ-components`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-H0`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AlgebraicTopology#stage-6-cohomology-products-and-manifold-duality`.

**Sources.**

- [Frank Calegari, David Geraghty, *Modularity lifting beyond the Taylor–Wiles method*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4224v2), §8.2, p. 79. Exactly the statement.

### The GL_2/ℚ quotient and the upper half-plane

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-upper-half-plane-component` · theorem.

For G = GL_2 over ℚ, K_∞ = ℝ^× SO(2) and U ⊂ GL_2(𝔸_f) compact open, G(ℚ)\G(𝔸)/K_∞U ≃ ⊔_{c ∈ ℚ_{>0}\𝔸_f^×/det U} Γ_c\ℍ, where Γ_c = GL_2(ℚ)^+ ∩ g_c U g_c⁻¹ for g_c ∈ GL_2(𝔸_f) with det g_c = c, acting on ℍ by Möbius transformations; for det U = ℤ̂^× there is a single component.

**Hypotheses.** G = GL_2/ℚ; U compact open.

**Planet:** GL2 check: upper half-plane.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use gl2-real-quotient and upper-half-plane-action-conventions: the raw GL₂(ℝ) action gives ℍ±, whereas Mathlib glAction is its folded ℍ version. The rational negative-determinant matrix exchanges the two half-planes, so one may restrict to ℍ and GL₂(ℚ)^+.
2. π_0 = GL_2(ℚ)^+\GL_2(𝔸_f)/U ≃ ℚ_{>0}\𝔸_f^×/det U by class-set-abelianization (SL_2 is simply connected with SL_2(ℝ) noncompact; strong approximation for SL_2).
3. AA.3/component-decomposition then identifies each component; the analytic identification of Γ\ℍ with the modular curve is ModularCurvesPartII R12.2.

**Uses.**

- `AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.5`: the classical/adelic function dictionary for GL_2 (RS-04 link AA.5 → AF.5)
- `ShimuraVarieties:V8`: underlying GL_2 quotient comparison (RS-04 link AA.5 → V8)

**Acceptance checks.**

- For U = GL_2(ℤ̂): SL_2(ℤ)\ℍ (one component).
- For U = K(N): (ℤ/N)^× copies of Γ(N)\ℍ (gl2-principal-level).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/component-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/class-set-abelianization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-theorem`, `tauceti:Matrix.SpecialLinearGroup.map_intCast_zmod_surjective`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/upper-half-plane-action-conventions`, `mathlib:Matrix.GeneralLinearGroup.det`, `ModularCurvesPartII:R12.2`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-real-quotient`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.13, p. 57. Exactly the decomposition for (GL_2, ℍ^±).

### Principal congruence level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-principal-level` · lemma.

For U = K(N) = ker(GL_2(ℤ̂) → GL_2(ℤ/N)), det K(N) = {x ∈ ℤ̂^× : x ≡ 1 mod N}, the components are indexed by (ℤ/N)^×, and each Γ_c is Γ(N) = ker(SL_2(ℤ) → SL_2(ℤ/N)) (Mathlib CongruenceSubgroup.Gamma).

**Hypotheses.** N ≥ 1.

**Construction or proof.**

1. ℚ_{>0}\𝔸_f^×/det K(N) ≅ ℤ̂^×/(1 + Nℤ̂) ≅ (ℤ/N)^×.
2. GL_2(ℚ)^+ ∩ K(N) = SL_2(ℤ) ∩ K(N) = Γ(N) (determinant a positive unit of ℤ is 1); other components are conjugate by elements of GL_2(ℤ̂) normalizing K(N) with determinant c.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For N = 1 or 2 there is one component.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-upper-half-plane-component`, `mathlib:CongruenceSubgroup.Gamma`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.13, p. 57. The groups Γ_c.

### Compactness for a definite quaternion algebra

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/definite-quaternion-compact` · theorem.

For a definite quaternion algebra D over ℚ and G = D^×: G(ℚ)\G(𝔸)^1 is compact; G(ℚ)\G(𝔸_f)/U is finite for every compact open U; each Γ_{x,U} = D^× ∩ xUx⁻¹ is finite; and D^×/ℚ^× is discrete in (D ⊗ 𝔸_f)^×/𝔸_f^×.

**Hypotheses.** D a quaternion division algebra over ℚ with D ⊗ ℝ ≅ ℍ (Hamilton).

**Planet:** Definite quaternion compactness.

**Construction or proof.**

1. G^der = SL_1(D) is anisotropic: D is a division algebra, so D^× has no nontrivial unipotent elements (a unipotent u gives the nilpotent u − 1 ≠ 0 in D); apply AA.3/compactness-anisotropic.
2. Finiteness of class sets: AA.3/class-number-finite.
3. For γ∈Γ_{x,U}, Nrd(γ) is a positive rational number that is a unit at every finite place, hence equals 1. Thus Γ lies in the compact real group SL_1(ℍ); as a discrete subgroup it is finite. Apply the finite-adelic-discreteness criterion to the adjoint quotient D^×/ℚ^× for the last claim.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For D = (−1, −1)_ℚ and U the units of the Hurwitz order, the class number is 1 and Γ/{±1} has order 12.
- For M_2(ℚ) (split, not definite) the quotient is not compact.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/arithmetic-subgroup-of-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/finite-adelic-discreteness-criterion`, `mathlib:QuaternionAlgebra`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/division-algebra-no-unipotent`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), §3, p. 33. Exactly the compactness argument.

### Volume comparison under level change for a definite quaternion algebra

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/definite-quaternion-mass` · theorem.

For D definite over ℚ and compact open U′ ⊂ U ⊂ (D ⊗ 𝔸_f)^×, with Γ_x = (D^× ∩ xUx⁻¹)/(ℚ^× ∩ U) the finite stabilizers: ∑_{x ∈ Cl(U′)} 1/|Γ′_x| = [U : U′]/[ℚ^× ∩ U : ℚ^× ∩ U′] · ∑_{x ∈ Cl(U)} 1/|Γ_x|, where Cl(U) = D^×\(D ⊗ 𝔸_f)^×/U.

**Hypotheses.** D definite; U′ ⊂ U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Identify the class set with the level quotient using K∞=D∞×. This subgroup contains the positive real split centre and is compact modulo it.
2. Full stabilizers A_x are finite by the quaternion norm-one argument. Effective stabilizers are A_x/(ℚ×∩U).
3. Apply level-map-fibre-mass and sum over the finite base. This proves the effective mass formula directly, without identifying a full quotient volume with the effective mass. The full groupoid mass instead scales by [U:U′].

**Acceptance checks.**

- For the Hurwitz maximal order and an Eichler order of level 3, the local unit index is #ℙ¹(𝔽_3)=4=3+1, so the masses scale by 4 (the rational central intersection is unchanged).

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/definite-quaternion-compact`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-volume-index`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-map-fibre-mass`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/level-quotient-groupoid`.

**Sources.**

- [James Arthur, *An introduction to the trace formula*](https://www.claymath.org/library/cw/arthur/pdf/62.pdf), §2, p. 12. The compact-at-infinity case; volumes of the finite class set.

### GL_2(ℝ) modulo ℝ^× SO(2)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-real-quotient` · lemma.

GL_2(ℝ)/ℝ^× SO(2) is homeomorphic to ℍ^± = ℂ ∖ ℝ through g ↦ g·i, equivariantly for the Möbius action; GL_2(ℝ)^+ acts transitively on ℍ with stabilizer ℝ^× SO(2) at i.

**Hypotheses.** The groups and maps are the concrete ones specified in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use upper-half-plane-action-conventions for the raw action on ℍ±. For x+iy with y>0, the matrix (sqrt(y), x/sqrt(y); 0, 1/sqrt(y)) sends i there. Negative determinant reaches the lower half-plane; the stabilizer of i is ℝ^×SO(2).
2. The stabilizer of i in GL_2(ℝ)^+ is ℝ_{>0}·SO(2) up to ±1; complex conjugation exchanges ℍ and its conjugate.

**Acceptance checks.**

- diag(1, −1) maps i to −i.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/upper-half-plane-action-conventions`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.11, p. 56. Transitivity of G(ℝ) on X = ℍ^±.

### Units at level U_Q form a lattice of rank r₁ + r₂ − 1

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-units-lattice` · lemma.

For a compact open U ⊂ Ô^×, Γ_U = F^× ∩ U is a finite-index subgroup of 𝒪_F^×, and its image under the logarithmic embedding is a lattice of rank r₁ + r₂ − 1 in the trace-zero hyperplane of ℝ^{r₁+r₂}.

**Hypotheses.** F a number field; U compact open.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Identify F×∩Ô× with O_F×. Compactness of Ô× and openness of U give finite index, hence the congruence-unit image has finite index in the logarithmic unit image.
2. Use the pinned instZLattice_unitLattice, not just unitLattice_rank, for discreteness and full span.
3. A finite-index subgroup of this full lattice is a full lattice of the same rank; restricting further to positive units retains finite index. The omitted-place logSpace coordinates identify it with the weighted trace-zero hyperplane.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For F = ℚ(√2), Γ is generated up to torsion by a power of 1 + √2.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:NumberField.Units.unitLattice_rank`, `mathlib:NumberField.Units.rank`, `mathlib:NumberField.Units.instZLattice_unitLattice`.

**Sources.**

- [Frank Calegari, David Geraghty, *Modularity lifting beyond the Taylor–Wiles method*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4224v2), §8.2, p. 79. The unit lattice whose quotient gives the torus components.

### Raw Möbius action and the Mathlib folded action

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/upper-half-plane-action-conventions` · lemma.

The raw Möbius maps of GL₂(ℝ) induce an action on ℍ±=ℂ∖ℝ. Folding the lower half-plane by conjugation is equivariant for this action and Mathlib glAction on ℍ. For positive determinant the raw and folded formulas coincide; diag(1,−1) sends i to −i in the raw action and fixes i in glAction. The existing pinned imaginary-part and denominator formulas supply the carrier and sign checks.

**Hypotheses.** g∈GL₂(ℝ), z∈ℂ with Im z≠0.

**Construction or proof.**

1. Use the pinned moebius_im and denom_ne_zero_of_im formulas to preserve ℂ∖ℝ. Direct matrix multiplication and the nonzero denominators prove the action law. The raw maps commute with complex conjugation because their coefficients are real.
2. Folding z to z or its conjugate according to its imaginary sign is equivariant: positive determinant preserves both half-planes and negative determinant swaps them. Compare with coe_smul and its σ definition. The source quotient uses this raw action; no new scalar-formula declaration is planned.

**Acceptance checks.**

- diag(1,−1) distinguishes the two actions at i.

**Prerequisites:** `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.glAction`, `mathlib:Matrix.GeneralLinearGroup.det`, `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.moebius_im`, `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.denom_ne_zero_of_im`, `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.coe_smul`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Lemma 5.11, p. 56. The full real-orbit convention; the explicit folding bridge is derived by matrix algebra.

### Canonical logarithmic torus at GL₁ level

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-logarithmic-torus` · lemma.

For the level U_Q of gl1-XQ-components, the identity component is the logarithmic quotient W/Λ_Q, where W=ℝ^{r₁+r₂}/ℝ·(1,…,1) and Λ_Q is the image of totally positive congruence units F×∩U_{Q,f}. It is a compact real torus of dimension r₁+r₂−1. The complex circle factors have already been divided out by K∞.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. Take absolute values and logarithms after quotienting the complex compact circles; positive real archimedean factors become ℝ^{r₁+r₂}.
2. The diagonal positive split centre becomes the constant line. Its quotient identifies with the product-formula weighted trace-zero hyperplane.
3. gl1-units-lattice gives a full lattice Λ_Q; a lattice basis identifies W/Λ_Q with a product of r₁+r₂−1 circles.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-units-lattice`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient`.

**Sources.**

- [Frank Calegari, David Geraghty, *Modularity lifting beyond the Taylor–Wiles method*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.4224v2), §8.2, pp. 78–79. The level and component group are retained; the circle quotient precedes the dimension calculation.

### GL₂ components for O(2) and SO(2)

**Declaration:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-orthogonal-level-components` · lemma.

For GL₂/ℚ and principal finite level K(N), the quotient with K∞=ℝ×SO(2) has components (ℤ/N)× and raw real symmetric space ℍ± before rational orientation reduction. With K∞=ℝ×O(2), the real space is the folded ℍ and the component set is (ℤ/N)×/{±1}. At N=3 these cardinalities are respectively two and one.

**Hypotheses.** All objects, actions and measures have the hypotheses in the statement..

**Construction or proof.**

1. The positive determinant arithmetic subgroup gives the SO(2) component quotient ℚ_{>0}\𝔸_f×/det K(N).
2. Passing to O(2) allows rational negative-determinant matrices; their determinant adds the sign subgroup {±1} to the finite unit quotient.
3. Use the raw/folded action bridge, and compute the two finite unit quotients at N=3.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The conclusion uses the stated canonical maps and the stated hypotheses.

**Prerequisites:** `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-principal-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-real-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/upper-half-plane-action-conventions`.

**Sources.**

- [J. S. Milne, *Introduction to Shimura varieties*](https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf), Theorem 5.17, pp. 59–61; zero-dimensional example, p. 63. The real compact and determinant sign convention determine the components.

## Supplier contracts

These are imports or additions in the named owner’s direction. They are exact contracts, rather than replacements for the missing owner’s definitions.

### ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0

The topology on X(R) = Hom_{alg}(A, R) for an affine scheme of finite type and an arbitrary Hausdorff topological ring R (not only a local field): the weakest topology making all evaluation maps continuous (Conrad, Proposition 2.1), with functoriality in X and in continuous ring maps R → R′ (embeddings, open and closed embeddings, discreteness: Conrad, Example 2.2), compatibility with fibre products, closed immersions to closed embeddings, local compactness when R is locally compact, the topological-group axioms for Hopf algebras, and compactness and openness of 𝒳(𝒪_v) in X(F_v) for affine models over 𝒪_v (Conrad, Example 2.3 and Corollary 3.7). AA.1 evaluates it on 𝔸_F, 𝔸_{F,f}, F_v, 𝒪_v and F ⊗ ℝ. Supply the finite-type affine-group coordinate-comodule theorem: every finite-dimensional algebraic representation is a subquotient of finite sums of tensor words in a closed faithful representation and its dual. This is not true for arbitrary faithful point-group homomorphisms.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/restricted-product-comparison`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/integral-points-level`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/rational-points-discrete`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-map`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/closed-subgroup-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/product-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/zariski-dense-closure-open`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/algebraic-tensor-eigenvalues`.

### ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.0a

Weil restriction Res_{E/F} for affine finite-type schemes along a finite separable extension of number fields, with the identification of points Res_{E/F}(X)(R) ≅ X(E ⊗_F R) natural in the F-algebra R and compatible with group structures. Supply the point comparison as an equivalence of group-valued functors natural in every continuous value-algebra map, its compatibility with morphisms in G, identity and tower base change, and the tensor-product associator. The adelic comparison uses the canonical topological F-algebra isomorphism E⊗_F A_F→A_E, not an arbitrary equivalence of point sets.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`.

### ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.4

The Cartan decomposition G(E) = K M(E) K for connected reductive G over a nonarchimedean local field E of characteristic 0, with K a special maximal compact subgroup containing representatives of the relative Weyl group, and M = Z_G(A) for a maximal E-split torus A; together with compactness of M(E)/A(E)-modulo-M(E)^1 used to see that M(E) = A(E)·M(E)^1 up to finite index. In addition to existence of local Iwasawa factorization, supply openness and the local P-K integration formula with left Haar density δ_P(m)^−1 dn dm dk (or the equivalent quasi-invariant quotient cocycle), including normalized integral factorization at good places. The Kneser–Tits theorem over nonarchimedean local fields of characteristic 0 (RT-AREA-automorphic-1/7): for G simply connected, absolutely almost simple and isotropic over E, define G(E)^+ as the subgroup generated by the E-points of the unipotent radicals of E-parabolics and prove Platonov's theorem G(E) = G(E)^+, Tits's simplicity of G(E)^+ modulo its centre (using the Tits system of the Iwahori–Bruhat decomposition), and the consequence that G(E) has no proper subgroup of finite index and no proper noncentral normal subgroup. This is the sub-stage RG2.4:kneser-tits proposed in RT-AREA-automorphic-1.fixes.md; AA.4 consumes only the finite-index consequence.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/local-unimodular-reductive`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa-factorization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/iwasawa-integration-compact`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-sufficiency`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-4-finite-adeles

Local compactness of the finite adele ring FiniteAdeleRing (𝓞 K) K, and the placewise projections as continuous K-algebra maps 𝔸_{K,f} → K_v.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/finite-adele-haar`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-5-full-adeles-and-the-additive-quotient

Local compactness of the adele ring and the continuity of the diagonal K → 𝔸_K.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/adelic-points-locally-compact`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-6-additive-strong-approximation-and-ideles

The idele group 𝔸_K^× with Mathlib's units topology is locally compact and Hausdorff, and RestrictedProduct.unitsEquiv is a homeomorphism for the finite part; the idele norm ‖·‖ : 𝔸_K^× → ℝ_{>0} as a continuous homomorphism trivial on K^×; compactness of the norm-one idele class group; density of K in 𝔸_{K,f} (denseRange_algebraMap_finiteAdeleRing). Strong approximation for the additive group: denseRange_algebraMap_finiteAdeleRing (K dense in 𝔸_{K,f}). Compactness of the norm-one idele class group IdeleClassGroup.normOne and the description of the identity component of the idele class group, for the GL_1 validation.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.0/idele-haar`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/gm-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/log-height`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-adelic-quotient`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/ga-strong-approximation`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/unipotent-class-number-one`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-XQ-components`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-8-finite-extensions-of-adeles-and-ideles

The base-change comparison adeleBaseChangeEquiv : 𝔸_K ⊗_K L ≃A[𝔸_K] 𝔸_L for a finite extension L/K, as a continuous algebra equivalence with the module topology on the source, compatible with the diagonal embeddings and with the decomposition 𝔸_K ⊗_K L = ∏_v (K_v ⊗_K L).

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/base-change-local-factors`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/LieGroups#layer-9-the-cartan-iwasawa-and-kak-decompositions

For a real reductive Lie group G(ℝ) (also G(ℂ) viewed as a real group) with maximal compact K: the KAK decomposition G = K·closure(A⁺)·K with Weyl group representatives in K, and the Iwasawa decomposition G = K A N. For a real reductive group and the specified rational parabolic, give the open P-K factorization, its δ_P^−1 integration density, and Cartan-stable Levi coordinates; mere surjectivity does not provide integration. Supply the nested reductive-subgroup Cartan restriction and conjugacy theorem of Borel–Harish-Chandra §1.8, used in the finite-chain induction §1.9.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1/local-unimodular-reductive`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/horospherical-decomposition`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa-factorization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/iwasawa-integration-compact`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/simultaneous-self-adjointness`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-0-places-completions-and-the-product-formula

Functoriality of normalized absolute values under a finite extension E/F: |x|_w for x ∈ F_v equals |x|_v^{[E_w:F_v]}, with the local degrees in the exponents, and the product formula, used to compare the gauge-form measures of Res_{E/F} G with those of G.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-restriction-scalars`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-4-the-relative-discriminant

The relative discriminant ideal relDiscr 𝒪_F 𝒪_E and the tower formula |d_E| = |d_F|^{[E:F]} · N(relDiscr), used for the discriminant factors of Tamagawa measures under restriction of scalars. Supply the scalar Jacobian product for an arbitrary F-basis β of E, using finite lattice indices and real determinants with complex measure 2dxdy: ∏j_{β,v}=|d_F|^{[E:F]/2}|d_E|⁻¹/². Do not assert this factor at every individual finite place; an integral local basis has j=1.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-restriction-scalars`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/restriction-global-jacobian`.

### ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.1

Field-generic Borel–Tits structure theory over a field of characteristic 0 (in particular a number field): existence and G(F)-conjugacy of minimal F-parabolic subgroups and of maximal F-split tori; the relative root system Φ(S_0, G) with positive roots from a minimal parabolic and simple roots Δ_0; the bijection between subsets of Δ_0 and standard parabolics; the relative Bruhat decomposition G(F) = ⊔_w P_0(F) w P_0(F); and the Borel–Tits criterion that a connected reductive group has a proper F-parabolic iff its derived group is F-isotropic iff G(F) contains a nontrivial unipotent element. RG2.1 states these for local fields E; AA.3 needs them over a number field. For reductive H⊂G in characteristic zero, supply affineness of H\G and local finite-dimensionality of its rational coordinate comodule. This is the closed-orbit realization input (Borel–Harish-Chandra §3.8), not adelic orbit finiteness. Supply the canonical decomposition of a semisimple simply connected F-group as a product of restrictions of scalars of absolutely almost simple simply connected groups, including the correspondence between the places above S. Supply the actual central simply connected derived cover and its lifted conjugation/commutator morphism. For finite-type smooth geometrically connected characteristic-zero affine groups, the geometric character group is finitely generated and torsion free via the torus quotient; for reductive groups restriction to the connected centre is an isogeny on character lattices. Also supply the adjoint quotient with kernel the scheme-theoretic centre and its natural local/adelic point maps.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/minimal-parabolic-data`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-anisotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/compactness-isotropic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/relative-chamber`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-realization`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-theorem`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/plus-subgroup`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/rational-characters-free`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-product-closed`.

### ReductiveGroupsPartII:RG2.3

For connected reductive G over a number field, spread out to a smooth reductive model at almost all finite places and choose hyperspecial compact integral points. At each such place choose a maximal F_v-split torus containing the given global maximal F-split torus; the local split rank can be larger. Supply good-position compatibility with this local torus for Iwasawa. A maximal global split torus need not itself be maximal locally. Extend the reductive abelianization morphism with simply connected derived kernel to smooth models away from finitely many places; identify its fibres as torsors and supply Hensel lifting. Also extend the derived central cover as a finite morphism of the models.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/good-maximal-compact`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/adelic-iwasawa`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/abelianization-integral-lifts`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/cover-integral-image`.

### AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups:AF.1

The real reductive scale-function comparison for proper algebraic representations with their duals: ‖g‖_{ι′}≤C‖g‖_ι^N and conversely, together with bounded-factor invariance under compact translations. Arbitrary faithful representations with a norm that does not control the inverse are insufficient. AA.3 consumes the archimedean comparison; RS-04 assigns the generic adelic height package to AA.3 and narrows AF.0 accordingly.

Needed by: .

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalNumberFields#layer-1-weak-approximation-and-multiplicative-congruences

Weak approximation for number fields (weakApproximation_denseRange, already in Tau Ceti) and its congruence/sign corollaries.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-gln`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-12-separate-arithmetic-global-existence-the-norm-index-and-the-global-correspondence

For a quadratic extension E/ℚ, the quadratic character χ_E of ℚ^×\𝔸^× with kernel ℚ^× N_{E/ℚ}(𝔸_E^×) (global reciprocity and the norm index [𝔸^× : ℚ^× N 𝔸_E^×] = 2), viewed as a character of ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/torus-image-residual`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/residual-joint-limit`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/GlobalQuadraticForms#layer-5-hasseminkowski-isotropy

Hasse–Minkowski for quadratic forms over ℚ in five variables, used to show that a positive rational number is a reduced norm of a definite quaternion algebra over ℚ (the Hasse–Schilling–Maass theorem for quaternion algebras). Also supply local isotropy of every five-dimensional form over ℚ_p (u(ℚ_p)=4), and the real indefinite criterion; indefiniteness at infinity alone is not the local-global hypothesis.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quaternion-reduced-norm-image`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev

Layer 10 Chebotarev Dirichlet density and its consequence: infinitely many unramified finite places with any prescribed conjugacy class in the Galois group of the specified finite splitting extension. Layer 11 coefficient summatory functions do not supply this statement.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-necessity`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`.

### ModularCurvesPartII:R12.2

The analytic identification of Γ(N)\ℍ (and Γ₀, Γ₁ quotients) with the corresponding level quotients, as an isomorphism of analytic spaces with the functorial action, used to identify the GL_2/ℚ adelic component with the upper half-plane quotient.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl2-upper-half-plane-component`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CompactGroups#layer-5-the-peter-weyl-theorem

For a strongly continuous unitary Hilbert-space representation of a compact abelian group, the orthogonal character-isotypic summands have dense Hilbert direct sum, with projections ∫χ(z)⁻¹R(z)dz. The central-character use first twists by an extension of ω′; no untwisted action on X/X′ is asserted. Also supply character separation and Fourier uniqueness of probability measures on compact abelian groups. For compact Hausdorff abelian groups, supply character separation, density of finite character sums in continuous functions, Haar character orthogonality and uniqueness of measures from Fourier coefficients. These are the exact inputs used by the quadratic-kernel limit, without a separate full Chabauty duality theorem.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-quotient-change`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/chabauty-limit-kernels`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quadratic-kernel-fourier`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/diagonal-coset-limit`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-9-pinned-chevalleydemazure-group-schemes-over-ℤ

Supply the uniform finite-field reductive order formula in terms of Frobenius on the geometric character lattice and fundamental degrees, implying L_v(X,1)#G(k_v)q_v^{-dim G}=1+O(q_v^{-2}) for good reductive models. This is finite-group structure input, separate from the adelic convergence theorem. Supply Lang vanishing for torsors under connected reductive groups over finite residue fields, for the integral lifting argument.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/abelianization-integral-lifts`.

### tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AlgebraicTopology#stage-6-cohomology-products-and-manifold-duality

Supply the cohomology exterior-power calculation for a finite product of circles with constant ℤ_p coefficients, its vanishing above the number of factors, and naturality under translations homotopic to the identity. Use the homology/product input already owned by Stage 5.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-component-dimension`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/gl1-hecke-action`.

## Remaining mathematical inputs

### Measures of gauge forms on nonarchimedean analytic groups

Defining |ω|_v on G(F_v) for a nonarchimedean local field F_v needs the F_v-analytic manifold structure of G(F_v) for smooth G and the change-of-variables formula μ(ψ(U)) = ∫_U |det Dψ|_v dμ for F_v-analytic diffeomorphisms ψ between open subsets of F_v^d. Mathlib has the inverse function theorem over complete normed fields and the real change-of-variables theorem, but not its p-adic analogue, and no layer of the atlas plans it (searched: 'analytic manifold', 'p-adic Lie', 'change of variables'). The archimedean case is covered by Mathlib's real change of variables.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/local-form-measure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/weil-volume-formula`.

### Leading coefficient of Artin L-functions at s = 1

For a connected group G whose character module X*(G_{F̄}) has nontrivial Galois action, the constant ρ_G needs the meromorphic continuation of the Artin L-function L(X, s) to a neighbourhood of s = 1 and the nonvanishing at s = 1 of L(σ, s) for the nontrivial irreducible constituents σ (Brauer induction and the nonvanishing of Hecke L-functions at s = 1). No layer of the atlas plans Artin L-functions of number fields in this generality (atlas search 'Artin L' finds only function-field and Iwasawa layers), and the libraries lack them. The split case (trivial Galois action), which covers GL_n, split tori and every semisimple group, uses only the residue of the Dedekind zeta function (Mathlib).

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-measure`.

### Orders of finite reductive groups (Steinberg's formula)

The finite-field order estimate #𝓗(k_v)q_v^{-d}L_v(X,1)=1+O(q_v^{-2}) uniformly at good reductive places is requested from Tau Ceti ReductiveGroups layer 9, the finite-group/model owner. Its Steinberg/Bruhat/Lang proof is not supplied by the pinned libraries. GL_n and SL_n have the explicit elementary counts in this packet; the general estimate is a supplier obligation, not an adelic measure theorem.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence`.

### Cartan's closed-subgroup theorem for p-adic analytic groups

The openness of the p-adic closure of a Zariski-dense subgroup (Rapinchuk Lemma 2.7) uses Cartan's theorem that a closed subgroup of a p-adic analytic group is an analytic subgroup with a Lie algebra, together with the irreducibility of the adjoint representation of an absolutely almost simple group. Neither library has p-adic analytic groups and no atlas layer plans this theorem (searched 'Cartan', 'p-adic Lie', 'analytic group').

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/zariski-dense-closure-open`.

### Borel density theorem

Zariski density of infinite S-arithmetic subgroups of absolutely almost simple groups with G_S noncompact (Platonov–Rapinchuk Theorem 4.10, cited by Rapinchuk §2.4) is planned in no layer and absent from the libraries; Platonov–Rapinchuk is not freely available and the proof (Furstenberg's projective-measure argument) is not read here.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/borel-density`.

### Kneser's and Harder–Chernousov's Galois-cohomology theorems

Kneser's vanishing of H¹(F_v, G) for simply connected semisimple G at nonarchimedean v, the Hasse principle of Kneser–Harder–Chernousov and weak approximation for simply connected groups (Platonov–Rapinchuk Theorems 6.4, 6.6, 7.8, cited by Harpaz–Wittenberg §6) are stated here with their exact hypotheses, but their proofs (classification-based, using Bruhat–Tits theory and case-by-case work for E8) are not decomposed: the book is not freely available and no atlas layer plans the classification of simply connected groups over local and global fields.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/kneser-local-torsor`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/hasse-principle-simply-connected`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/weak-approximation-simply-connected`.

### Central measurable sections and character extension

The exact Borel-section theorem for Polish-group homogeneous quotients, the topological isomorphism X/(X∩Γ)→XΓ/Γ when XΓ is closed, identification of the covariant completion with measurable square-integrable associated-line sections, and extension of continuous characters of closed LCA subgroups still require source-complete analytic decomposition. The density and strong-continuity proof now uses the corrected subordinate-function argument from Bekka–de la Harpe–Valette Appendix E.1; its measurable-model identification remains a separate input.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-associated-line`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-measurable-section`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-l2-completeness`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/central-character-extension-twist`.

### Number-field Tamagawa and Artin analytic inputs

Equation (48) in Gordon is a number-field torus source, whereas Rosengarten §3 is function-field only. The canonical formula here fixes λ_v=L_v(X,1), the positive leading coefficient ρ_G, |d_F|^{-d/2}, and the additive local measures. The general finite-image Artin positive leading coefficient at s=1 and its ramified induction determinant argument still need a source-complete proof. The reductive order estimate is requested from the finite-group owner.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/convergence-factors`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-convergence`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/artin-factor-induction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2/tamagawa-restriction-scalars`.

### Squared-weight estimate for closed real orbits

Decompose Borel–Harish-Chandra §§5.1–5.3 into the minimum-norm/Cartan squared-weight estimate and properness of the closed-orbit map modulo the stabilizer. These analytic representation lemmas are not supplied by a discrete∩compact argument or by the later adelic orbit theorem. The weight-bound node exposes this primitive input instead of using the consumer.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/closed-orbit-weight-bound`.

### Primitive real reduction and transfer to parabolic Siegel sets

Hermite–Minkowski reduction for arbitrary n, its bounded-denominator real Siegel overlap estimate, and the transfer from self-adjoint GL_n intersections to the specified parabolic domains need their full lemma proofs. The Mathlib modular-domain result proves only rank one. For disconnected groups additionally supply the compact component quotient and a Levi/semidirect covering; these are not inferred from the final class-number theorem.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-reduction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/gln-real-overlap`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/siegel-covering-adelic`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/real-siegel-finite-overlap`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/class-number-finite`.

### Geometry-of-numbers height counting

Supply the projective/affine number-field height-counting proof: polynomial ideal-norm counting, ideal-class representatives, a unit logarithmic fundamental domain and a uniform lattice-box count. The count node isolates those inputs; a compact height ball and discrete rational points prove finiteness but cannot prove a polynomial bound.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/rational-coordinate-height-count`.

### Levi extension of adelic volume and invariant-measure criterion

For connected nonreductive and disconnected linear groups, prove the Levi/unipotent product integration and compact component-quotient reduction before using the general finite-volume criterion. The reductive proof and its split-centre decomposition alone do not cover these groups.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/finite-volume-criterion`.

### Real root-intersection and pivot estimates

Finish the root-contraction/parabolic-normalizer lemma for intersections of deep Siegel domains (Orr–Schnell §D, Lemma 3, pp. 1234–1236), extend it to the stated distinct nonminimal parabolics, and prove the Cholesky pivot bounds in the reduced-form dictionary. The finite root-cone containment nodes do not imply these intersection estimates.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/deep-distinct-parabolics`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/incompatible-morphism-obstruction`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduction-siegel-dictionary`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3/reduced-form`.

### Arithmetic closure and anisotropic finite quotients

Supply the trace-field/restriction-of-scalars Lie argument for the full S-integral group over a number field, exclude graph Lie subalgebras between distinct finite places, and eliminate arithmetic finite quotients at anisotropic compact factors. These three inputs are isolated in arithmetic-native-lie-closure and arithmetic-finite-index-elimination. Rapinchuk §2.6 reads only the ℚ single-isotropic-prime branch and refers the general proof to an uncleared book.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-native-lie-closure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/arithmetic-finite-index-elimination`.

### Obstructions to torus and non-simply-connected approximation

Decompose the power-isogeny/Chebotarev obstruction for a nontrivial torus and the central-cover obstruction for a non-simply-connected absolutely almost simple group, including the finite-generation control on rational S-units. The explicit G_m/ℚ failure is verified directly; it does not establish all tori.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/torus-strong-approximation-failure`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/strong-approximation-necessity`.

### Quaternion norm topology and integral images

Supply openness of the actual local reduced-norm map onto its image and its unit image at all good places, together with the integral central-cover models. These establish the restricted-product quotient homeomorphism in reduced-norm-components; a bijection of square-class sets alone does not establish it.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/reduced-norm-components`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4/quaternion-reduced-norm-image`.

### Hurwitz and Eichler concrete validation inputs

Supply the norm-Euclidean argument giving class number one for the Hurwitz maximal order in (−1,−1)/ℚ, enumerate its 24 norm-one units and their 12-element effective quotient, and identify the split local Eichler unit subgroup at 3 as the stabilizer of a line in 𝔽₃², of index four. These concrete order computations are not consequences of the abstract compactness or level-mass theorem and are not yet supplied by an atlas owner.

Needed by: `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/definite-quaternion-compact`, `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5/definite-quaternion-mass`.

No undecomposed internal mathematical gap is retained in AA.0.

**AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.1 — planned.** Obtain RG2.0 affine-points topology and coordinate-comodule language, RG2.0a natural Weil restriction, and GlobalNumberFields layer 8 continuous adelic scalar extension. Implement the integral-model, point/topology comparisons and examples with those canonical structures.

**AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.2 — planned.** Finish nonarchimedean gauge integration/change of variables, positive Artin leading coefficients and induction at ramified places, and the requested finite reductive order estimate. Supply Borel sections, measurable associated-line completion, LCA character extension and the closed-product quotient topology in the central-character construction. The quotient-integration and corrected density arguments are now decomposed.

**AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3 — planned.** Supply the primitive squared-weight closed-orbit estimate, general Hermite–Minkowski/Siegel overlap and transfer to parabolic domains, Cholesky pivot and deep root-intersection estimates, geometry-of-numbers rational height counting, and the nonreductive/disconnected Levi extensions. Obtain RG2.1/RG2.3 and real/local Iwasawa integration suppliers.

**AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.4 — planned.** Supply native-field arithmetic closure and exclusion of graph Lie subalgebras, anisotropic finite-index elimination, p-adic analytic closed-subgroup theory, Borel density, local/global simply connected cohomology and torus/central-cover obstruction proofs. Obtain integral-cover, Lang/Hensel and Kneser–Tits suppliers; finish quaternion norm topology. Implement neatness and quotient/Hecke maps using the actual algebraic representations and canonical topology.

**AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.5 — planned.** Obtain the unit/logarithm/idele topology and torus cohomology suppliers; implement the SO(2)/O(2) comparisons for the specified levels. Supply the concrete Hurwitz class-number/unit enumeration and level-3 Eichler unit-index checks.

AA.4 contains 67 nodes in approximation, algebraic neatness, level maps and the residual quotient. The accepted ownership correction makes neat elements and levels an early reusable package consumed by ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces:ALS.0, ShimuraData:D5 and ShimuraVarieties:V0.

## Source corrections

The findings below apply to the identified source versions. Their descriptions and corrections are in our own words; no source passages are reproduced. Existing independently checked findings retain their review records.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E1

**error · §4.5, p. 18 of arXiv:1810.04801v2; JAMS p. 933**

**Source assertion:** The second claim says that if b is (e′,C′)-reduced, any basis e meeting Definition4.11(3) for C>0 makes b (e,C″)-reduced, where the positive constant C″>0 depends solely on e,C,e′,C′.

**Correction:** b is (σe, C″)-reduced for the permutation σ of e that sorts the values b(e_i, e_i); condition (2) transfers only up to reordering.

**Reason:** Take e′ the standard basis of ℚ², e = (e₂, e₁) and b = diag(1, 1000): b is (e′, 2)-reduced and satisfies (3) in e with C = 2, but condition (2) in the order e requires 1000 < C″·1 for every b in the family diag(1, t), which fails as t grows.

**Scope:** the proof. Not corrected in print or in the 2023 erratum; first recorded as PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E3 in the reviewed extraction, rechecked here.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); The 2023 JAMS erratum (DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E2

**error · Definition 2.5 and Proposition 2.7, pp. 8–9 of arXiv v2 (JAMS p. 924)**

**Source assertion:** Proposition2.7(1) claims a covering of Γ\G/M by images of a finite collection of Siegel sets S_i=U_i×A_{P_i,t_i}×W_i associated with P_i,x_i, citing BJ06a Proposition2.5.

**Correction:** Siegel sets for G/M must be associated to one fixed maximal compact K ⊃ M; Proposition 2.7 holds when all Siegel sets use the same K.

**Reason:** For SL₂(ℤ) and x ≠ i in ℍ, a Siegel set B_N B_A K_x is not contained in finitely many translates of Siegel sets for K_i (erratum §1.6.1), so the definable structures for different K differ.

**Scope:** a stated result. Erratum, J. Amer. Math. Soc. 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025, §1.1 and Theorem 1.2(1)

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); The 2023 JAMS erratum.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E3

**misprint · §2.2, (2.3), p. 8 of arXiv v2 (JAMS p. 923)**

**Source assertion:** The source labels (2.3) a right self-action of P and writes its rule as (n₀a₀m₀)(n,a,m)=(n₀·(a₀m₀)n(a₀m₀)⁻¹,a₀a,m₀m).

**Correction:** the left action of P on itself (and on G); read m₀ for m_o, and close the parenthesis after [BJ06a, Lemma 2.3].

**Reason:** The formula computes the product p₀·(n a m), i.e. left multiplication: (n₀a₀m₀)(n a m) = n₀ (a₀m₀ n (a₀m₀)⁻¹)(a₀ a)(m₀ m) since A_P and M_P commute.

**Scope:** nothing. Not corrected in print; first recorded as PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E10 in the reviewed extraction, rechecked here.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); The 2023 JAMS erratum.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E4

**misprint · Proposition 2.7(3), p. 9 of arXiv v2 (JAMS p. 924)**

**Source assertion:** The source claims eventual emptiness γS₁∩S₂=∅ for large enough t₁,t₂, with U_i,W_i fixed and P₁, P₂ representing distinct Γ-conjugacy classes.

**Correction:** Then γ𝔖₁ ∩ 𝔖₂ = ∅ for all γ ∈ Γ, once t₁, t₂ are sufficiently large.

**Reason:** γ is unbound in the printed sentence; the intended uniformity in γ is what Borel–Ji prove and what the proof of the definable structure uses.

**Scope:** nothing. Not corrected in print; first recorded as PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E39 in the reviewed extraction, rechecked here.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); The 2023 JAMS erratum.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E5

**error · §2.3, arXiv v3 pp. 15–16; Annals p. 162; repeated in proof of Proposition 3.6, arXiv v3 p. 25; Annals p. 172**

**Source assertion:** The source assigns the entire target as the image in the infinity-split case for B. In the other case, it gives ℚ_{>0}\ℝ_{>0}×𝔸_f^×/𝔸^{×2} as a subgroup of index two.

**Correction:** The reduced norm induces a bijection G(ℚ)\G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+ → ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} in both cases.

**Reason:** When B is definite the image ℚ_{>0}\(ℝ_{>0} × 𝔸_f^×)/𝔸^{×2} surjects onto ℚ^×\𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2}: an idele with negative archimedean component is multiplied by −1 ∈ ℚ^× into the positive part.

**Scope:** a stated result. Not corrected in print; first recorded as PAPER-KHAYUTIN-19/E39 in the reviewed extraction, rechecked here.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); Annals of Mathematics article page.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E6

**error · §2.3, arXiv v3 p. 15; Annals p. 162**

**Source assertion:** The source describes the reduced-norm map G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+→𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} as a monomorphism between abelian groups, both of which it calls compact.

**Correction:** a monomorphism of locally compact abelian groups; neither G(𝔸)/G(𝔸)^+ nor 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} is compact (only their quotients by G(ℚ) and ℚ^× are).

**Reason:** 𝔸^×/𝔸^{×2} surjects onto ∏_p ℚ_p^×/ℚ_p^{×2} restricted to almost all units, which is an infinite discrete-by-compact group with infinitely many open cosets of ∏_p ℤ_p^×/ℤ_p^{×2}; it is not compact.

**Scope:** the proof. Not corrected in print; first recorded as PAPER-KHAYUTIN-19/E40 in the reviewed extraction, rechecked here.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); Annals of Mathematics article page.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E7

**misprint · Proof of Proposition 3.6, arXiv v3 p. 25; Annals p. 172**

**Source assertion:** The proof uses isotropy of T over ℚ to justify calling the abelian quotient [T(𝔸)] compact.

**Correction:** Because T(𝔸) is abelian and T is anisotropic over ℚ, the homogeneous set [T(𝔸)] is a compact abelian group.

**Reason:** T = E^×/ℚ^× for a quadratic field E is ℚ-anisotropic; compactness of T(ℚ)\T(𝔸) uses anisotropy.

**Scope:** nothing. Not corrected in print; first recorded as PAPER-KHAYUTIN-19/E10 in the reviewed extraction, rechecked here.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); Annals of Mathematics article page.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E8

**error · §8.2, arXiv v2 p. 79 (Inventiones p. 403)**

**Source assertion:** The source describes the group of components of X_Q as the largest quotient of the ray class group with conductor Q having exponent p^n.

**Correction:** π₀(X_Q) = F^×\𝔸_F^×/U_Q(F ⊗ ℝ)^{×,0}: an extension of the narrow class group of F by a quotient of ∏_{v∈Q} 𝒪_v^×/𝒪_v^{×p^n}, not the maximal exponent-p^n quotient of the ray class group; correspondingly H⁰(X_Q, ℤ_p) = ℤ_p[π₀(X_Q)].

**Reason:** Take F = ℚ(√−5), Q = ∅ and p = 3: X_Q has π₀ = F^×\𝔸^×/Ô^×(F ⊗ ℝ)^{×,0} ≅ Cl(F) ≅ ℤ/2, while the maximal quotient of exponent 3^n of the ray class group of conductor 1 is trivial.

**Scope:** a stated result. Confirmed in arXiv v2; not addressed by the 2022 Correction, Invent. Math. 227, pp. 855–856, read in full. Prior record PAPER-CALEGARI-GERAGHTY-18/E160; no novelty claim. The published original was not independently collated in this review.

Correction search: The reviewed paper extraction in research/blueprint/papers/ (its sourceIssues and review); The arXiv version history of the paper; The journal page for errata and corrigenda (Crossref); The authors' Correction, Invent. Math. 227 (2022) 855–856.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E9

**error · §4.5, arXiv v2 p. 17; JAMS p. 932; corrected in erratum §1.5, p. 3**

**Source assertion:** The source cites §7.5 of Borel–Harish-Chandra62 as justification for containing the inverse image in X of a Siegel set within the union displayed next.

**Correction:** Use the rational finite-translate preimage theorem: Proposition 28.1 in the BGST 2021 author PDF (published Proposition 3.4). Its hypotheses include the compatible Siegel containment. Borel–Harish-Chandra §7.5 supplies a real statement and does not give the needed rational translates.

**Reason:** The official erratum §1.5 explicitly replaces this citation. A real finite-translate theorem does not give the rational translates needed by the application.

**Scope:** the proof. Official BKT erratum §1.5; already recorded as PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E7, independently confirmed here.

Correction search: Complete official 2023 erratum; arXiv v2 §4.5; Published copy pp. 932–933; Reviewed PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20 sourceIssues.

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E10

**misprint · arXiv:1806.10723v3 §3, p. 25, local L-factor display before equation (3.4)**

**Source assertion:** The displayed local L-factor is defined as the Frobenius determinant rather than its reciprocal.

**Correction:** L_v(Ĥ,s) = det(1−q_v^(−s) F_v | V^{I_v})⁻¹.

**Reason:** For H=G_m the printed determinant gives 1−q_v^(−s), whose global product is ζ_k(s)⁻¹, while the following sentence requires a pole at s=1 of rank 1. At s=1 its product with μ_v(𝒪_v^×)=1−q_v⁻¹ fails the asserted nonzero absolute convergence. The inverse factor satisfies both checks.

**Scope:** nothing. Confirmed in the hashed arXiv v3 copy by text and page image. No claim of novelty or of the published formula is made; the publisher landing page was accessible but the PDF download returned HTML.

Correction search: arXiv v3 displayed formula, subsequent pole statement and equation (3.4); arXiv version history and journal-reference metadata; Publisher article page DOI 10.2140/ant.2021.15.1865; Web searches for a Rosengarten Tamagawa erratum or correction (none located).

### AdelicAlgebraicGroups/E11

**gap · Author manuscript dated 23 February 2007, Lemma E.1.3 proof, pp. 408–409; not scoped to the Cambridge version of record**

**Source assertion:** After covering a compact support by neighborhoods Ux_i, the proof assigns every approximation function the same weight f/n and uses an oscillation bound relative to every center x_i at every point x.

**Correction:** Choose nonnegative compactly supported f_i with support in Ux_i and sum equal to the cutoff f, where f=1 on the compact support Q. Only indices with x in Ux_i contribute, so the local oscillation bound applies term by term. Keep the subsequent fibre-average and L² estimate.

**Reason:** A cover guarantees that each point is near some center, rather than all centers. Already for H trivial and a compactly supported scalar function taking opposite values on separated neighborhoods, equal weights average distant samples and the asserted pointwise error bound fails. Subordinate weights give the required uniform approximation.

**Scope:** the proof. The same proof problem and partition-of-unity repair were mentioned in the MathOverflow discussion https://mathoverflow.net/questions/425141 (June 2022); it is absent from the authors’ official erratum last updated 24 September 2015. No claim that this is new or a finding against the published version.

Correction search: Author manuscript at Bekka’s homepage, dated 23 February 2007; Authors’ official Erratum-Kazhdan.pdf, all four pages, updated 24 September 2015; Cambridge book listing and UNIGE archive entry; the version of record was not retrieved; Search for Lemma E.1.3 corrections; MathOverflow 425141 reports the issue without an official corrigendum.

- `mathlib:NumberField.InfinitePlace.Completion.isometryEquivComplexOfIsComplex` in `Mathlib/NumberTheory/NumberField/Completion/InfinitePlace.lean`: A complex infinite-place completion is isometrically equivalent to ℂ; the underlying equivalence is ringEquivComplexOfIsComplex. statement read at Mathlib 082e2d3
- `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.moebius_im` in `Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/UpperHalfPlane/MoebiusAction.lean`: For every real invertible 2×2 matrix and z∈ℂ, the imaginary part of num/denom is det(g) Im(z)/normSq(denom). statement read at Mathlib 082e2d3, line 84
- `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.denom_ne_zero_of_im` in `Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/UpperHalfPlane/MoebiusAction.lean`: For g∈GL₂(ℝ) and Im(z)≠0 the raw Möbius denominator is nonzero. statement read at Mathlib 082e2d3, line 58
- `mathlib:UpperHalfPlane.coe_smul` in `Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/UpperHalfPlane/MoebiusAction.lean`: The coerced glAction value is σ(g)(num(g,z)/denom(g,z)); σ is identity for positive determinant and conjugation otherwise. statement read at Mathlib 082e2d3, line 189
- `tauceti:TauCeti.GeneralLinear.coordinateHopfAlgebra` in `TauCeti/Algebra/AlgebraicGroup/GeneralLinear/Coordinate/HopfAlgebra.lean`: The determinant-localized GL_n coordinate ring bundled as CommHopfAlgCat, with its matrix-multiplication Hopf structure. - `mathlib:MeasureTheory.Measure.ext_of_integral_eq_on_compactlySupported` in `Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/RieszMarkovKakutani/Real.lean`: Two regular Borel measures are equal if all integrals of compactly supported real continuous functions agree. - `mathlib:NumberField.Units.instZLattice_unitLattice` in `Mathlib/NumberTheory/NumberField/Units/DirichletTheorem.lean`: The actual logarithmic unit subgroup is a discrete full ℤ-lattice in logSpace F; this includes the span-top condition, unlike the rank formula alone. statement read at Mathlib 082e2d37e8b0463410cdb532e111cd43d5a66174
- `tauceti:TauCeti.geometricallyReducedCommHopfAlgProperty` in `TauCeti/Algebra/AlgebraicGroup/GeometricallyReduced/CommHopfAlgCat.lean`: The actual all-extension reducedness predicate on the coordinate Hopf algebra; it is required when using the pinned geometric-character torsion theorem. statement read at the pinned commit
